The KeepPeek Book

KeepPeek

This page introduces KeepPeek’s scope, architecture principles, and chapter map. KeepPeek is in Alpha qualification and is not yet production-ready; active promotion evidence is tracked in Alpha gate #145.

KeepPeek is a local-first network video recorder (NVR) and WebRTC media gateway for IP cameras. It runs natively on Linux, macOS, and Windows and keeps camera media on hardware you control without requiring a vendor cloud relay or an AI runtime.

Core capabilities

AreaWhat KeepPeek provides
Camera ingest and controlsDiscover and connect Reolink (reo-proto), ONVIF/RTSP (Retina), Hikvision/Annke (SADP/ISAPI), and manual RTSP cameras. Supports advertised PTZ controls and Administrator browser Talk on cameras with a confirmed speaker.
Zero-re-encode MP4 recordingWrite camera-native H.264/H.265 video and supported audio directly to fragmented ISO MP4 segments across named storage volumes and an optional fourth-tier local archive.
Live and recorded WebRTCServe multi-camera live walls, kiosk display, focused single-camera viewing, UTC day timelines, synchronized multi-camera review, bounded timelapse review, bounded MP4 clip exports, and isolated guest review links (/share).
Events and analyticsStore camera-native events (Reolink, Hikvision/Annke ISAPI, ONVIF PullPoint, RTSP metadata) and external WebRTC analytics; search with boolean expressions and hybrid semantic ranking (/events) and inspect bounded temporal summaries (/activity).
Alerts and integrationsEvaluate server-owned notification rules (browser alerts, Pushover, webhooks, and opt-in bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling), forward normalized events over MQTT 5, expose Prometheus GET /metrics, and embed cameras directly via the Home Assistant card.
Operations and healthTrack authoritative camera, stream, storage, and server health across the web UI, foreground terminal dashboard (TUI), and metrics; manage state in config.toml and secrets.toml with validated ZIP export/apply, backups, and recording maintenance.

Video walkthrough

Watch nine real-time RTSP cameras configured on an empty KeepPeek server and streamed into the live wall with WebRTC health evidence; see Demo videos for all narrated workflow walkthroughs.

The virtual-camera source is Big Buck Bunny, © 2008 Blender Foundation, licensed under CC BY 3.0.

Architecture and development principles

  • Compact native server, browser-decoded UI: The server is written in Rust for memory-safe, predictable concurrent media handling without bundling video codecs or a desktop browser runtime. The Svelte 5 web interface relies on the browser and OS media stack for decoding; an optional Tauri desktop app (keeppeek-desktop) wraps the same UI on Windows and macOS by connecting to an existing server.
  • Modular analytics and transcoding: Recording and playback do not depend on object detection, external AI services, or transcoders. Optional inference services, transcoders, and automation bridges connect through the open MIT-licensed API while the recorder remains independent.
  • Human-directed, AI-assisted engineering: KeepPeek uses AI tools for implementation, validation, documentation, and review; people remain accountable for architecture, security, and release decisions.

Feature guide and navigation

Use this chapter to locate the KeepPeek screen, Settings section, or integration path that owns a task, and to separate active features from unavailable controls. KeepPeek is in Alpha qualification; a visible panel, design reference, or generated API type does not prove runtime support, and availability also depends on your role, camera grants, device capabilities, recording coverage, and browser codec support.

Screen and route directory

Desktop navigation uses the left rail (Dashboard, Viewer, Keep, Events, plus Administrator-only Cameras, Health, and Settings) with live status indicators and a theme toggle at the bottom. Mobile navigation uses the bottom bar (Dashboard, Viewer, Keep, Events, plus Administrator-only Health and More for /settings). Non-Administrator sessions cannot open Administrator routes; Shared review (/share) runs in an isolated guest context outside the authenticated shell.

ScreenRouteRoleUse it forRead next
Dashboard (Peek)/User, AdminWatch live camera walls, switch saved dashboards, edit layouts, and open kiosk display.Live wall
Viewer/viewerUser, AdminFocus on one camera, switch streams, digitally zoom, listen to audio, use Talk (Admin + confirmed speaker), and open History.Live wall, Digital zoom, Camera controls
Keep/keepUser, AdminInspect a camera’s UTC recorded day, gaps, stories, swimlanes, synchronized multi-camera review, bounded timelapse review/export, clip export, and guest sharing.Recording and evidence
Events/eventsUser, AdminSearch stored events with filters, boolean expressions, hybrid semantic ranking, and saved searches; review, dismiss, bookmark, export, or jump to Keep.Recording and evidence
Activity summary/activityUser, AdminView bounded event totals, temporal rollups (24h, 7d, 30d), heatmaps, and comparisons; Administrators can rebuild rollups. Opened from Events > Activity summary.Recording and evidence
Shared review/shareGuest linkOpen a time-bounded, account-free clip review. Streams or downloads MP4 only when permitted by the link; cannot access live cameras or other footage.Guest review links
Cameras/camerasAdminInspect camera inventory, open per-camera configuration, or start guided setup.Camera controls
Add camera/cameras/newAdminDiscover cameras, match the embedded CCTV Camera Database catalog, verify credentials and keyframes, or add explicit RTSP URLs.Camera controls
Camera details/cameraAdmin (edit), User (granted view)Inspect camera status, edit device settings, calibrate live/recorded audio timing, and use advertised PTZ or Talk controls.Camera controls
System health/system-healthAdminDiagnose server, storage, camera, stream, WebRTC, and integration health across the fleet.Camera and stream health
Camera health/system-health/camera/[cameraId]AdminInspect lifecycle state, stream progress, native event status, and recording evidence for one camera.Camera and stream health
Recording integrity/recordingsUser, AdminInspect recording coverage, gaps, and catalog/file integrity before planning maintenance.Recording maintenance
Recording maintenance/recordings/maintenanceAdminExplicitly plan and confirm destructive maintenance, eligible index rebuilds, or archive retention actions.Recording maintenance
Settings/settingsAdminManage dashboards, runtime profiles, storage, volumes, archive, backups, access, guest links, notifications, MQTT, appearance, system status, usage reporting, and bind settings.Visual configuration management
Logs/settings/logsAdminStream live server and browser logs, update the active server capture filter, download scrubbed diagnostics, or open Report bug.Reporting bugs
First run/setupAdmin (local)Verify local storage writability, claim the initial Administrator access key, and launch camera setup.Get started

Two additional operator interfaces connect to the same server: KeepPeek Desktop (keeppeek-desktop, a Tauri window for Windows and macOS; see Desktop application) and the foreground terminal health dashboard (TUI; see Get started and Camera and stream health).

Camera, Keep, and Event links carry identity and timestamp query parameters. Keep those query parameters when sharing an internal deep link, and note that a deep link never grants the recipient camera permissions; use /share for external clip review.

From live view to evidence

  1. Open Dashboard (/) or Viewer (/viewer) and confirm the camera’s live stream is advancing.
  2. Select History to open Keep (/keep), or locate a detection in Events (/events) or Activity summary (/activity) and open its recording context.
  3. Verify the selected camera, UTC date, and UTC timestamp. A gap means recorded coverage is absent for that interval; a loaded player or thumbnail does not prove continuous recording.
  4. Scrub, play, use synchronized multi-camera or timelapse review, and digitally zoom as needed. Digital zoom changes only the browser viewport; PTZ moves physical hardware under separate capability and permission checks.
  5. Review or bookmark the event, export a bounded MP4 clip (or silent timelapse MP4 when an external encoder is available), or issue a bounded guest link. Verify retained coverage and the completed export before relying on the file.

For controls, limits, UTC rules, and failure states, read Recording and evidence. For missing footage, start at Camera and stream health, then use Recording maintenance or Recording archive recovery if persisted data is affected.

Video walkthrough

Focus a live camera in Peek and follow History directly into its Keep timeline.

Settings sections (/settings)

Desktop renders all sections on /settings; mobile shows a searchable section index under More. Saving a draft, applying a live camera change, and restarting the server are distinct steps—check the reported result rather than assuming a saved file is already active.

Theme changes in Appearance & time (or the left-rail toggle) apply immediately in the current browser only when local storage is available and never modify config.toml or Keep’s UTC timelines.

Choose an event or integration path

GoalPathKey boundary
Record without analyticsConfigure cameras onlyNo detector, cloud account, or notification service is required for recording or playback.
Use camera-native analyticsEnable a supported native adapter (auto default)Supports Reolink, Hikvision/Annke ISAPI, ONVIF PullPoint, and RTSP metadata; capabilities vary by model and firmware (Native camera events).
Run custom AI / detectionConnect an external WebRTC analysis serviceSubscribes to encoded streams and publishes events over the API; KeepPeek does not host models (External analysis).
Send human alertsCreate rules in Settings > NotificationsSupports Browser, Pushover, Webhook, and opt-in bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling (109-byte signed signal, off by default). Rule matching does not confirm delivery; the Forwarder rule action is unavailable (Notifications and integrations).
Automate or monitorConfigure MQTT 5, Home Assistant card, or PrometheusMQTT 5 runs from Settings > Integrations; the Home Assistant card connects browser-to-recorder directly; Prometheus scrapes GET /metrics (Notifications and integrations, Home Assistant).
Connect an AI / MCP clientRun keeppeek mcp stdio bridge or enable POST /mcpDisabled by default for network access; supports bounded diagnostics, privacy-gated snapshots, and explicit plan-then-apply configuration changes (MCP server and LLM configuration).
Share a clip externallyCreate a guest review link from Keep or EventsRecipient opens /share without an account for a bounded clip window and expiry (Guest review links).

Implemented vs. unavailable controls

Read the reason displayed on any disabled or unavailable control. Do not treat an unavailable count as zero, a saved configuration as a healthy connection, or an advertised camera capability as a verified operation.

Feature or controlCurrent statusBoundary
Browser TalkShipped (conditional)Available only for an Administrator on a camera with a confirmed speaker (Camera controls).
Live participant groups (ListGroups, JoinGroup, LeaveGroup, Settings > Groups)UnavailableProtobuf types exist without a runtime handler. Camera access labels, dashboard audiences, and planned live participant groups are separate concepts (Groups).
Notification rule Forwarder actionUnavailableRejects delivery as channel_unavailable; configure MQTT separately in Settings > Integrations (Notifications and integrations).
Event source registry (Register a source, Manage source, Rotate token)UnavailableSettings > Event sources shows read-only catalog counts and origin definitions only; configure native events per camera (Native camera events).
Automatic update check & bulk erase (Update check, Update channel, Erase all recordings)UnavailableReplace server binaries manually (Upgrades and migrations) and remove recordings through confirmed maintenance (Recording maintenance).
Server time zone / clock / week-start / language selectorsUnavailableAppearance & time reports browser context only; it does not alter server time handling or Keep’s UTC timestamps.
Integrations UI cards for Home Assistant, Webhooks, PrometheusRead-only reference cardsConfigure Home Assistant externally (Home Assistant), webhooks inside notification rules, and Prometheus scraping against GET /metrics.

See Release readiness and known limitations for qualification limits and Reporting bugs for capturing reproducible failures.

Get started

This chapter covers installing KeepPeek on Linux (Docker or binary), macOS, or Windows, monitoring a foreground server in the terminal dashboard, completing /setup, backing up configuration, and verifying your first camera. First-run setup requires Administrator access (granted automatically from trusted local networks by default).

Alpha status: KeepPeek is in Alpha qualification and is not yet production-ready. Track promotion evidence in the Alpha gate.

Choose an installation path

Select a host with enough disk capacity for your camera bitrates and retention policy. KeepPeek records and streams camera media without transcoding, so viewing browsers must support the camera’s codec. Download builds and checksums from the releases page and record the version and .sha256 digest.

PlatformPackage / artifactArchitecturesRuntime / serviceDefault config & secrets directory
Dockerghcr.io/xnorpx/keeppeek:<tag>amd64, arm64Non-root UID 65532, port 8081/config/keeppeek/
Linuxkeeppeek-<version>-linux-<arch>.tar.gzx86_64, aarch64Foreground keeppeek binary${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/keeppeek/
macOSkeeppeek-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmgaarch64KeepPeek.app + com.keeppeek LaunchAgent~/Library/Application Support/keeppeek/
Windowskeeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>-installer.exe, standalone .exex86_64, aarch64keeppeek.exe or KeepPeekService (SCM)%APPDATA%\keeppeek\
DesktopWindows unified installer / .exe; macOS desktop .dmgWin x64/arm64, macOSNative Tauri client only (no server)Reads/writes [desktop] in config.toml

On Windows and macOS, you can optionally install KeepPeek Desktop to open an existing local or remote KeepPeek server in a dedicated window.

Security default: Clients on private and local networks receive Administrator access without signing in. Review Authentication and access control before exposing port 8081 beyond trusted devices.

Linux with Docker

The container image supports linux/amd64 and linux/arm64, runs KeepPeek as non-root user 65532, and listens on port 8081. Configuration, owner-only secrets.toml, recordings, recordings.db, thumbnails, and logging settings live under /config/keeppeek.

  1. Create a host data directory owned by UID/GID 65532 (for a new directory; adjust permissions if reusing existing storage):
mkdir -p keeppeek-data
sudo chown 65532:65532 keeppeek-data
  1. Run the container with /config mounted from the host:
docker run --rm --name keeppeek -p 8081:8081 \
  -v "$(pwd)/keeppeek-data:/config" \
  ghcr.io/xnorpx/keeppeek:latest
  1. Open http://localhost:8081.

Use an explicit release tag or digest instead of latest for reproducible deployments, and add a restart policy (such as --restart unless-stopped) for unattended operation. If the container cannot write configuration or recordings, verify host mount permissions for UID 65532. For native Linux hosts without Docker, extract keeppeek-<version>-linux-<arch>.tar.gz, verify its .sha256 file, and run ./keeppeek.

macOS

macOS releases target Apple Silicon (arm64) only.

  1. Download keeppeek-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmg and its .sha256 file, then verify the checksum:
shasum -a 256 -c keeppeek-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmg.sha256
  1. Open the DMG, drag KeepPeek.app to /Applications, and launch it once. It installs and starts the per-user com.keeppeek launchd agent, which starts at user login.
  2. Open http://localhost:8081.

Code signing and notarization depend on the release workflow’s configured credentials; verify the downloaded artifact rather than assuming both are present. See the macOS installation guide for service logs and Upgrades and migrations before replacing an existing app.

Windows

Official Windows releases publish Authenticode-signed, RFC 3161 timestamped x86_64 and aarch64 executables (including uninstall.exe), each with a .sha256 file:

  • Unified installer: keeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>-installer.exe — select at least one of KeepPeek Desktop (keeppeek-desktop.exe), KeepPeek Server with TUI (keeppeek.exe), and KeepPeek Windows Service (keeppeek-service.exe / KeepPeekService).
  • Standalone assets: keeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>.exe, keeppeek-service-<version>-windows-<arch>.exe, keeppeek-desktop-<version>-windows-<arch>.exe, and server archive keeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>.zip.
  1. Download the unified installer for your host architecture and verify its .sha256 hash and Authenticode signature (Get-AuthenticodeSignature).
  2. Run the installer and select your desired components. For silent installs, combine /S with /DESKTOP, /SERVER, /SERVICE, /NO_DESKTOP, /NO_SERVER, or /NO_SERVICE (at least one component must remain enabled, or the installer exits with status 2).
  3. Open http://localhost:8081 after startup.

Inspect the Windows service from PowerShell:

sc.exe query KeepPeekService
sc.exe qc KeepPeekService

KeepPeekService reads configuration from its own service account’s profile, which may differ from your interactive %APPDATA%. Check the service identity and active data paths before migrating between standalone and service runs. See Upgrades and migrations and the Windows service guide for lifecycle and logging details.

Foreground terminal dashboard (TUI)

Running keeppeek in an interactive terminal opens a read-only live health dashboard by default (--tui auto). It polls server health every 2 seconds (marking samples STALE after 5 seconds and keeping the last snapshot if a check fails) and shows process CPU, RSS, viewer count, storage pressure, warnings, and per-stream rows (Camera, Source at >=115 columns, Stream, Health, Codec/Resolution/FPS/Recording at >=100 columns, Recv kbit/s, and Age) with a 4-line selected-stream detail pane.

Flag / environmentBehaviorNotes
Foreground TTY (Linux, macOS, Windows Terminal / PowerShell)DashboardDefault --tui auto; needs interactive stdin, stdout, stderr (TERM != dumb).
Interactive SSH (ssh -t) or Docker (docker run -it)DashboardPass --tui never to emit plain logs instead.
Non-TTY SSH/Docker, pipes, redirected stdioPlain logsNo escape sequences emitted; --tui always logs one diagnostic and keeps logs.
keeppeek config ..., --help, --versionPlain outputSubcommands and metadata flags never enter alternate screen, even --tui always.
keeppeek-service (Windows) and com.keeppeek (macOS)Plain / service logsBackground service entry points never start the terminal dashboard.

Controls: Up/Down or Page Up/Page Down select a stream row; q detaches to plain logs while recording continues; Ctrl+C requests a graceful shutdown and finalizes active recordings. After a hard kill, open a new terminal or run reset on Unix.

First-run setup (/setup)

Open http://localhost:8081/setup from a trusted-local browser to complete the evidence-based first-run check:

  1. Where footage goes: Confirm storage.medium_term_path, observed free capacity, and a WRITE PROOF VERIFIED status. Use Verify storage again to re-test or Change recording storage (/settings?edit=storage#storage) to pick another path.
  2. What time it is here: Verify the browser-detected time zone. The server does not expose a timezone configuration command, so host OS time remains authoritative.
  3. Remote Administrator credential: If you need remote access, select Retrieve initial key, save it with Copy or Download (keeppeek-initial-administrator-key.txt), then select Hide permanently. The key is displayed once and remote access stays unready until it is retrieved. Create named credentials per person or integration in Settings → Access; see Authentication and access control.
  4. Select Continue to camera setup (enabled once storage write proof passes) to open /cameras/new (or /cameras if cameras already exist).

This walkthrough verifies first-run storage proof, browser time zone, and the one-time Initial Administrator key claim on /setup:

Configuration, secrets, and recovery backup

First startup creates config.toml and owner-only companion secrets.toml in the installation’s default directory. Passing -c <path> / --config <path> selects a custom config.toml and looks for secrets.toml beside it; omitted storage paths still fall back to the OS default KeepPeek directory.

Never commit secrets.toml to source control or attach it to logs, screenshots, or support bundles. See Passwords and secret references for {secret:KEY} syntax, precedence, and full configuration schemas.

Before relying on the recorder:

  1. Review Settings → Storage & retention to confirm recording, catalog, and thumbnail paths, free space, and retention caps. Built-in defaults are starting values, not fleet sizing recommendations.
  2. Open Settings → Backup and restore as an Administrator and export a validated configuration ZIP (GET /config/export, or keeppeek config export --output <file>). Store the ZIP securely off-host—it contains exactly config.toml and plaintext secrets.toml and omits recordings.db, sessions, MP4 footage, and thumbnails.
  3. Verify restoration on an isolated instance (POST /config/apply or keeppeek config apply <file> --confirm). Apply validates files, capacity, and paths before staging; live files switch only on controlled restart. See Backup and restore.
  4. Set up a separate media and catalog backup if footage must survive disk loss; see Recording archive recovery.

Add and verify the first camera

With zero cameras configured, select Add camera from the Dashboard’s No cameras yet prompt, or open Cameras → Add camera (/cameras/new).

  1. Run subnet discovery, enter an IPv4 or rtsp:// address manually, or match your model in the embedded CCTV Database catalog.
  2. Provide camera credentials (or use server defaults) and run Verify streams—KeepPeek requires authenticated video and a keyframe on every stream needed by the selected recording policy before saving.
  3. Choose a recording policy that fits your workload (the default is event-boost, not continuous dual-stream recording) and save.
  4. Edit per-camera settings on its Camera page (/camera); reserve Settings (/settings) for server-wide configuration.

See Camera controls for the full wizard and control details, and Cameras, brands, and protocols for vendor paths and compatibility boundaries.

This walkthrough shows adding and verifying a camera through the production UI:

First-use verification checklist

Before trusting an installation, verify all four checks:

  1. Media and evidence: Confirm live playback, a newly finalized interval in Keep (/keep), and a small downloaded evidence export. Keep a browser-compatible H.264 substream available when main streams use unsupported codecs.
  2. Remote access boundary: Test a disposable User credential from a non-local address across allowed cameras, denied cameras, and a shared dashboard audience.
  3. Events and integrations: If you enabled native events, external analysis, notifications, or MQTT, trigger one controlled event and verify end-to-end delivery.
  4. Restart durability: Restart KeepPeek once and confirm recording resumes, durable settings persist, and remote users can sign in again. Pending notification and MQTT deliveries do not survive a restart.

Next steps: Camera and stream health, Recording and evidence, and Release readiness and known limitations.

Desktop application (Windows and macOS)

KeepPeek Desktop (crates/keeppeek-desktop) packages the KeepPeek web interface in a native Tauri v2 window for Windows and macOS so you can connect to an existing local or remote KeepPeek server without managing browser tabs. It is a client only—it never bundles, starts, or stops a recorder—and cross-origin OIDC sign-in or popup administrator verification requires a standard browser.

Deployment model

ConcernOwner
Recorder startupOperator or OS service (keeppeek, Windows KeepPeekService, macOS com.keeppeek, or Docker).
Recorder shutdownOperator or OS service manager. Closing or quitting KeepPeek Desktop never stops recording.
Desktop window and UIkeeppeek-desktop Tauri shell + OS webview (WebView2 on Windows, WKWebView on macOS).
Server config and DBTarget KeepPeek server (config.toml, secrets.toml, recordings.db, media directories).
Saved server URL[desktop].server_url in the existing canonical config.toml (no separate settings file).

Keeping the desktop client decoupled lets a 24/7 recorder continue capturing footage after you close the window and lets one laptop switch between local and remote recorders.

Supported platforms and artifacts

OSMinimum versionArchitecturesNative webviewRelease artifacts
WindowsWindows 10 1809+ / Windows 11x86_64, aarch64Microsoft Edge WebView2 (Chromium)keeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>-installer.exe, keeppeek-desktop-<version>-windows-<arch>.exe
macOSmacOS 11 (Big Sur)+aarch64 (Apple Silicon)System WKWebView (WebKit)keeppeek-desktop-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmg

Linux and mobile desktop builds are out of scope; use a standard browser on those platforms.

Installation and uninstall

Windows

Official Windows releases publish Authenticode-signed, RFC 3161 timestamped executables (keeppeek.exe, keeppeek-service.exe, keeppeek-desktop.exe, uninstall.exe, and the unified installer), each with a matching .sha256 file.

  1. Download keeppeek-<version>-windows-<arch>-installer.exe (or standalone keeppeek-desktop-<version>-windows-<arch>.exe for portable use) and its .sha256 file from the releases page.
  2. Verify checksum and signature in PowerShell:
Get-FileHash keeppeek-<version>-windows-x86_64-installer.exe -Algorithm SHA256
Get-AuthenticodeSignature keeppeek-<version>-windows-x86_64-installer.exe
  1. Run the installer and select KeepPeek Desktop (optionally also KeepPeek Server with TUI and/or KeepPeek Windows Service). For unattended installs, combine /S with /DESKTOP, /SERVER, /SERVICE, /NO_DESKTOP, /NO_SERVER, or /NO_SERVICE—at least one component must stay selected (for example, /S /DESKTOP /NO_SERVER /NO_SERVICE).
  2. Launch KeepPeek Desktop from the Start Menu. On older Windows 10 builds without WebView2, install the Microsoft Edge Evergreen WebView2 Runtime if prompted.
  3. Uninstall via Windows Installed apps or signed C:\Program Files\KeepPeek\uninstall.exe. Uninstalling removes binaries, shortcuts, and KeepPeekService if present, while preserving %APPDATA%\keeppeek\config.toml.

macOS

  1. Download keeppeek-desktop-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmg and its .sha256 file, then verify:
shasum -a 256 -c keeppeek-desktop-<version>-macos-aarch64.dmg.sha256
  1. Open the DMG, drag KeepPeek Desktop.app to /Applications, and launch it. Signing and notarization depend on the release workflow’s credentials; verify the downloaded artifact.
  2. Uninstall by quitting (Cmd+Q) and deleting /Applications/KeepPeek Desktop.app. This leaves KeepPeek.app, com.keeppeek, and ~/Library/Application Support/keeppeek/config.toml untouched.

Connecting to a server

URL resolution precedence

On startup, keeppeek-desktop resolves its target origin in this order:

  1. --server-url <URL> CLI flag
  2. KEEPPEEK_DESKTOP_SERVER_URL environment variable
  3. [desktop].server_url in config.toml (see Desktop client settings)
  4. Derived local origin http://127.0.0.1:<port> from root host and port in config.toml
  5. Fallback http://127.0.0.1:8081
CLI flagPurpose
--server-url <URL>Connect to a specific canonical http:// or https:// server origin
--no-autoconnectOpen the launcher screen instead of auto-connecting on startup
-c, --config <PATH>Read and write [desktop].server_url in an alternate config.toml

Pre-flight probe and launcher controls

Before loading the server UI, the launcher executes a bounded GET /auth/session probe (5-second timeout, 0 redirects, 64 KiB response cap, max 64 advertised auth methods).

  • On success: The webview navigates directly to <origin>/. Trusted-local Administrator access, bearer access-key sign-in, and same-origin identity-proxy sessions run directly from the server.
  • On failure: The launcher stays open with an actionable error message, allowing you to edit Server URL, toggle Save as default in config.toml, and choose Connect or Retry.
Failure stateCause and recovery
Unreachable / timeoutServer stopped, wrong port, or VPN/network blocked. Start the server and click Retry.
TLS verification errorUntrusted certificate. Install/trust the certificate in the OS trust store, then retry.
HTTP 426Server enforces HTTPS for remote access. Use an https:// origin or a trusted local host.
Redirect / non-serverEndpoint redirected or did not return KeepPeek session JSON. Enter the direct server URL.
OIDC redirect / popupBlocked by single-origin and no-popup rules. Use a standard browser for OIDC sign-in/proof.

Menu actions and shortcuts

Menu itemWindowsmacOSAction
Connection → Switch Server…Ctrl+Shift+SCmd+Shift+SReturn to the launcher without auto-connect
Connection → ReconnectCtrl+Shift+RCmd+Shift+RRe-probe the active origin and reconnect
Connection → ReloadCtrl+RCmd+RReload the current server page
Connection → Quit KeepPeek DesktopCtrl+QCmd+QExit the client (recorder keeps running)

Window close, reopen, and exit

  • Windows: Closing the window exits keeppeek-desktop.exe and releases WebView2 child processes; keeppeek.exe and KeepPeekService keep running.
  • macOS: Closing the window navigates back to the launcher (triggering pagehide so WebRTC peers close cleanly) and hides the window. Clicking the Dock icon reopens and reconnects. Choosing Quit KeepPeek Desktop (Cmd+Q) exits the client while com.keeppeek continues running.

Security boundaries and playback limits

  • Zero native access for server pages: crates/keeppeek-desktop/capabilities/default.json restricts capabilities to "local": true on the "main" window with "withGlobalTauri": false. Once navigated to a server origin, remote pages cannot call native Tauri commands.
  • Strict launcher CSP: default-src 'self' with no inline scripts, inline styles, eval, or wildcards.
  • Single-origin navigation and no popups: The Rust on_navigation guard permits only the bundled launcher origin (tauri://localhost, http(s)://tauri.localhost) and the verified server origin. Disallowed schemes (file:, javascript:, data:), embedded credentials, third-party origins, and all popup windows (target="_blank", window.open) are blocked.
  • Server-enforced policy: Local/remote classification, cookies, CSRF tokens, bearer keys, camera grants, and privacy schedules remain enforced exclusively by the KeepPeek server.
  • Webview codec behavior: KeepPeek streams without transcoding. Windows WebView2 matches Edge (H.264 standard; HEVC depends on OS video extensions and GPU). macOS WKWebView uses WebKit/VideoToolbox, which can reject non-standard H.264 profiles or high bitrates; retain a compatible H.264 substream. Unsupported codecs remain visibly reported by the UI. Both launcher and server UI preserve keyboard navigation, focus outlines, landmarks, and role="alert" / role="status" announcements.

For local builds, signing scripts, automated contract tests, and recorded Windows benchmarks, see the desktop engineering guide.

Authentication and access control

This chapter explains KeepPeek’s local-first trust model, trusted-local vs remote classification, Administrator and User roles, per-camera/group grants, HTTP authentication endpoints, bearer and external sign-in (OIDC and identity-aware proxy), session revocation, reverse-proxy HTTPS rules, and audit/metrics boundaries. Configuring access requires Administrator privileges; external authentication is opt-in and never disables trusted-local recovery.

Access model

KeepPeek evaluates every request in two steps:

  1. Network classification: Is the client’s effective IP address inside access.local_networks?
  2. Remote identity: If not local, does the client present a valid named bearer credential or an active opt-in external authentication session (OIDC or identity-aware proxy)?
ClassificationSource address ruleResulting access
LocalInside access.local_networksAdministrator automatically, no sign-in prompt
RemoteOutside access.local_networksMust authenticate with bearer key or configured external method
Unknown / malformedMissing or invalid forwarded IP / peerTreated as remote; denied without a valid credential

Security boundary: Treat access.local_networks as an authentication boundary. Every device in a configured local range receives full Administrator access without signing in.

First run

On first startup, KeepPeek generates a cryptographically random Initial Administrator key (UUID).

  1. From a trusted-local browser, open /setup (or Settings → Access).
  2. Select Retrieve initial key. The key is shown only for this operation and is never logged.
  3. Save it immediately using Copy or Download (keeppeek-initial-administrator-key.txt), then select Hide permanently.

KeepPeek reports remote access as unready until the initial key is retrieved, while local camera setup remains available. If the key is lost, a local Administrator can rotate the Initial Administrator credential in Settings → Access.

Local networks and reverse-proxy classification

Default access.local_networks covers IPv4/IPv6 loopback, RFC 1918 private ranges, IPv4/IPv6 link-local ranges, and IPv6 unique-local ranges. IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses are normalized before matching.

  • Docker caution: Container bridge ranges commonly fall inside 172.16.0.0/12 (local by default). Narrow local_networks if sibling containers must not get Administrator access.
  • Replacement semantics: Setting local_networks in config.toml replaces all defaults; include 127.0.0.0/8 and ::1/128 explicitly when customizing.
  • VPN policy: Include the VPN client CIDR in local_networks to grant all VPN clients Administrator access, or omit it to require individual credentials and roles.
  • Apply change: Restart KeepPeek after editing [access] network settings.

See Access policy for every [access] key, default, and bound.

Roles

KeepPeek enforces two fixed roles across every HTTP endpoint and WebRTC control command. Hidden UI controls improve clarity for Users but are never the security boundary. Custom roles are not supported.

CapabilityAdministratorUser
View live media, recordings (Keep), and eventsYesGranted cameras
Operate PTZ, recall presets, play supported quick replies/chimesYesGranted cameras
Use personal notification and event-review stateYesGranted cameras
Use browser Talk and Broadcast talkbackYesNo
Add/configure cameras, storage, recording, runtime profiles, MQTTYesNo
Export evidence, delete recordings, change retention or backupsYesNo
View logs, health diagnostics, GET /metrics, and security auditYesNo
Manage credentials, camera grants, external auth, and sessionsYesNo

Camera and dashboard access

  1. Open Settings → Access (/settings#access) as an Administrator.
  2. In Access credentials, select the person icon (User access) beside a User credential.
  3. Choose Everything (default; includes current and future groups and cameras) or Selected groups and cameras.
  4. When restricted, select camera groups, individual cameras, or both. Matching either an allowed group or an explicit camera ID grants access; selecting neither grants zero cameras.
  5. Save. Saving camera grants immediately closes that User’s active sessions so they sign in again with updated scope. Stale concurrent saves return a revision conflict without discarding your draft.

Key boundaries:

  • Camera groups come from configuration namespaces (for example, [outdoor.front_door] belongs to outdoor). Each credential supports up to 128 group_ids and 128 camera_ids, and cannot combine all_cameras = true with explicit IDs.
  • Server-wide enforcement covers live and recorded streams, event thumbnails/previews, notification inbox/history, recording coverage, and PTZ/motion-read APIs. Grants never unlock Administrator-only operations such as export or camera configuration.
  • Dashboard audiences are separate. Sharing a dashboard layout (peek_layouts) controls who can see that layout; it never grants access to denied cameras. A User sees only permitted camera tiles and cannot modify or share server-owned layouts, while the Administrator’s full layout stays intact.
  • Trusted-local precedence. To restrict someone on your LAN or VPN, exclude their address from access.local_networks so they authenticate as a remote User.

Camera grants persist in access_credentials.credentials[].camera_access and dashboards in peek_layouts; see Credential records for exact fields and limits.

HTTP authentication endpoints

All /auth/* responses include Cache-Control: no-store and Referrer-Policy: no-referrer. Browser auth endpoints enforce HTTPS (or a configured trusted proxy) and same-origin Sec-Fetch-Site checks. Credentials are never accepted in URL query parameters.

Method & pathPurpose
GET /auth/sessionReports local, bearer_enabled, configured external methods, active identity, and CSRF token; bootstraps proxy sessions and clears invalid cookies
POST /auth/loginStarts an OIDC authorization-code + PKCE S256 login flow for a configured provider
GET /auth/callbackCompletes the OIDC callback, validates tokens/claims, and issues the host-only session cookie
POST /auth/logoutRevokes the active browser session cookie and dependent WebRTC/control connections
POST /createCreates a WebRTC session (Authorization: Bearer <UUID> or valid external session cookie)
POST /deleteCloses a WebRTC session (a client may delete only its own session; idempotent)

Bearer credentials and remote sign-in

Browser and API sign-in

  • Browser: Open KeepPeek over HTTPS and enter the UUID in Remote sign-in. The browser keeps the key in memory only—never in the URL, logs, localStorage, or sessionStorage. Reloading or closing the tab discards the key.
  • API clients: Send Authorization: Bearer <UUID> on HTTP requests. Never pass keys in query parameters, copied links, SDP payloads, or shell history.

Credential lifecycle (Settings → Access)

Create a dedicated named credential for every person, browser, service, or integration (up to 128 credentials). Metadata, camera grants, and SHA-256 verifiers persist atomically under [access_credentials] in config.toml; raw keys are never stored there (the compatibility Initial Administrator key lives in owner-only secrets.toml).

ActionEffect
New credentialSets name (<=64 B), optional description (<=256 B), role, optional expiry (1–3,650 d). Key shown once.
Rotate credentialIssues a replacement key once, increments revision, and closes sessions on the old key.
Disable / EnableSuspends without deleting history; disabling closes sessions; enabling bumps revision.
Revoke credentialPermanent (requires Confirm revoke). Closes sessions; cannot be re-enabled/rotated.

External sign-in (OIDC and identity-aware proxy)

Configure opt-in OIDC providers or identity-aware proxies under Settings → Access using exact origins and explicit role/camera mappings:

  • Cookies and tokens: Browser cookies are Secure, HttpOnly, host-only, and bound to a single allowed origin. Provider tokens never reach the web UI. Restarting KeepPeek invalidates all external browser sessions.
  • Secrets: Provider secrets remain {secret:KEY} references into secrets.toml.
  • Anti-lockout guard: Any change or configuration ZIP restore that alters or removes the last usable remote Administrator path requires fresh verification against the exact proposed candidate and explicit confirmation on the original control connection. Unavailable providers, stale proofs, or failed writes leave the active config untouched.
  • Desktop client note: Cross-origin OIDC login and popup-based admin proof require a standard browser; see Desktop application.

See External authentication configuration for TOML fields and Operate external authentication for reverse-proxy header sanitation, mixed-mode migration deadlines, and troubleshooting.

Sessions, limits, and audit

Each WebRTC session uses a random opaque ID bound to its principal, role, effective address, classification, credential revision, and expiry; a session ID never authorizes a request alone.

Control / limitDefault / bound
Session idle / absolute timeout30 minutes (1800 s) / 24 hours (86400 s); credential expiry can shorten
Concurrent session caps64 per principal, 128 per effective address
Failed bearer auth rate limit5 failures per 60 s per effective address (up to 1,024 tracked addresses)
External login/callback rate limit30 attempts per 60 s per peer IP; 10 per 60 s per browser handle
Expiration sweep & graceful closeSwept every 100 ms; 1 s graceful WebRTC close before warning
Bounded security audit buffer1,024 in-memory events (resets on restart)

Rotating, disabling, revoking, or expiring a credential (or revoking a session in Settings → Access → Active sessions) terminates matching WebRTC sessions and log streams and releases owned PTZ movement, talkback, stored-media cursors, searches, discovery, and queued transfers.

Audit and metrics: Security audit records principal, role, action, target, result, client classification, and UTC timestamp—never raw keys, verifiers, Authorization headers, cookies, SDP, log text, or media. Audit history, active sessions, and last-use timestamps live in memory and reset on restart. For durable compliance evidence, collect audit records externally before eviction/restart and retain protected service logs off-host. GET /metrics exposes label-free auth/session counters and gauges and requires Administrator.

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough demonstrates creating named credentials, assigning roles, scoping per-user camera and group grants in User access, and revoking an active session in Settings → Access:

Reverse proxies and HTTPS

Prefer a private VPN for remote access. If KeepPeek is exposed behind a reverse proxy, terminate HTTPS at the proxy, firewall port 8081 against direct remote bypass, and list only the proxy’s exact CIDR in access.trusted_proxies. With require_secure_remote = true (default), direct remote HTTP returns HTTP 426.

KeepPeek accepts one strict forwarding contract: a single X-Forwarded-For header with comma-separated IPs (no ports or quotes), walked right-to-left to the first non-trusted IP. Strip Forwarded, X-Real-IP, X-Forwarded-Host, and X-Forwarded-Proto at the proxy:

location / {
    proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:8081;
    proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $remote_addr;
    proxy_set_header Forwarded "";
    proxy_set_header X-Real-IP "";
    proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Host "";
    proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto "";
    proxy_set_header Host $host;
}

[access]
trusted_proxies = ["127.0.0.1/32"]
require_secure_remote = true

Troubleshooting

SymptomCause and resolution
Local browser shows Remote sign-inSource IP is outside local_networks, or an untrusted peer sent a forwarding header. Fix trusted_proxies and strip non-X-Forwarded-For headers.
Remote request returns HTTP 426Plain remote HTTP hit KeepPeek with require_secure_remote = true. Use HTTPS via a trusted proxy (a VPN alone does not satisfy TLS checks if kept outside local_networks).
Remote sign-in rejected / rate limitedExceeded 5 failed attempts in 60 s (or external login budget). Wait out the window and confirm credential is active and unexpired.
Credential works for viewing, not SettingsCredential has the User role. Issue an Administrator credential only for operators who manage config/security.
Shared dashboard is empty or missing tilesDashboard audience does not grant cameras. Update User access for that credential, save, and sign in again.
Restricted person still has AdministratorTheir effective IP matched access.local_networks. Remove their subnet from local_networks while keeping local loopback recovery.
Key was lost or exposedFrom a local Administrator session, rotate that named credential, store the new key, and update only that client.

Session expired after a permission change

Saving User access camera grants deliberately closes that credential’s active sessions, as do rotation, disable, revoke, and expiry. If a browser tab opened during that transition stays on an expired-session error (such as Your session expired or was revoked or API session expired or was revoked), reload the page and sign in again with the current key.

For rotated credentials, enter the replacement key. Disabled, expired, or revoked credentials will not reconnect after reload; ask an Administrator to verify the credential’s status in Settings → Access.

Verify access before deployment

  1. Create a disposable User credential and test from a genuinely remote-classified IP (local browser checks do not test remote boundaries).
  2. Verify allowed vs denied cameras across live view, Keep, event previews, PTZ, and a shared dashboard containing one denied camera, and confirm Settings, exports, and diagnostics are denied.
  3. Modify the User’s camera grants, confirm the old session closes, reconnect to verify the new scope, then revoke the disposable credential.
  4. Record your proxy/VPN topology in your release qualification evidence.

Configuration ZIPs include [access_credentials] and grants (but not active sessions, last-use times, or audit history). Ensure you retain a valid local recovery path before restoring a ZIP from another host; see what survives a restart.

Camera controls

This chapter covers onboarding cameras (/cameras, /cameras/new), inspecting and editing camera details (/camera), operating PTZ and motion-detection switches, browser Talk and Broadcast talkback, and switching live streams or runtime profiles. Adding/editing cameras, toggling motion detection, using browser Talk, and managing runtime profiles require Administrator; User accounts can operate PTZ, recall presets, and play supported doorbell sounds on their granted cameras.

Evidence boundary: A live video feed, manufacturer name, or capability badge does not guarantee a camera command will succeed. KeepPeek reports command errors separately from camera and stream health.

Add and manage cameras (/cameras, /cameras/new)

Open Cameras (/cameras) to search your fleet, filter Not healthy sources, select cameras for bulk Configuration, or launch Add camera (/cameras/new).

The /cameras/new wizard guides you through five steps:

  1. Find & connect: Run subnet discovery (customizable /24 prefixes, cancellable), enter a manual IPv4 or rtsp:// address, or search the embedded CCTV Database catalog for model-specific credential-free RTSP hints (Reolink matches default to reo-proto, ONVIF port 8000, HTTP port 80).
  2. Connection options: Enter per-camera credentials (or leave blank to inherit server defaults), optional ONVIF/HTTP ports, backend (auto, retina, reo-proto), and transport (tcp/udp). Unless you supplied a manual rtsp:// URL, KeepPeek probes ONVIF across common ports automatically.
  3. Streams: Review or edit Main stream (recording) and Sub stream (live) URLs, optionally Restore catalog streams, and run Verify streams. KeepPeek authenticates and checks streams in parallel; every stream required by your recording policy must deliver decoded video and a keyframe (VIDEO + KEYFRAME) before saving:
    • event-boost or both: both main and sub verified
    • event-only: selected event stream verified
    • main or sub: that stream verified
    • off: at least one stream verified Editing either RTSP URL invalidates prior verification.
  4. Recording: Set the camera display name, recording policy (default event-boost), Store generic motion events (false by default), event duration (1–3600 s, default 60 s), and optional pre-recording (0–30 s) and event stream.
  5. Review & save: Confirm settings and write the camera to config.toml (passwords remain write-only).

This walkthrough shows LAN discovery, embedded CCTV Database catalog lookup, and stream verification before the first configuration write:

Camera detail page (/camera)

Select any camera to open /camera?camera=<id>. Desktop navigation provides eight sections:

SectionPurpose
OverviewLive preview (sub H.264 preferred) and PTZ control panel when supported and privacy is inactive
ConfigurationEdit settings for credentials, ports, RTSP URLs, backend/transport, and recording policy
PrivacyEdit schedule for server-enforced privacy blocking across all live, recorded, and control paths
ConnectionHealth state, hardware identity, Manufacturer override, Test connection, Open camera UI, and CCTV Database link
EventsMotion detection status and Enabled switch (where controllable)
StreamsConfigured media profiles table and Live stream health metrics (auto-refreshes every 5 s)
AudioDiscovered audio profiles plus recorded and live audio timing offset controls (with live preview)
AdvancedReported capabilities (PTZ, Audio, Events, Recording, Analytics, Imaging, Two-way audio)

Use Camera (/camera) for per-camera edits and Settings (/settings) only for server-wide settings.

PTZ movement and presets

On a PTZ-capable camera, the Overview panel exposes an 8-way directional pad, optical zoom (Wide / Tele), Stop PTZ, and device presets:

  1. Verify the live image for the target camera.
  2. Press and hold a direction or optical zoom button; release to send stop. Keyboard users can focus any movement button and hold Space or Enter.
  3. Select Stop PTZ to halt movement explicitly.
  4. Select a listed preset to recall that saved camera position.

Ownership and vendor boundaries:

  • Continuous movement belongs to one controlling WebRTC session; transitions are serialized and concurrent commands are rejected while another is in flight.
  • KeepPeek sends a stop on disconnect or uncertain movement acknowledgement. If stop cannot be confirmed, KeepPeek reports the error and retains ownership rather than assuming the camera is idle—inspect the camera directly and keep the camera’s own movement timeout enabled.
  • Control transport is independent of media backend: Hikvision/Annke uses ISAPI HTTP (signed per-axis speeds scaled to reported spaces), while Reolink uses Reolink HTTP on channel 0 even when media uses reo-proto (directional movement + speed 1..64; arbitrary NVR channel control is not supported). Speed scales differ across vendors, and missing axes or presets are never software-emulated.

For non-mechanical magnification on live views, recordings, or event images, use Digital zoom and pan.

Motion detection switch

On /camera, Motion detection → Enabled modifies the camera’s hardware detector configuration (not whether motion is happening right now). KeepPeek reads the full alarm configuration, toggles only the enabled field while preserving masks and sensitivity, writes it back, and verifies via read-back. A failed write or mismatched read-back reports an error; KeepPeek never reboots a camera automatically.

SettingScope and effect
Motion detection → EnabledToggles the camera’s on-device motion detector (Administrator only).
Store generic motion eventsControls whether KeepPeek persists unclassified motion events in storage.
Camera recording policySelects recorded streams (event-boost, main, sub, both, event-only, off).

Disabling generic motion retention does not disable the detector or native event subscriptions that smart person/vehicle classifications rely on. Configure regions, schedules, sensitivity, and vendor filters in the camera’s own UI. See Native camera events and Recording and evidence.

Two-way audio (Talk) and doorbell sounds

Listening to camera audio is independent of transmitting microphone audio to a camera speaker. Talk and Broadcast talkback require the Administrator role and a speaker confirmed by KeepPeek’s metadata probe:

Vendor / backendConfirmation requirement for browser TalkTransport & codec sent
ReolinkPowered, direct camera on reo-proto TCP (not battery/UDP); channel grants CGI talk permission AND read-only Baichuan probe confirms compatible mono ADPCM speaker profile at 8 or 16 kHz. Firmware answering only while a speaker stream is open leaves Talk unavailable.Native Baichuan IMA ADPCM (8/16 kHz mono)
Hikvision / AnnkeISAPI audio discovery reports an explicit enabled speaker channel (a microphone channel alone never enables Talk).Raw ISAPI 8 kHz mono G.711 A-law or mu-law (bounded 300 s session)
Other ONVIF / RTSPBrowser Talk is unavailable unless a supported speaker adapter confirms a speaker channel.Not supported
  • Single-camera Talk: In the focused live view, select the microphone (Talkback) button and grant browser microphone permission. Select it again (Stop talkback) or close the page to release the microphone. Incoming camera audio stays active while you talk.
  • Broadcast talkback: On the Dashboard (/), Administrators with confirmed speaker cameras can pick Target (All cameras or a camera group) and select Broadcast talkback / Stop broadcast (one active talkback owner at a time, up to 128 targets). Losing speaker evidence, replacing credentials, or removing a camera automatically retires its active talkback route.
  • Quick replies and chimes: Cameras reporting doorbell sounds show a ♫ (Play doorbell sound) menu in live view. Both Administrators and permitted Users can trigger quick replies and chimes (disabled while Talk is active).

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough demonstrates continuous PTZ movement, Stop PTZ, preset recall, the Motion detection → Enabled switch distinction, and the browser Talk boundary on /camera:

Live stream switching and runtime profiles

  • Per-viewer stream switching: In focused live view (/ or /viewer), choose Auto (network-adaptive), High, Low, Main, or Sub. If a browser cannot decode the requested stream’s codec, KeepPeek falls back to a compatible stream and shows a notice.
  • Server-wide runtime profiles: Administrators manage up to 32 named profiles in Settings → Runtime profiles (/settings#runtime-profiles), overriding up to 127 cameras each across four levers: availability, recording_mode, privacy_enforced, and notifications_suppressed. Profile switches apply asynchronously with per-camera pending, applied, or degraded status; see Runtime profiles.

Unavailable controls

Relative PTZ movement, preset creation/deletion, focus, guard position, sensitivity, detection regions, schedules, and vendor smart-rule editing are not shared KeepPeek browser controls. Manage them in the camera’s native UI (Open camera UI on /camera).

Application and test coverage

KeepPeek’s camera control stack is verified with synthetic integration and browser tests rather than physical-device certification across every model and firmware:

  • Hikvision/Annke (test-hikvision): Covers HTTP Digest, motion config read-back, capability updates, PTZ session ownership and original-target disconnect cleanup, ISAPI audio busy/open/close, raw G.711 speaker writes, full-duplex progress, cancellation, expiry, and malformed responses.
  • Reolink: Covers CGI talk permission + read-only Baichuan speaker-profile verification, talkback routing/retirement on config change or removal, idle activity vs enabled detector state, and channel-0 PTZ behavior.
  • Browser E2E: Verifies Administrator-only Talk/Broadcast gating and User quick-reply/chime permissions in Peek.

Real-world speaker volume, latency, half- vs full-duplex behavior, and firmware quirks still vary by physical camera model, firmware version, account permissions, and network topology—test directly on your hardware before relying on controls. See the camera control reference for protocol-level vendor mappings.

Troubleshooting

SymptomWhat to check
Controls are missing or disabledRole requirement (Administrator vs User), camera grant, active privacy schedule, confirmed capability evidence, and WebRTC connection state.
Verify streams fails in /cameras/newCamera credentials, ONVIF/RTSP port, TCP vs UDP transport, and whether the stream emits a valid video keyframe.
PTZ works in vendor app onlyConfigured HTTP port, device account permissions, channel mapping (Reolink HTTP targets channel 0), and supported adapter; RTSP alone does not verify HTTP controls.
Talk button does not appearRequires Administrator role plus confirmed speaker evidence (Reolink powered TCP + CGI permission + 8/16 kHz ADPCM profile; Hikvision explicit enabled speaker channel).
Motion is enabled but no events appearCamera detector schedule, regions, notification linkage, record_generic_motion_events, and native event status.
Capability badge shows yes but command failsDevice reachability and the returned command error; cached discovery evidence does not guarantee current reachability.
Zoom only crops the pictureYou are using Digital zoom; optical zoom is in the PTZ panel.

Build a structured boolean filter in Events, inspect a matched card’s canonical thumbnail, bounding box, and bookmark actions, then open Activity to inspect bucket charts, weekday×hour heatmaps, and CSV/JSON export.

Configure, issue, preview, and revoke a guest review link

Enable canonical HTTPS guest sharing policy, issue a one-time bounded link from a ready export, preview the isolated /share guest surface, and revoke the grant with an audit reason.

Triage fleet health and diagnose a camera

Inspect fleet verdicts and evidence dimensions on /system-health, drill into an offline camera, and apply Switch to TCP on a UDP-degraded camera.

Manage camera templates and bulk fleet changes

Inspect inherited versus overridden camera fields on /cameras, review templates preserving {secret:...} references, preview a server-owned bulk diff, and apply it with per-camera results.

Export, stage, and validate a configuration ZIP

Export the two-file config.toml + secrets.toml ZIP archive, stage a verified upload pending explicit server restart, and observe rejection handling for an invalid archive.

Preview recording deletion and reconcile catalog drift

Preview bounded whole-recording deletion with typed DELETE N confirmation and durable ledger tracking, then inspect and reconcile catalog drift on /recordings/maintenance.

Configure notification rules, mobile push, and MQTT 5

Inspect notification rules, Inbox, and History, test bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling, and inspect and test the MQTT 5 forwarder.
