Load camera sources in the Home Assistant visual card editor, switch grid columns and single-camera layout, and focus and unfocus a live tile.

Inspect appearance, live logs, diagnostics, and bug reports

Switch browser-local appearance themes, filter and pause live logs, download diagnostics, and walk through the redacted Administrator Report a bug review flow.

Live wall and kiosk display

This page covers multi-camera live viewing on Dashboard (/), single-camera viewing on Viewer (/viewer), saved dashboard management and layout editing, wall display/resource controls, Screen Wake Lock kiosk behavior, and how runtime profiles affect live viewing. Display and streaming settings govern browser presentation only; they never alter camera recording, detection, or exported media.

Dashboard (/) and Viewer (/viewer)

KeepPeek separates multi-camera monitoring from focused single-camera inspection:

SurfaceRoutePurpose
Dashboard/Multi-camera live wall with dashboard switcher, Wall display settings, and bounded browser admission.
Viewer/viewerFull-shell single-camera view with a PEEK / camera overlay, camera selector, filmstrip, stream quality controls, and local digital zoom.
Keep/keepRecorded-day timeline, event context, and evidence exports. See Recording and evidence.
Cameras/camerasDevice settings, physical PTZ, detector configuration, and browser Talk. See Camera controls.

Legacy links to /?camera=<id> redirect to /viewer?camera=<id>. Viewer remembers the last selected camera on that device and falls back to the first authorized camera. Live and History are viewing modes; pausing or closing a browser tab never stops server recording.

When no cameras are available, Administrators see No cameras yet with Add camera (opens the camera wizard), while Users see No cameras available and a prompt to ask an administrator for access.

Select the information control on any tile or Viewer bar to inspect transport, decoded video, and recording evidence without mistaking a paused browser tile for a server outage.

Live wall walkthrough

Watch nine real-time RTSP cameras populate Dashboard (/) and inspect live WebRTC feed evidence:

The virtual-camera source is Big Buck Bunny, © 2008 Blender Foundation, licensed under CC BY 3.0.

Saved dashboards, layout editor, and audience sharing

Use the dashboard selector in the upper-left of / to switch layouts. All cameras is the automatic fleet layout whose grid updates whenever cameras change; its tile placement cannot be rearranged, but Administrators can edit its display settings.

Administrators manage custom dashboards in Settings → Dashboards using New dashboard, Rename dashboard, Manage access, Duplicate dashboard, Delete dashboard, Export, and Import:

  1. Create or duplicate a custom dashboard, then open the layout editor.
  2. Add available cameras to the 12×12 grid, select a tile, and move or resize it with pointer controls or arrow keys. Toggle Activity focus if desired.
  3. Select Save to publish the layout, or Discard / Cancel to keep the saved revision intact.

Audience vs. camera grants: A dashboard’s audience controls who can see that layout (everyone or selected named Users; Administrators always retain access), not which cameras they may view. The server filters every viewer’s tiles to the cameras granted to that identity, so a shared dashboard can contain cameras for one User and appear empty for another. Never broaden camera grants merely to match another person’s wall layout. See Authentication and access control.

  • Offline and removed cameras: An offline configured camera keeps its normal tile; a removed camera shows a labelled placeholder until removed or remapped.
  • Optimistic revisions: Every save sends the current registry revision. Stale saves are rejected instead of overwriting a newer revision while keeping your unsaved draft; reload the saved layout before retrying.
  • Layout JSON export/import: Carries dashboard definitions, display settings, and explicit audience credential IDs—never access keys, camera credentials, or media. Import validates the full document before mutation, requires mapping or omitting missing camera IDs, and assigns imported dashboards Administrator-only access until explicitly shared. See Export and import configuration.

Layout editor and wall display walkthrough

Watch arranging a custom dashboard layout, adjusting Wall display settings, and assigning a dashboard audience:

Wall display settings and persistence

Open Wall display settings (sliders icon, upper-right of /) to adjust presentation and browser streaming policy for the active dashboard. Changes are persisted per dashboard in [peek_layouts] in config.toml under its versioned display object (version = 1), following duplication, layout export/import, configuration export/import, and restart. See Peek layout registry.

SettingDefaultSupported valuesRuntime effect
Tile shape16:916:9, 4:3, NativeSets tile presentation ratio; Native latches first valid decoded ratio.
Media fitContainContain, Cover (cropped)Contain preserves full frame; Cover fills slot with Cropped badge.
Gap10 pxInteger 0–24 pxSpacing between grid tiles; changing gap can alter visible tile count.
Corner radius10 pxInteger 0–24 px0 px removes normal tile borders; health warnings keep an inset outline.
Streaming modeSmartSmart, ContinuousChooses priority/prefetch admission vs. all visible tiles.
Stream limit12 (capped by browser decoder estimate)Integer 1–12Upper bound on concurrent browser-admitted live streams.
Keep display awakeOffOpt-in switchRequests browser Screen Wake Lock while / is active and visible.

Administrators preview edits live, then select Save (or Discard changes to reload server values). A failed or conflicting save keeps the preview intact; discarding after an error reloads the latest server revision. Reset wall settings previews defaults and releases any active wake lock until saved. Users without edit permission see read-only controls.

Browser-local wall preferences are neither read nor written. Older clients that omit display preserve existing server values; invalid versions, fields, types, or ranges are rejected before configuration mutates.

Appearance presets, tile shape, and media fit

PresetGapCorner radius
Current10 px10 px
Hairline2 px0 px
Flush0 px0 px

Presets update only gap and corner radius without changing shape, fit, streaming mode, or wake lock.

  • Native tile shape: Accepts decoded aspect ratios from 1:16 through 16:1, reserving 16:9 until valid dimensions arrive (invalid or missing metadata keeps 16:9). The latched ratio stays stable across later Main/Sub switches during that wall mount and never repacks neighboring grid slots. Labels, health indicators, and diagnostics stay outside the resized media frame, including for portrait and panoramic cameras. On narrow screens, tile slots keep at least 160 px height while scrolling inside the wall viewport.
  • Contain vs. Cover (cropped): Shape, fit, and radius changes never recreate WebRTC sessions, restart recording, or crop Viewer/exports. Contain may show letterboxing; Cover (cropped) fills the frame without stretching pixels and displays a Cropped label. Selecting any tile opens /viewer, which always shows uncropped video.

Streaming modes and browser resource limits

All wall tiles and warm background streams share one browser admission scheduler and one WebRTC peer connection.

Policy dimensionSmart (default)Continuous
Target tilesFocused tile, visible tiles, nearby offscreen prefetch, audio/visibility priorityEvery visible tile independently of motion/events; no offscreen prefetch
Release grace1 s grace after leaving viewportImmediate release when offscreen
Preview streamLowest browser-compatible quality rank (Viewer keeps its own quality selection)Lowest browser-compatible quality rank
Hidden tabSuspends all media subscriptions (wake lock does not override suspension)Suspends all media subscriptions

Effective decoder budget: KeepPeek estimates browser decoder capacity as floor(hardwareConcurrency / 2) clamped to 4–12 decoders (4 if hardware concurrency is unavailable). The effective ceiling is min(decoderEstimate, streamLimit). Wall display settings displays requested streams/decoders, effective budget, active count, and any over-budget count before you enable Continuous. Lower Stream limit if the viewing device cannot decode smoothly; a codec unsupported by the browser is never requested.

  • Admission pacing: Admits up to 3 cameras per batch with a 40 ms delay while additional cameras are queued, releasing replaced subscriptions before requesting replacements.
  • Server authorization and eviction errors: The server authorizes every subscription under its negotiated transport and resource limits (the capability snapshot does not expose a numeric server quota). A server-refused tile shows Server did not admit this stream while other admitted streams continue. If a subscription eviction fails, KeepPeek stops the local live session and reports an unavailable state rather than retaining unconfirmed media work—lower Stream limit, inspect diagnostics, and retry.

Viewer stream switching and live video quality

In /viewer, select Auto, High, Low, Main, or Sub for the focused camera. When a requested stream uses a codec the browser cannot decode, or live playback fails to start, Viewer falls back to a compatible stream and shows an explicit notice rather than silently hiding the mismatch.

  • Auto quality: Uses native RTP bandwidth feedback on the shared WebRTC connection, starting with Sub when available. KeepPeek reserves bandwidth for audio and manually selected video streams, then splits the remaining estimate across Auto video subscriptions—adding cameras can lower each Auto camera’s budget.
  • Keyframe-gated transitions: Auto waits for sustained headroom before upgrading and sustained pressure before downgrading. The current picture stays visible until the replacement supplies a compatible keyframe. An alternative missing codec configuration needed to verify compatibility (such as an unverified H.264 profile) is reported as unavailable rather than replacing a working stream.
  • Manual selections: High and Low keep the requested quality when compatible and available (Auto never overrides manual selections), even if the choice exceeds current network capacity. Fixed reliable-data delivery does not use RTP Auto adaptation.
  • Recorded playback independence: Stored playback does not use this live controller, and a matching recorded time range is not guaranteed to carry identical captured frames.

Open the live video information panel to compare runtime metrics:

Diagnostic fieldMeaning
Requested qualityOperator’s chosen quality preference (Auto, High, Low, Main, Sub).
Active streamServer’s currently active stream source (does not prove browser decoded a fresh frame).
Pending streamReplacement stream waiting for a compatible keyframe gate before handoff.
Auto statusController state: holds, missing network evidence, single variant, or unavailable alternative.
Session network estimateShared WebRTC connection bandwidth estimate (unknown when unavailable).
Camera budgetAdaptive Auto camera’s allocation (0 differs from unknown or unsupported older server).
Receive bitrateMedia bitrate arriving in this browser.

Reconnecting or removing a subscription clears prior diagnostic values.

Frame freshness vs. server health

Server-reported camera health and browser tile admission are independent facts: a healthy, recording camera can still display a paused tile when the browser hits its decoder budget.

Tile indicator / stateMeaning
Admission state badgesIdentifies queued (waiting for admission), over device budget, offscreen, hidden, unsupported, or unavailable.
Frame ageElapsed time since the current viewer observed the retained frame.
Frame time unknownCached frame lacks an observed capture timestamp.
No frame receivedNo frame has been observed to display yet.

None of these browser states proves the camera stopped recording. Use camera diagnostics or Camera and stream health for authoritative server evidence.

Runtime profiles and live privacy

Administrators can switch between Base and named profiles in Settings → Runtime profiles to apply per-camera privacy, availability, recording, and notification overrides. See Runtime profiles.

When an active profile enables camera privacy, the server’s privacy gate immediately blocks live media for that camera. Viewers carrying an affected stream may briefly disconnect; KeepPeek automatically attempts up to 3 reconnects to restore remaining authorized streams (an access denial ends retries). Any interrupted browser talkback, chime, or quick reply is not automatically resumed—restart it only if intended. Changing a runtime profile never alters saved dashboard definitions.

Kiosk display and Screen Wake Lock

Enable Keep display awake in Wall display settings and save it on the dashboard used by a wall monitor or kiosk. KeepPeek uses the browser Screen Wake Lock API, which requires a secure context (HTTPS or localhost) plus browser, embedding-policy, and OS permission.

  • Persisted intent vs. runtime state: Only the enabled preference is saved on the server; lock handles, permissions, visibility, and decoder estimates are local runtime facts.
  • Activation and lifecycle: A saved enabled preference waits for a trusted user interaction before its first request. Only one request can be in flight at a time, and requests with no response within 10 s are reported as denied. Switching to a dashboard with wake lock off, hiding the tab, leaving /, or closing the view releases the lock (including any late-resolving request). Returning to a visible tab may reacquire it if permitted.
Wake lock statusAction / behavior
OffWake lock disabled for this dashboard.
Waiting for interactionSaved on; click, tap, or press a key on / to issue the browser request.
Requesting / ActiveLock request in progress or currently holding the display awake.
ReleasedReleased on tab hide, navigation, or OS/battery-saver policy (not aggressively retried).
Unsupported in this browser or connectionNon-HTTPS origin or browser lacks Wake Lock API.
Denied by browser or devicePermission denied or 10 s timeout elapsed; toggle off and on to retry.
Release failedClose the Dashboard tab to release the browser wake-lock resource.

Screen Wake Lock is not native OS kiosk management and cannot guarantee that OS power policies or battery-saving modes will never sleep the display.

Recording and evidence

This page covers recorded investigation and exports in Keep (/keep), event search and triage in Events (/events), bounded temporal analytics in Activity (/activity), fleet recording integrity (/recordings), recording policies, the local fourth-tier archive, RTSP source-clock inspection, and live audio calibration. For full TOML schemas see Configuration reference; for manual deletion and catalog drift repair see Recording maintenance.

KeepPeek treats footage as evidence only when media is finalized, keyframe-indexed, and playable. A connected camera or MP4 filename alone does not prove an interval can be reviewed later; see Camera and stream health for live evidence dimensions.

Keep workspaces (/keep)

Open Keep, choose a camera and UTC recorded day, and select one of five investigation modes:

ModePurpose
TimelineInspect footage and event intervals around one UTC playhead, with exact frame stepping and digital zoom.
StoriesReview exact story events published by event sources alongside the Footage calendar (only days with a dot are selectable).
SwimlanesCompare indexed recording availability across authorized cameras around a shared UTC window.
TimelapsePlan sparse sampled frames across a range and export a silent MP4 plus SHA-256 JSON provenance manifest.
ExportPrepare, track, and download standalone evidence MP4s (job creation requires Administrator role).

Stories note: Story conclusions and wording belong to the publishing event source; KeepPeek never rewrites them.

Keep timeline, export, and timelapse walkthrough

Watch navigating Timeline, Stories and the Footage calendar, Swimlanes, Export, and Timelapse in Keep (/keep):

Playback, exact frame stepping, and shortcuts

Transport controls (play/pause, skip, position slider, volume/mute, speed, quality, fullscreen) sit below the video and stay separate from digital zoom. Stored playback does not transcode unsupported codecs—retain a browser-compatible H.264 stream when browsers must play recordings directly.

InputAction
SpaceToggle play/pause.
J / K / LReverse shuttle / pause / forward shuttle.
Left / RightStep previous/next recorded frame when paused at 1.0x (pans when digitally zoomed & focused).
[ / ]Set export range start / end at the current playhead.

To inspect adjacent pictures in Timeline, pause playback and use Previous recorded frame, Next recorded frame, or Left/Right. KeepPeek verifies sample order (including duplicate-timestamp pictures) from a random-access GOP bounded to 4,096 samples, 10 s, and 32 MiB. Recorded frame displays the UTC recording position (syncing timeline and export handles); Source presentation also appears when original camera timing differs. Use Read selected frame time for screen-reader announcements and Play recording to resume. Gaps, recording changes, or unresolvable source timing keep the last displayed picture and explain why. Older recorders or browsers without exact decoding fall back to estimated frame-rate stepping (variable-rate media may skip or repeat pictures).

Synchronized multi-camera comparison

In Keep, open Compare with and use Add camera or Group to compare up to 4 cameras on one UTC playhead (2 active decoders on compact screens; groups larger than four load the first four authorized cameras with a notice). Remove drops a camera (dropping to one returns to single-camera view at the shared time).

  • Navigation: Play, Pause, Speed, 5-second seek buttons, Seek to UTC → Go to time, and Previous event / Next event (current UTC day, up to 256 events/stream with truncation notice) drive one shared UTC clock. When paused on a focused camera supporting exact stepping, Prev frame / Next frame steps that camera’s verified picture while sibling cameras seek to the stepped UTC timestamp without claiming identical frame boundaries.
  • Drift and codec diagnostics: Each tile displays latest presented UTC frame time, stream/codec, and Diagnostics (drift, forward buffer, audio state, correction count). Drift of 100–250 ms uses a bounded 3% rate slew; drift > 250 ms seeks once buffered and shows Synchronizing with measured offset until back within tolerance (750 ms minimum between corrections per player). After two failed paused corrections, Retry recording reopens that camera. Gaps show explicit gap status and rejoin when playable footage resumes; unsupported codecs name the stream and codec instead of showing a gap.
  • Audio, focus, and export: Audio selects the single audible camera (Mute / Unmute; tiles show active, muted, unavailable, or configured audio-offset state). Focus sets decoder priority, paused frame-step target, and export camera. Mark up to 2 min with Set range start / Set range end, then choose Export focused camera (Back to comparison restores cameras, focus, audio, range, and playhead). Single camera exits to the focused camera; Back to event returns to Events.
  • Session behavior: Browser storage remembers playback speed and mute. Pausing, seeking, or changing focus/audio updates the URL; Copy link to this moment captures the live playhead while playing. Hidden tabs release players and reopen on return; disconnects clear views and reopen paused at the last shared UTC time. Unavailable cameras in a link still open remaining authorized cameras.

Keep on phones

In portrait, Keep shows a compact camera/date row, player, and horizontal timeline. Tap the camera or date to open Camera and date (search or wrap-around previous/next camera; available days only). Tap Playback options for volume, speed, quality, Copy link to this moment, and Refresh recordings (Done, close, Escape, or browser Back returns focus without resetting playback). In multi-camera comparison on phones, Comparison options groups camera, audio, speed, UTC seek, and range controls. All five modes remain accessible.

Bounded timelapse review and silent MP4 export

Open Timelapse in Keep to inspect quiet periods via sparse sampled frames without loading continuous video. Choose Last 15 min, Last hour, or Custom (absolute UTC milliseconds, unaffected by DST transitions), set cadence by target count or second interval, and review lazily loaded thumbnails. Selecting any sampled frame opens Timeline at its exact UTC source timestamp.

Unavailable ranges (Missing, Retained, Privacy, UnsupportedCodec, Corrupt, OpenRecording) render as explicit gap cards—KeepPeek never repeats a stale frame across a gap.

Generating a timelapse MP4 (Generate Silent MP4) is a separate Administrator action using a capability-gated local FFmpeg worker (libx264 / libopenh264, fixed safe arguments). Output is always silent (-an, never carries source audio) and is a time-compressed derivative rather than continuous source evidence. Optional timestamp overlays render in the configured site timezone, while the companion SHA-256-verified JSON provenance manifest (schema_version = 1, Manifest JSON) records exact UTC source timestamps, (recording_id, fragment_sequence, sample_ordinal) identities, sampling settings, and omitted gap intervals. Source recordings remain read-only and immutable.

Timelapse boundLimit / default
Requested range<= 31 days (2,678,400,000 ms)
Sampled frames<= 3,600 (deduplicated; tolerance default 10 s, max 300 s)
Output dimensions & FPS64×64 to 1920×1080 (even dimensions), 1–30 FPS
Output size / source read<= 512 MiB MP4 / <= 4 GiB source read budget
Concurrency & thumbnails1 generating job globally and per requester; <= 2 thumbnail fetches (64 MiB cache)
Explicit confirmationRequired at >= 600 samples or >= 64 MiB estimated output; gaps require Allow partial

See Bounded Timelapse Review and Export.

UTC rules and explicit gaps

Recorded days, timelines, and event time filters use UTC. Switching cameras keeps the exact UTC instant even if the destination camera has a gap; switching recorded days preserves the UTC clock time on the chosen day (midnight stays 00:00:00 UTC of that day).

KeepPeek never silently jumps an unavailable timestamp to the latest clip. Gaps stay visible with Previous recording and Next recording actions (searching a bounded ±5 min window within that UTC day) or direct timeline selection. Loading, no recorded days, unavailable footage, and decode failures are distinct states.

Copy a recording moment

Use the link icon in Keep’s command bar (or beside Open at this moment in Events) to copy an authenticated /keep deep link. Copying reads the current clock without pausing, seeking, or modifying history. If clipboard access is denied, unsupported, or exceeds 2.5 s, a dialog selects the URL in a read-only field for manual copy (Escape closes and restores focus).

These links require server authentication and camera authorization—they are not public shares and contain no access keys, session IDs, media URLs, or file paths (treat camera IDs and event filter context as private operational data). Inspection zoom crops are never included.

/keep query parameterMeaning
cameraServer-provided camera source identifier.
atAbsolute Unix timestamp in integer milliseconds (wins over conflicting date).
dateUTC YYYY-MM-DD derived from at.
streamRequested preference: auto, high, low, main, or sub.
modeOmitted for Timeline; otherwise stories, swimlanes, timelapse, or export.
eventOptional event identifier.
returnToOptional validated local /events route preserving supported filters and selected event.
compare, audio, rangeStart, rangeEndOptional synchronized comparison cameras (2–4), audio camera, and marked range (<= 2 min).

Playback restoration for retained, supported media is verified within max(1 s, 1 source frame). Missing and unauthorized cameras share one unavailable-or-not-authorized message; malformed links fail visibly instead of substituting another camera.

Export evidence

Administrators can export up to 2 minutes of continuous or explicitly partial retained footage from Keep → Export as a standalone MP4 (no re-encoding on the normal path; timestamp burn-in requires a configured re-encoding worker and otherwise fails explicitly). Opening export from an event seeds ±15 s around the event while preserving camera, stream, event revision, filters, and Back to event.

  • Lifecycle states: running, ready, partial, failed, cancelled, expired.
  • Job guarantees: Deduplicates identical running jobs; offers an existing matching ready file before creating a new one; names every missing interval before allowing a partial export; stops after 30 s without progress or 5 min total runtime; deletes partial files on failure/cancel; marks interrupted jobs failed and retryable after restart; re-verifies SHA-256 immediately before download.
  • Retention and scope: Ready files expire after 24 hours; bounded job history retains up to 500 jobs for 30 days, scoped to the creating Administrator identity. A partial file covers only its available intervals—never describe it as continuous coverage.

Events search, boolean queries, and triage (/events)

Open Events to search authorized cameras by UTC date/time range, camera, event kind, origin, zone, confidence, image availability, text prefix, review state, and bookmarks. Select a card to inspect its canonical preview, metadata, revision, Open at this moment, or export (Back to event restores filters and selection). Check server capability notices before assuming an empty result means no events exist.

Events search, review, and Activity walkthrough

Watch Advanced search boolean filtering, inspecting the event detail drawer, managing bookmarks, and opening Activity summary (/activity):

Advanced boolean and hybrid semantic search

Select Advanced search (requires keeppeek.event-search.advanced.v1) to build nested AND, OR, and NOT trees over 14 indexed predicates (camera_ids, group_ids, event_types, origins, zones, missing_zone, missing_confidence, has_image, confidence, time_range, text, workflow, producer_ids, model_ids).

  • Strict two-valued null and negation rules: Every leaf evaluates to true or false inside the caller’s authorized cameras and UTC window—never SQL UNKNOWN. Missing zone or confidence makes positive zone/threshold predicates false, so NOT (zone in ['porch']) includes events with missing zones, and NOT (...) never widens camera authorization or UTC bounds.
  • Hybrid semantic ranking (keeppeek.event-search.semantic.v1): Ranks structured-predicate matches by exact cosine similarity against embeddings supplied by authorized external producers (Administrator role). KeepPeek never generates embeddings implicitly and never returns raw embedding vectors in hits, summaries, logs, errors, exports, or planner diagnostics. Embeddings are keyed by (event_id, model_id, model_version) and bound to the event’s current revision (invalidated/cascaded in the same transaction on update or delete). Queries compare only within exact (model_id, model_version, dimensions), filter minimum_score before top-K, and tie-break deterministically by (score DESC, start_time_ms DESC, event_id ASC). If semantic storage is unavailable, structured boolean and common search continue normally.
  • Saved searches: Owner-scoped saved searches persist under [saved_event_searches] in config.toml (not copied with catalog DB embeddings; see Saved event searches). Each entry tracks a revision, flags missing/tombstoned camera or group references, and preserves newer schema versions read-only.
Advanced search boundLimit
Expression depth / AST nodes<= 8 depth / <= 64 nodes
Predicate list values / text<= 64 total list values; <= 256 B value (128 B model/producer); <= 16 KiB expression
Cameras / UTC window<= 127 authorized cameras / <= 31 days
Candidates / page / URL expr<= 10,000 candidates / <= 128 hits per page / <= 2,048 B URL param (use saved search ID above that)

Review workflow, bookmarks, and canonical previews

  • Review states: Reviewed and Dismissed are independent per-identity flags (Unreviewed = neither set). Dismissal removes an item from the queue without deleting recordings or attachments, and event updates never reset workflow flags. Counts are server-computed. Bulk actions (Mark N visible reviewed / Mark N selected reviewed) support up to 128 selected events across pages; undo applies only to acknowledged targets, and stale writes prompt Reload and retry while keeping drafts.
  • Identity: Named credentials retain review state across credential rotation. Trusted-local browsers use a persistent Local workspace identity (clearing site storage starts a new workspace; unavailable storage disables mutations instead of silently using an ephemeral ID).
  • Saved bookmarks: Shared with authorized camera viewers; creator or Administrator can edit plain-text notes (up to 1,024 UTF-8 bytes) or delete. The bookmark library has its own source/date/creator filters. Bookmarks and exports do not pin recordings through retention; deleted-event references persist for 90 days as honest metadata-only entries naming missing events, sources, or media.
  • Canonical preview: Events, Keep, notifications, MQTT, and exports share the same event revision and canonical preview (producer-named attachment, else snapshot → story frame → retained thumbnail by deterministic ordinal/time/ID order). Missing canonical bytes report unavailable instead of silently substituting another image; bounding boxes render only when their coordinate space matches the canonical image. Event kind is authoritative; semantic icons are presentation-only.

Activity summary and temporal analytics (/activity)

Open Activity summary from Events (or /activity, requires keeppeek.activity-analytics.v1) for bounded temporal summaries of producer-reported recording_events metadata. Activity never reprocesses video, runs object detection, deduplicates real-world objects across cameras, or infers real-world identity, occupancy, or threat totals.

  • Scope and privacy: Camera authorization is enforced before counting, comparing, or ranking. reviewed and dismissed workflow filters stay strictly principal-local (global reviewed totals are never computed).
  • Windows and civil timezones: Choose Last 24 hours, Last 7 days, or Last 30 days (underlying storage always queries UTC half-open [start, end)), granularities (Hour, Day, Week, Month, Hour of day (0-23), Weekday vs weekend), an IANA display timezone, week start (Monday/Sunday), and Comparison (Previous equal duration, Previous calendar window, or No comparison). Civil day/week/month boundaries respect 23/25-hour DST days and leap days; hour buckets record utc_offset_seconds and dst_fold (spring-forward missing hours are never fabricated); non-hour-offset zones (such as Asia/Kolkata) sum interior UTC-hour rollups and scan exact partial edge slices.
  • Measures and confidence bands: Reports event_count (== distinct_event_count), clipped interval durations (total_duration_ms, average_duration_ms excluding zero-length point events, plus open_interval_event_count), completeness percentages (image, confidence, zone), and fixed confidence bands: unknown, low [0.0, 0.5), medium [0.5, 0.8), high [0.8, 1.0].
  • Completeness and source health: Retention deletions mark overlapping buckets partial_retention. Historical event-source health is never extrapolated backward from present status—cameras are classified per window as reporting, healthy_no_reports, missing_health, no_expected_source, or unknown_history.
  • Exports and rollup recovery: Use Export CSV or Export JSON (capped at 25,000 aggregate rows). Incremental double-buffered rollups degrade safely without blocking canonical event writes if quotas (500,000 default rows, range 1,000–5,000,000) or integrity checks trip; Administrators can run Rebuild rollups for a bounded shadow rebuild (<= 512 events/tx, <= 65,536 dirty catch-up IDs) without modifying canonical events.
Activity boundLimit
Time buckets / grouped series<= 366 buckets / <= 64 grouped series / <= 64 ranked rows per dimension
Cameras / group_by / range<= 127 authorized cameras / <= 8 dimensions / <= 10 years UTC
Canonical fallback / workflow<= 7 days short-range fallback / <= 250,000 events workflow scan
Response payload / export<= 1 MiB serialized response / <= 25,000 aggregate export rows

See Event Activity Summaries and Temporal Analytics.

Camera recording policies and pre-recording

Each camera uses one recording policy in its camera settings (see Camera defaults and camera entries):

PolicyBehavior
offRecords no video; intentional and never reported as a gap.
subRecords substream continuously.
mainRecords main stream continuously.
bothRecords main and substream independently.
event-boostDefault. Records substream continuously, switches to main at a keyframe on events, then returns to sub.
event-onlyRecords selected main or sub stream only for accepted events plus available pre-event memory history.
  • Pre-recording memory buffer: event-only and event-boost accept 0–30 s of in-memory pre-event history (default 0 s, default RAM ceilings 64 MiB/stream and 256 MiB globally in advanced storage settings). In event-boost, KeepPeek delays continuous sub writes by at most the configured pre-roll so main-stream history can replace it in one recording (uncommitted delay can be lost on crash; memory pressure shortens history). Recordings always begin at a retained decodable keyframe; startup, long GOPs, or byte limits can shorten the interval. Audio packets crossing the video boundary are omitted without re-encoding. History does not survive restart, privacy activation, reconnect, or storage pause. Before downgrading to a version without event-only support, switch policies, set pre-recording to 0, and back up configuration (committed media needs no conversion).
  • Runtime profiles and write-failure recovery: When a runtime profile reports a camera Degraded (storage rejected settings, queue/acknowledgement failed, or an active input write failed after acceptance), new recording input stops while live viewing can continue. Pending awaits writer acceptance; Applied confirms configuration acceptance only (verify retained footage in Recording integrity). After repairing settings or storage, save/discard profile edits and select Reapply active policy in Settings → Runtime profiles (idle event recording and ordinary writes do not clear a latched write failure automatically).
  • Admission vs. retention: Recording policies control admission, not independent per-event-class retention deadlines; storage pressure can shorten coverage. See recording policy audit.

Local fourth-tier archive (ARCHIVE)

KeepPeek separates RAM buffering, active recording files, finalized primary media, and the optional fourth ARCHIVE tier (a verified replica on a mounted local filesystem—distinct from the archive named-volume placement role, which relocates primary files without creating a second replica). Offsite providers are not yet supported.

  1. As Administrator, create a private directory owned by the recorder account on a mounted local disk that does not overlap primary or metadata roots.
  2. Configure target ID, root, limits, and optional source filter in Settings → Storage → Local archive (see Fourth-tier local archive), save with revision check (Reload archive settings discards drafts), restart KeepPeek, test the destination, and refresh jobs (a pending probe is not a passing test).
  3. Queue manual copies of finalized recordings or completed exports, or enable automatic copying after minimum age (empty source list selects all). Copies verify the replica independently; refresh before retrying uncertain results (duplicate replies reconcile by durable job ID).
  • Playback and primary eviction: Primary eviction defaults off. Enable automatic eviction deliberately or confirm Evict primary per job (checked against verified archive, active reader leases, and retention holds). Evicted recordings remain indexed in Keep and stream needed byte ranges directly from archive with the notice ARCHIVE — Playing from archive. Startup and seeking may take longer. Use Restore primary to transfer a verified copy back.
  • Archived exports: After local export expiry/pruning, Keep → Export → Find completed exports (same account, no segment selected) lists up to 64 additional verified archived exports newest-first (older exports remain accessible by job ID; active camera privacy hides and blocks downloads; download verifies the mounted target).
  • Verified archive deletion: Preview archive deletion or Preview expired archive copies (16 jobs/page when retention days are set; blank retains indefinitely; expiry alone never deletes) previews targets/IDs/bytes; Confirm archive deletion authorizes only that page. Deletion verifies both copies within one operation timeout, then records retirement intent, quarantines, stages, and re-verifies before unlink (resuming from the last durable step on restart). Windows holds an exclusive read handle through removal; Linux requires fs-verity plus immutable inode flag (chattr -i -- /abs/path/to/verified-primary.mp4 only after acknowledged if normal moves/retention must resume; fs-verity persists); mutable Linux/macOS retain both copies. Acknowledged deletions exclude that object from future automatic/manual archiving in the current catalog. Evicted recordings require Restore primary first; holds, active readers, changed media, or unavailable primary block deletion.
  • Cancellation and offline mounts: Cancel stops cancellable copy/restore jobs and cleans partial staging (Retry / finish cleanup keeps job identity and waits for acknowledged cleanup). Durable primary eviction intents cannot be cancelled and must reconcile. An offline archive mount marks archive health unavailable without blocking live recording ingest; never substitute a missing mount. See Recording archive recovery.

Prove recording integrity (/recordings)

Open Recording integrity to verify retained footage before an incident occurs. It reports policy demand, writer state and latest frame/write/finalization/catalog timestamps, oldest/newest media, effective duration, attributed bytes, estimated daily growth, keyframe-indexed playable coverage (24 h, 7 d, 30 d), and exact open or closed gaps with causes (source silence, transport outage, stale frames, decode failure, writer failure, disk pressure, retention deletion, storage migration, catalog mismatch, unknown) plus links to nearby footage, health, and logs.

Fleet stateMeaning
Recording healthyEvery requested stream has current writer progress.
Recording degradedA requested writer is stalled or failed.
Paused by policyEffective policy intentionally requests no recording.
Not configuredNo usable recording stream is configured (shown only when count > 0).
UnknownInsufficient current evidence for a truthful determination.

Bounds: 25 cameras/page default, at most 256 exact recent ranges per stream, and deterministic time buckets with exact totals over longer windows.

Interrupted legacy recording initialization

On Unix, if a failed legacy header write encounters two files at the same active name, the current occupant stays in place and the displaced entry moves to .keeppeek-header-recovery-v1/ in that recording hour. Its private <active name>.pins-v2/ directory holds a hard link encoding the writer’s device and inode across restarts. Listings, integrity scans, and pruning skip the recovery directory; the next request for that active name verifies the pin before reclaiming a failed partial file or restoring the displaced file. For older recordings, stop directory writers and inspect both files before changing either (entries without a valid recovery record or in untrusted/inaccessible directories are left untouched). Named storage volumes use their allocation ledger instead.

Compare RTSP source clocks

RTSP streams log camera.rtsp.source_timing / RTSP source timing at info level on completed sessions and failed PLAY attempts with accepted RTP. To collect periodic samples, enable keeppeek::rtsp::timing=trace—the first enabled collection starts one 5-minute window per recorder process (reconnects/toggles do not extend it). Each single-track media stream with at least one accepted RTP packet emits its own structured entry, keyed by four network-order 32-bit UUID words (session_uuid_0..3, and camera_uuid_0..3 for registered cameras; unregistered cameras log camera_registered=false).

Log fieldMeaning
first_rtp_ticks, latest_rtp_ticks, clock_rate_hzRaw 32-bit RTP timestamps (elapsed_ticks * 1000 / clock_rate_hz = ms; use extended fields across wrap).
missing_sequence_numbers, backward_timestamp_stepsSequence gaps and backward PTS steps (reordering can cause backward steps; neither alone proves a clock reset).
sender_rtp_ticks, sender_ntp_seconds, sender_ntp_fractionLatest matching RTCP SR RTP/NTP clock (camera-supplied; may be absent or inaccurate).
latest_audio_minus_video_play_usAudio minus video position on camera PLAY timeline (positive = audio ahead; interleaving affects readings).

Readings precede transcoding, timeline normalization, and presentation offsets; measure audible sync with a flash/tone preview or physical clap. Newly recorded RTSP files retain per-sample source timing for the file’s lifetime; older files retain stored PTS only. When long GOPs or repeated clock discontinuities exhaust a file’s metadata budget, KeepPeek logs recording recovery budget exhausted, preserves the valid prefix, and rotates at the next keyframe (leaving a coverage gap until that keyframe).

Calibrating live audio

Open a camera’s Audio synchronization settings and select Live audio to align live audio and video on the Main or Sub stream:

  1. Produce a clearly visible sound (such as a clap) on the target stream and listen across several repetitions to separate constant offset from network/browser jitter.
  2. Enter a whole-millisecond offset from -200 through +200: use a positive offset to delay audio when sound arrives early, or a negative offset to delay video when sound arrives late (preview displays the added video latency).
  3. Select Apply preview to test in the current viewer, Reset preview to restore saved timing, then save as the camera default or as a per-stream override (0 disables correction; Inherit uses the camera default).

Live timing is independent of recorded-audio sync and never modifies stored recordings or exports. Live diagnostics show saved vs. applied timing separately from browser jitter-buffer delay, and HealthCommand / camera health exposes live_audio held bytes, held encoded video frames, and capacity/expiry drops (rising drops indicate the server’s time or memory hold budget was exceeded). Measurements end at the server listener handoff; camera capture, external transcoders, speakers, and physical distance require separate measurement.

Further references: recording integrity, event presentation, and evidence exports.

Guest review links

This page covers creating, opening, and revoking bounded guest review links on /share, deployment prerequisites in Settings → Access → Guest sharing, management in Settings → Access → Guest links, transport peer classification, and security/rate limits. Guest links grant temporary access to one completed export or one bounded recording range without a KeepPeek account.

Video walkthrough

Watch enabling the guest sharing HTTPS policy, creating a bounded guest link, opening the isolated /share review page, and revoking the grant:

Prepare the deployment

Guest sharing defaults off. Before creating links, an Administrator must configure the external HTTPS origin and storage prerequisites:

  1. Open Settings → Access → Guest sharing, set External HTTPS origin, enable sharing, and save. Use a canonical HTTPS origin such as https://review.example or https://review.example:8443 (no path, credentials, query, fragment, or trailing slash).
  2. Supply DNS, valid HTTPS termination, reverse proxy, firewall rules, and recipient reachability. KeepPeek provides no tunnel or relay and does not verify external reachability.
  3. Configure [access].trusted_proxies explicitly if behind a reverse proxy and preserve the expected external Host and Origin headers. See Authentication and access control and Guest review deployment policy.

Manual edits to [sharing] in config.toml apply on restart (do not edit the server-owned revision field); use Settings for live changes.

Operational database boundary: Grant verifiers, revocations, and usage counters live in a private operational database under the identity-bound metadata owner (separate from config.toml, requiring private local filesystem permissions and NTFS on Windows). Never construct or relocate this directory manually. Configuration ZIP exports do not include this database; keep sharing disabled during metadata recovery because an older backup is not current revocation evidence.

Create a guest review link

KeepPeek supports two creation paths, both capped at 2 minutes and 512 MiB and requiring H.264 video with supported AAC audio:

Option A: Share a completed export

  1. In Keep → Export, create an export as the Administrator who will share it, wait for Ready, and select Create guest link. (Requires a retained local export with file expiry, creator/camera identity, and measured media proof; archive-only and legacy exports require creating a fresh export.)
  2. Inspect camera, measured file bounds (which may start before the requested range to include a leading keyframe), gaps, byte size, and file expiry. This path shares the entire previewed MP4 without trimming.
  3. Set display name, optional private note, expiry (<= 24 hours and <= export expiry), playback/download permissions (Download defaults off), and any positive usage limits, then create the link.
  4. Copy and send the link while the dialog is open. KeepPeek holds the secret only in dialog memory—after closing, Guest links can revoke the grant but never recover the secret.

If creation times out or is unconfirmed, retain the displayed reference and check/revoke that grant in Settings → Access → Guest links before creating another; retrying cannot recover a lost secret.

Option B: Share a recording range

  1. Select Share recording range from Keep → Export, an event’s detail drawer, or beside a saved bookmark. Event and bookmark actions use the event’s exact recorded start and end without padding (open events and missing recordings cannot supply a bounded interval).
  2. KeepPeek reuses a matching ready export you own or prepares a new local export for the camera and stream. Review both the selected interval and measured file bounds, gaps, and expiry.
    • KeepPeek declines the range if any keyframe, audio sample, or trailing frame extends outside the selected interval, or if the stream has a nonzero recorded-audio correction. Adjust bounds in Keep when needed.
    • In Keep, your selected stream is preserved. From events or bookmarks, KeepPeek tries the latest-expiring matching ready file first (even if the original recording expired) and falls back to an available alternate stream if the primary stream has incompatible video, crosses a boundary, or uses corrected audio.
    • Use Reload preview to inspect a failed preparation job, or Prepare a fresh export to start a new attempt (for example, to replace a legacy file lacking source proof).
  3. Configure permissions, expiry, and usage limits, create the link, and copy it immediately before closing the dialog.

What the guest receives (/share)

Opening the link loads the isolated /share page, which strips the secret URL fragment before the application initializes and exchanges it via /guest/session for a scoped Secure, HttpOnly cookie (__Host-keeppeek-guest).

Guest capability / boundaryBehavior
Allowed actionsStream and seek the shared MP4 (requires browser H.264/AAC decode), Download MP4 when permitted, and End review (ends session without revoking link).
Denied accessNo KeepPeek account, role, control channel, live wall, other recordings, events/search, snapshots, camera controls, Talk, settings, health, or logs.
Tab isolationA second guest tab cannot switch an existing tab to different evidence via the shared cookie.
Usage accountingFirst delivered media bytes durably commit 1 view (seeks in that session reuse it); downloads count per started transfer. Page loads and failed checks consume 0 uses; crashes count conservatively and committed uses are never refunded.
HTTP boundary checks/share and /guest/* require HTTPS, exact configured Host and Origin, reject Authorization headers, and enforce bounded request sizes.

Revoke, invalidation, and restart behavior

Inspect usage counters and revoke links with a reason in Settings → Access → Guest links.

  • No retention hold: Storage retention, manual deletion, missing storage, or a replaced export can end media availability before grant expiry.
  • Immediate invalidation triggers: Camera removal, active camera privacy, creator issuer revision or Administrator role loss, or any sharing policy change ends active sessions and blocks delivery. Source bindings track a configured camera incarnation, not historical physical hardware identity.
  • Uncertain policy write: Suspends guest access until an Administrator reloads the policy and saves cleanly (reloading camera settings does not clear this suspension).
  • Server restart: Terminates active sessions and transfers while preserving grants, revocations, and committed counters. Reopening requires the original link and remaining usage allowance.

Limits, peer classification, and rate budgets

ScopeHard limit
Active grants256 globally, 32 per creator
Grant history & cleanup4,096 retained records (creation rejected when full); purged 30 days after earlier of expiry/revocation (256/batch while running; all overdue purged on startup)
Evidence size & duration<= 512 MiB, <= 2 minutes, H.264 + supported AAC
Grant & session lifetimesGrant <= 24 hours (capped by export expiry); session <= 5 minutes (stops at expiry)
Concurrent sessions8 globally, 2 per grant (2 concurrent transfers per session)
Egress bandwidth pacing16 MiB/s per classified peer address, 64 MiB/s globally
Request budgets (60 s window, up to 128 tracked peer addresses)Exchange: 20 / address, 120 global · Status: 240 / 1,200 · Media: 120 / 480 · Download: 12 / 48

Transport peer classification: When a guest request arrives through a proxy listed in [access].trusted_proxies, per-address request and egress budgets use the classified client IP from X-Forwarded-For. For all other requests, KeepPeek uses the direct transport peer address and ignores forwarding headers.

Digital zoom and pan

This page covers browser-side digital zoom and pan (1.0x–8.0x) on already-decoded media in Viewer (/viewer), Keep recorded playback (/keep), and Events detail images (/events). Digital zoom enlarges local pixels only—it never moves a camera, switches streams, changes source resolution, or modifies recordings and exports.

Video walkthrough

Watch 1.0x–8.0x browser-side Digital zoom, bounded drag and keyboard panning, and instant reset—independent of physical PTZ:

Supported surfaces

SurfaceRouteDigital zoom supportedNotes
Viewer/viewerYesShares one top control row with Live/History, quality, and camera info.
Keep player/keepYesPlayback controls, timeline, and fullscreen chrome stay outside zoom.
Event detail image/eventsYesEvent bounding boxes scale and pan with the canonical image.
Dashboard tiles/NoCompact wall tiles do not capture inspection gestures; open /viewer.
Camera filmstrip/viewerNoSelect a camera into the focused viewport first.

Controls, gestures, and shortcuts

The Digital toolbar sits at the top-left above the media frame without obstructing the image (scrolling horizontally in Viewer rather than wrapping). Magnification spans 1.0x (full fitted frame) through 8.0x, preserves aspect ratio, and clamps panning to the fitted image rectangle so the media cannot be dragged out of view.

InputBehavior
Digital zoom in buttonDoubles magnification around center, up to 8.0x.
Digital zoom out buttonHalves magnification around center, down to 1.0x.
Reset digital zoom buttonReturns immediately to 1.0x, centered.
Two-finger pinch inside imageZooms around finger midpoint; moving the midpoint pans simultaneously.
Mouse, pen, or one-finger dragPans while magnification is above 1.0x.
Alt + wheel/trackpad (Option Mac)Zooms around pointer position.
Double-click or double-tapToggles between 1.0x and 2.0x around pointer or tap point.
+ or =Zooms in when the inspection viewport has focus.
-Zooms out when the inspection viewport has focus.
0Resets to 1.0x when focused.
Arrow keysPans by 40 px per press while zoomed (> 1.0x) and focused.
EscapeResets digital zoom first, before normal Viewer or event-detail close behavior.

Browser and accessibility behavior:

  • Ordinary wheel scrolling and Ctrl/Cmd zoom shortcuts remain browser-owned; touch gestures are captured only inside the focused image viewport.
  • Tab reaches the named Digital buttons; activating any button focuses the inspection surface for keyboard controls.
  • Peek desktop control groups are 32 px high; touch targets are at least 44 px. Buttons expose visible focus rings and disabled states at 1.0x / 8.0x, and magnification is announced via role="meter". Zoom and reset do not depend on animated transitions.

Keep playback and fullscreen interaction

  • Unscaled transport chrome: Keep’s play/pause, skip, scrubber, volume, mute, speed, and fullscreen controls stay outside the transformed video layer. Scrubbing the timeline always changes recording time, never the crop.
  • Arrow-key precedence: When the zoomed image (> 1.0x) has focus, arrow keys pan. At 1.0x, or when focus is outside the viewport, pause the recording and use Left/Right to step recorded frames (or approximate frame intervals on older recorders/unsupported browsers).
  • Portrait phone layout: The Digital bar stays above the image while Playback options opens volume, speed, and stream quality in a separate sheet without replacing the recording or moving the playhead. Changing stream quality requests different media; Digital enlarges only already-decoded pixels.
  • Fullscreen: Expands the full recorded player including controls and recomputes pan/zoom bounds on entry and exit. Browser-native Escape can exit fullscreen before the page sees the key. A disabled fullscreen button means the Fullscreen API is unavailable in that context; digital zoom still works.

Reset rules and evidence boundaries

TriggerEffect on digital zoom
Switch camera, recording segment, or canonical imageResets to 1.0x, centered.
Play, pause, or normal playback progressPreserves current magnification and pan offset.
Window resize, device rotation, decoded size changeRecomputes pan bounds for the current viewport size.
Copy moment link, create bookmark, export MP4Inspection crop is never saved; exports contain original media.

Event bounding boxes use the canonical image’s coordinate space and move with it. Controls, camera info, and diagnostics never scale, and live camera information reports digital magnification separately from decoded resolution.

Digital zoom vs. physical PTZ

Digital zoom is always labelled Digital, requires no PTZ permission or PTZ hardware, and works on recordings and offline-camera event snapshots. Physical pan, tilt, and optical zoom remain separate camera commands subject to device capability and access grants. See Camera controls.

Magnifying a low-resolution stream cannot recover detail absent from the encoded video—switch to a higher stream quality when you need more decoded pixels.

Camera and stream health

This page covers KeepPeek’s server-authoritative health model (contract version 1), fleet and client health diagnostics on /system-health, single-camera diagnosis on /system-health/camera/[cameraId], the foreground terminal dashboard (--tui), durable operational outage intervals, HealthCommand, and Prometheus GET /metrics. For historical footage coverage see Recording and evidence; for alert delivery see Notifications and integrations.

KeepPeek computes camera and stream health once on the server. Peek, Cameras, Health, camera diagnosis, fleet counts, the foreground TUI, the control API, and /metrics all project that same state. Clients never infer health from display text, local video decode, or alert severity, and must map unknown future states or reason codes to unknown—never healthy.

Video walkthrough

Watch triaging prioritized fleet findings on Health (/system-health), opening single-camera diagnosis (/system-health/camera/[cameraId]), and switching an affected camera to TCP transport:

Investigate an unhealthy camera

  1. Open Cameras (/cameras) and filter by Not healthy (every state except healthy) to narrow affected devices, or open Health (/system-health) for prioritized fleet findings and refresh on demand.
  2. On /system-health, switch between the Server tab (fleet counts, camera/stream dimensions, host CPU/memory/disks, WebRTC sources including live_audio) and the Client tab (this browser’s WebRTC session, ICE state, inbound tracks, receive bitrate, packet loss, jitter, RTT, and decoder implementation).
  3. Select a camera finding to open Camera diagnosis (/system-health/camera/[cameraId]), which presents server-observed TRANSPORT, FRAMES, DECODABLE, and RECORDING evidence, stream drop/error counters, Pre-recording buffer history/memory state, and an ordered checklist:
    1. Open the camera on this network (http://<ip>) to verify power and reachability.
    2. Note that credentials are write-only (no authentication probe endpoint exists).
    3. Test TCP (available when configured transport is UDP and keeppeek.runtime-config.v1 is supported) to switch RTSP transport to TCP without changing other settings.
    4. Open logs (/settings/logs) for redacted server diagnostics.
  4. Compare transport, advancing frames, keyframes, and recording progress separately: a connected socket can carry stale/undecodable media, and a live browser picture never proves recording. Check Recording integrity (/recordings) for retained historical coverage.
  5. Inspect native camera events and external analysis services separately—their failures never mark healthy camera media or recording offline.

Health findings are live snapshot evidence and clear automatically once positive evidence recovers; they have no dismiss action (alert acknowledgement belongs to the notification inbox). Health panels also follow role permissions—if a User can view video but cannot load fleet health, check Authentication and access control. A failed refresh marks the view unavailable without rewriting the last server state.

Foreground terminal dashboard (--tui)

Running KeepPeek in the foreground supports a read-only, non-blocking terminal dashboard controlled by --tui:

ModeBehavior
--tui autoDefault. Starts the TUI when stdin, stdout, and stderr are interactive terminals with screen support; otherwise logs plain text.
--tui alwaysRequests the TUI; prints a notice and continues with plain logs if no interactive terminal is attached.
--tui neverRuns with plain console logs only.
  • Bounded collection: Samples server health single-flight every 2 s and derives camera warnings from live camera state rather than querying stored event history. If a sample is >= 5 s old or collection fails, the header retains the last successful snapshot with a STALE badge and sample/failure age.
  • Columns and detail: Displays host process CPU %, RSS MiB, active viewers, connected/recording video counts, KeepPeek storage usage/pressure, and per-stream rows (camera name plus source ID on terminals >= 115 columns wide or on name collisions, Main/Sub video or audio kind, canonical state, decimal kbit/s bitrate retaining stale values with a stale label, and report/frame age). Selecting a row shows source ID, media type, recording progress age, counters, and health reason. 0 is a measured value; — means unavailable or not applicable.
  • Controls: Up/Down move selection, PageUp/PageDown page rows, q exits to plain logs (server keeps running), and Ctrl+C stops the server.

Authoritative health model

Evidence dimensions

DimensionServer-observed evidence
Configured and expectedCamera configuration, recording policy, and expected stream IDs
TransportRouter lifecycle plus expected vs. connected stream IDs
Report freshnessMonotonic age of the latest periodic ingress report
Frame freshnessMonotonic age of the latest advancing frame counter
DecodabilityMonotonic age of the latest advancing video keyframe counter
Ingress qualityMeasured FPS plus per-window reconnect, drop, and error deltas
RecordingRequested stream IDs, writer attempts, successful MP4 appends, and bounded writer errors
Battery sleepFresh registration heartbeat with no pending wake request from the optional battery wake service

Canonical states

StateMeaning
startingInside initial startup evidence grace period (20 s).
healthyOnly healthy state. Required transport, report, frame, keyframe, FPS, and recording evidence is current.
degradedMedia is current, but partial transport, keyframes, recording, FPS, errors, drops, or reconnects failed.
staleStream report is missing/stale, or frame counters stopped advancing.
reconnectingTransport is reconnecting while recent media evidence remains within the offline threshold.
offlineTransport is disconnected/reconnecting without recent media evidence.
stoppedMedia is not expected, shutting down, stopped, or a registered battery camera is sleeping.
unknownServer or client lacks authoritative evidence (never counted as connected, fresh, decodable, or healthy).

Precedence order

The server evaluates rules in strict order; the first match supplies canonical state and primary reason:

  1. Not expected, shutting down, or stopped → stopped (not_expected).
  2. Fresh battery registration with no connected transport and no pending wake → stopped (battery_sleeping). An accepted wake stays pending until connected media clears it, so wake failures become reconnecting or offline.
  3. Startup grace (< 20 s uptime) with incomplete or transiently unhealthy evidence → starting (starting); a complete healthy snapshot promotes immediately.
  4. Reconnecting transport → reconnecting (transport_reconnecting) while report age <= 90 s, else offline (transport_disconnected).
  5. Missing lifecycle and report → unknown (evidence_unavailable); missing report (no_stream_report), stale report (stream_report_stale), or non-advancing frames (frames_not_arriving) → stale.
  6. Any degraded condition → degraded, choosing primary reason in order: transport_partially_connected, keyframes_missing, recording_not_progressing, frames_below_expected, ingress_errors, ingress_drops, ingress_reconnects (all matching codes appear in reason_codes).
  7. Missing required evidence dimension → unknown (evidence_unavailable).
  8. All required evidence current → healthy (healthy).

A connected socket never overrides stale frames, missing keyframes, or stalled recording; a decoded browser frame or fresh capability snapshot never promotes server health.

Thresholds, jitter, and recovery

All evidence ages use server monotonic clocks (wall timestamps are for display only; clock jumps never make stale evidence fresh).

SignalThreshold / rule
Ingress report cadencePublished every 10 s (first report establishes baseline for cumulative reconnect/drop/error counters).
Startup grace20 s (two ingress report intervals).
Stream report stale> 30 s (tolerates one missed 10 s report without flapping).
Reconnecting → offlineLatest stream report age > 90 s.
Frame freshnessmax(20 s, 3 / expected_fps), capped at 120 s; also requires a fresh stream report.
Keyframe freshness3 × configured GOP (or 3 × observed keyframe interval), clamped to 30–120 s.
Recording progress freshshort_term_buffer + flush_interval + 30 s.
Frame-arrival jitter (P99)Absolute deviation from configured frame interval over 10 s window (not WebRTC jitter); diagnostic only. Max observed frame gap > 2 s remains actionable.

Asymmetric recovery: Failing states wait through the bounded debounce thresholds above, while positive evidence recovers immediately—an advancing frame or keyframe counter refreshes age, a successful MP4 append clears a bounded writer error, per-report deltas clear prior drop/error/reconnect penalties, and a fresh report clears stale state.

Operational outage intervals

KeepPeek projects four independent durable event kinds from the health dimensions:

Event kindScopeTrigger evidence
camera_offlineCameraNo expected transport connected, or aggregate transport disconnected
stream_staleCamera streamStream report missing/stale, or frames stopped advancing
decode_unavailableCamera streamNo recent decodable keyframe
recording_interruptedCamera streamRecording requested by policy, but writer progress stopped

Candidate intervals begin immediately on evidence loss and use monotonic elapsed time for hold-downs and recovery debounce (defaults under [operational_events]: warning_hold_down_secs = 15, outage_hold_down_secs = 60, recovery_debounce_secs = 10, record_short_flaps = false; per-camera overrides by ID or IP inherit omitted globals; bounds 0 s–24 h with warning <= outage). See Operational events.

  • Recovery before warning hold-down discards the candidate unless record_short_flaps = true.
  • Visible intervals keep one stable event ID; severity/cause changes and stable recovery increment revision, recording recovery time and total duration from initial evidence loss.
  • Open intervals restore from the recording catalog after restart, render alongside recording coverage on the timeline, and link from Health findings. Catalog persistence precedes notification delivery, so provider failures never alter or close health intervals.

Session durations, fleet counts, API, and /metrics

Current-process session durations reset on server restart or registry replacement and do not claim retained archive coverage:

  • session_duration_ms: longest observed ingress-worker duration across the camera’s expected video streams.
  • recorded_main_duration_ms / recorded_sub_duration_ms: video sample durations accumulated only after successful MP4 appends during the current run.
  • recorded_total_duration_ms: sum across requested streams (can exceed elapsed wall time when Main and Sub record concurrently).

Fleet counts aggregate expected video streams separately per dimension (configured, connected, fresh, decodable, recording_requested, recording, unknown). Audio freshness stays on each stream snapshot and is never folded into video totals.

Stable API fields: Every camera and stream exposes state, primary reason, ordered reason_codes, bounded human-readable detail, and raw dimensions. Automate on state and reason/reason_codes, not display text. HealthCommand (version 1) returns the protobuf snapshot including WebRtcSourceHealthSnapshot.live_audio timing occupancy and drop counters; see Calibrating live audio.

Prometheus GET /metrics exposes the identical projection (with no legacy online or degraded gauge—use camera_info state labels and explicit dimension gauges):

Metric familyPurpose
keeppeek_cameras_{connected,fresh,decodable,recording_requested,recording}Fleet camera counts per independent evidence dimension.
keeppeek_video_streams_{configured,connected,fresh,decodable,recording_requested,recording}Fleet expected video stream counts per dimension.
keeppeek_camera_infoPer-camera info gauge carrying canonical state label.
keeppeek_camera_health_dimension / _known (+ per-stream equivalents)Explicit dimension values paired with availability (_known) gauges.
keeppeek_operational_event_activeActive operational outage intervals labeled by camera, stream, kind, and severity.

Visual configuration management

This chapter covers visual configuration across /settings and /cameras, including draft vs. live-apply vs. restart behavior, browser-local preferences, shared camera inheritance, camera templates, and runtime profiles. Configuration writes require Administrator access (Users can change only browser-local theme settings and observe active runtime-profile status); KeepPeek stores all durable settings in config.toml and file-backed secrets in sibling secrets.toml with no raw TOML editor, no general secret editor, and no blind overwrites.

Video walkthrough

Edit notification rules, cloud push, or MQTT/settings#notifications, /settings#integrationsNoSee Notifications and integrations.
Export or apply full settings + secrets.toml ZIP/settings#backupsYes (apply only)See Export and import configuration.
Change UI theme or view server identity / logs/settings#appearance, /settings/logsNoTheme is browser-local; system fields are read-only evidence.
Configure file-only native camera eventsStopped config.toml ([cameras.<name>.events])Yes (manual restart)See Native camera events.

Persistence, drafts, and restart boundaries

Every persisted write holds the shared configuration-update lock, checks the edit-start revision, validates the complete candidate with the startup loaders, preserves unrelated TOML sections and {secret:KEY} / {secret:KEY|url} references, and atomically replaces config.toml with owner-only permissions. The browser never receives resolved secret values.

Change classExamplesWhen it takes effectFailure or reload behavior
Browser-local preferenceSettings → Appearance & time theme (Dark, Light, Match system)Immediately in this browser onlyNever written to config.toml; video surfaces always remain dark.
Live durable applySingle-camera edit, /cameras defaults/templates/bulk plan, Runtime profilesCommits atomically, then activates workersIf a worker fails to activate, persisted settings stay saved and the UI reports the per-camera reconnect or restart action.
Staged save (restart required)Edit server (Save server settings), Change storage (Continue to review → Stage storage changes), Edit volume draft, Edit archive settings (Save archive settings), Confirm metadata moveOnly after Apply changes, Restart and move storage, or service restartCurrent runtime configuration stays active until restart; navigating away prompts before discarding unsaved drafts.

Browser-local appearance and read-only system evidence

Under /settings#appearance, Appearance & time persists only your local browser theme (Dark, Light, or Match system). Language is fixed to English, Browser reduced motion reflects your OS/browser preference, and Server time zone, Clock preference, and Week starts are unavailable rather than cosmetic overrides—timeline and export timestamps stay unambiguous UTC.

Under System & updates, server version, host, OS, process, uptime, and camera-catalog metadata are read-only health evidence. Update channel, Update check, and Erase all recordings are intentionally disabled; use Restart to bounce the recorder cleanly, Open logs (/settings/logs) or Download diagnostics for scrubbed troubleshooting bundles, and Upgrades and migrations for version updates.

Camera fleet configuration (/cameras)

Open Cameras and select Configuration (or select cameras and choose Manage selected) to manage shared defaults, templates, and bulk updates. This surface requires the keeppeek.configuration.v1 capability; if unavailable, the camera fleet stays readable while write controls remain disabled.

Inheritance and secret references

Inheritable camera policies resolve in strict order: Built-in fallback → Shared default ([camera_defaults]) → Explicit camera override. The Overview and Defaults tabs show the shared default, camera override, final effective value, source, and whether the live camera worker currently matches the saved value.

  • Use inherited value deletes a camera override so future shared-default edits flow to that camera; it does not freeze the current value.
  • Use built-in value deletes a shared default.
  • Credential fields display configured-state booleans only and accept complete {secret:KEY} references. Define the matching key in local secrets.toml; never enter raw secret values in templates.

Preview and apply a fleet change

  1. Open Configuration on /cameras and choose the Defaults or Bulk tab.
  2. For Bulk changes, select a target scope: explicit camera IDs, the complete filtered result (including rows outside the virtualized viewport), one configured group, or all configured cameras (requires explicit confirmation).
  3. Choose a field patch or a saved template, then request a server preview.
  4. Inspect the authoritative camera count, semantic old/new values, skipped targets, validation issues, and reconnect or restart impact. Resolve any missing targets or validation errors before continuing.
  5. Apply the active preview, then verify per-camera activation status and Camera and stream health.

Fleet plan bounds: A plan targets at most 64 cameras, expires after 10 minutes, and must fit within the 64 KiB WebRTC control-message ceiling (oversized previews fail explicitly—split into smaller batches or fewer fields). The server retains at most 128 plans.

Create and exchange camera templates

In the Templates tab, create, edit, duplicate, delete, import, or export named templates stored in [configuration_templates] inside config.toml. Templates carry supported camera connection and recording-policy fields plus {secret:KEY} credential references; they do not store secret values or notification rules.

  • Applying a template writes explicit camera overrides onto the selected targets. Later edits or deletion of the template never mutate previously configured cameras.
  • Template exchange uses versioned JSON (not configuration ZIP or TOML), bounded to 16 KiB and 64 templates with case-insensitive unique names.
  • Import preview validates before mutation and rejects unsupported versions, unknown fields, duplicate IDs or names, inline credentials, invalid enums, and out-of-range numbers.

Runtime profiles (/settings#runtime-profiles)

Runtime profiles let an Administrator switch camera behavior between Base and up to 32 named profiles stored in [runtime_profiles] in config.toml. Profiles commit atomically and converge asynchronously across camera workers without restarting the recorder.

  • Activation boundary: Manage and activate profiles only in Settings → Runtime profiles or via an authorized WebRTC StateStoreCommand client on service/runtime-profiles/. No HTTP, MQTT, Home Assistant, CLI, webhook, or scheduled activation surface exists.
  • Target identity: Up to 127 camera overrides per profile are keyed by server-managed profile_identity UUIDs (identity_schema = 1 rollback guard). Editing a camera’s IP preserves its UUID; deleting and re-adding a camera creates a new UUID and leaves the old target listed as unavailable (configured camera is missing) until removed or Select Base is chosen.
  • Allowed override fields only: Each camera entry accepts only Availability, Recording (off, sub, main, both, event-boost, event-only), Privacy (feeds PrivacyRegistry; cannot weaken an active privacy schedule), and Notifications (suppresses candidates and queued outbox delivery without editing saved rules). Omitted fields inherit Base; profiles cannot enable a disabled Base camera or Base off recording.
  • Convergence and recovery: Visible cameras report pending, applied, or degraded with per-field provenance (base or profile). Camera stops share a 6-second deadline; failures or timeouts stay degraded (never silently reverting to Base) while restrictive privacy stays closed. After fixing the underlying camera or storage fault, choose Reapply active policy (requires no unsaved draft) or Select Base.

Resolve conflicts and activation failures

SymptomCauseNext action
Configuration changed while editingCompare-and-set revision mismatch (no blind overwrite)Review the reloaded current values against your retained local draft and request a new preview. Single-camera edits track config.toml revision; fleet/template edits track combined config + template revision so template changes do not conflict with single-camera drafts.
Preview expiredPlan exceeded its 10-minute lifetimeRequest and review a fresh preview before applying.
Missing target or oversized previewTarget camera deleted or response exceeds 64 KiB / 64 camerasUpdate target selection or split into smaller explicit batches.
Validation errorOut-of-range value, unknown field, or malformed {secret:KEY}Fix the highlighted field using the configuration reference.
Saved value differs from runtimeCamera worker activation failed or requires restartFollow the reported per-camera reconnect/restart action, then verify health and effective values.
Runtime profile camera stays degradedWorker stop timeout, missing credentials, storage write failure, or deleted camera UUIDFix the camera/storage fault or remove stale unavailable targets, save/discard drafts, then select Reapply active policy.
Write controls disabledMissing keeppeek.configuration.v1 or non-Administrator roleCheck server version and sign in as an Administrator.

For the full schema, defaults, and bounds, see the configuration reference and configuration engineering guide.

Configuration reference

This chapter is the canonical reference for every supported on-disk section, serialized Rust struct, field, type, default, limit, ownership boundary, apply mode, and secret-reference rule in config.toml and its companion secrets.toml. KeepPeek stores all durable settings—including layouts, notification rules, credential records, templates, runtime profiles, and saved searches—in config.toml rather than separate settings files; media files and catalog databases are data, not settings stores.

Use visual configuration for routine UI administration and configuration exchange for validated export and import.
