  4. Track progress or cancel before authoritative publication under Move jobs. Stop after current request halts further batch confirmations while leaving already-admitted jobs running.

Each move copies into a confined temporary file, verifies byte length and SHA-256, publishes the new catalog owner, and retires the source copy only after active readers finish. Interrupted jobs resume from their durable journal, and lost confirmation replies reconcile against the original job ID. An expired or stale preview requires a fresh review. Removing a volume definition requires stopped writes, zero owned/reserved bytes, no unfinished jobs or cleanup receipts, and no active archive-policy references; removing a definition leaves its directory and unrelated files untouched.

3. Relocate metadata (catalog and export history)

  1. Create and save a disabled named volume assigned only the metadata role.
  2. Under Catalog and export history, select that destination and choose Preview metadata move to review downtime and required bytes.
  3. Choose Confirm metadata move (locks storage settings until restart or Cancel pending metadata move), then trigger Restart and move storage or restart the service.

Startup copies and verifies both the catalog snapshot and export history before media workers resume, retaining the previous files for recovery. A missing bound root stays offline and is never recreated. If activation fails, preserve both copies and the pending configuration—never edit journals, overwrite a conflicting destination, or clear fencing records. See Named storage volumes.

Verify and roll back

After startup:

  1. Check server version, camera count, credentials/grants, dashboards, storage roots, and integration status.
  2. Test live playback, one newly finalized recording, and one known older recording; check /recordings integrity and any retained export/maintenance history.
  3. Reconnect remote clients (sessions and browser keys do not survive restart).

Rollback boundary: Configuration ZIP automatic rollback covers only config.toml and secrets.toml—never application binaries, catalog schema upgrades, or storage moves. If an upgrade fails, stop KeepPeek, preserve the failed directory state, restore an isolated complete pre-upgrade copy (binary + config + catalog + media), and verify it before replacing the failed deployment. Never run an older binary against the sole copy of a potentially migrated catalog. Record results and gaps in the qualification record.

Recording maintenance

This chapter covers fleet recording-integrity checks (/recordings), explicit plan-then-confirm recording deletion and bounded catalog reconciliation (/recordings/maintenance), and Administrator-confirmed fourth-tier archive retention (Settings → Storage & retention → Local archive). Reading /recordings requires User access; all deletion, reconciliation, and archive-retention mutations require Administrator access and the relevant server capability. Never delete recording files directly on disk or manually clear catalog rows or private staging directories to force progress.

Video walkthrough

Watch Administrator recording deletion preview, typed DELETE N confirmation, durable ledger execution, and bounded catalog reconciliation on /recordings/maintenance.

Recording integrity and coverage (/recordings)

Recording integrity (/recordings, backed by GET /recording-coverage) joins effective camera policy, live writer telemetry, keyframe-indexed finalized fragments, and the deletion ledger to prove whether requested streams are actually recording. It auto-refreshes every 30 seconds while visible and links Administrators directly to Maintenance (/recordings/maintenance) and Open Keep (/keep).

ElementCurrent contract & bounds
Fleet stateshealthy, degraded, paused_by_policy, not_configured (shown only when nonzero), unknown. Policy-disabled streams do not generate unexpected gaps.
Playable coverageCounts only finalized, random-access fragments with indexed keyframes (merges gaps ≤1 ms). Ongoing trailing gaps keep end_ms: null (observed_end_ms marks snapshot time).
Filters & intervals24h, 7d, 30d (API max 31 days), camera search (≤128 chars), state, stream (main/sub), group, and minimum camera gap (≥1m to ≥1h).
Pagination & detail bounds25 cameras/page (API 1–50), bound to catalog_revision with 15-minute page_token expiry (HTTP 409 resets pagination). Up to 256 exact recent ranges and 256 deletion intervals per stream (10,000-entry ledger); aggregated strips use 15m / 1h / 6h buckets with exact totals.

See Recording and evidence for coverage interpretation and recording integrity guide for Prometheus /metrics.

Storage safety invariants

  • Manual vs. automatic retention: Automatic retention runs separately from manual deletion. Active manual claims exclude their recordings from automatic retention and new playback/export resolution until they reach a terminal state.
  • Named storage volumes: Deletion follows each recording’s current catalog owner (including relocated recordings), requires the bound root to be online and writable, and blocks while readers or unfinished moves hold the object. Interrupted jobs retain capacity ownership until durable checkpoints finish.
  • Active files (.active): Startup preserves .active files and catalog rows in every root without assuming temporary names authorize deletion. Writers create .active files exclusively and fail rather than truncating or replacing an existing path.
  • No side-effect removals: Bookmarks do not protect footage from deletion, ready exports are not deleted when source recordings are removed, and unknown files are never automatically adopted or deleted.

Plan-then-confirm recording deletion (/recordings/maintenance)

Requires Administrator access and keeppeek.recording-maintenance.v1 over WebRTC. Before deleting irreplaceable footage, retain and independently verify an evidence export or archive recovery copy.

Deletion runbook

  1. Open Recording integrity → Maintenance (/recordings/maintenance, Recordings tab), or choose Review deletion from Keep’s export-range view to carry the camera, stream, and UTC interval over (closing Keep also releases its playback cursor).
  2. Select Camera, Stream (Sub or Main), Reason (Operator cleanup or Privacy request), and half-open UTC Start (UTC) / End (UTC) bounds ([start, end)), then choose Preview deletion.
  3. Review every listed whole-recording boundary, total bytes, catalog revision, gaps, bookmarks, related exports, and stated consequences. Selections containing active, protected, retention-pending, unknown-end, or identity-unavailable recordings cannot be confirmed.
  4. Close competing playback and wait for active exports, type the exact prompt text (for example DELETE 1), and choose Delete permanently. Choose Keep recordings to dismiss without starting, or Cancel job to cancel prepared/queued admission.
  5. Track per-object outcomes; use Refresh job after disconnects, Retry failed objects for recoverable failures, and Download report to save a redacted JSON record (contains no confirmation nonce or host paths).

A failed confirmation consumes its server nonce—choose Refresh preview, review the fresh snapshot, and re-enter the confirmation text. Losing Administrator access or the capability immediately discards the active review and any late preview responses.

Scope, durability, and crash-recovery contract

  • Whole-object scope & hard limits: Maintenance addresses stable source/stream/recording IDs only (never display names or caller paths) and selects every whole recording overlapping [start, end) without trimming boundary files. Limits: ≤128 objects/plan, ≤31-day range, ≤4,096 catalog rows inspected (exceeding limits fails explicitly rather than returning partial scope), 10-minute plan expiry (wall + monotonic; restart invalidates prepared plans), ≤128 prepared plans, and ≤256 confirmed/cancelled jobs (expired/terminal records older than 30 days may be pruned; unresolved claims are never evicted).
  • Identity binding & concurrency: Preparation locks an opaque device/file fingerprint, catalog revision, requester identity, and hashed nonce. One deletion worker runs at a time, shares the configuration-update lock with restore/access/export retries, and rechecks session authorization before filesystem mutations. Confirmation is idempotent across lost replies and restarts.
  • Restart recovery: Startup runs a 2-second bounded recovery pass before recording pipelines start. It settles catalog completion only when staging-directory filesystem identity and absence checks prove unlink already finished—it never moves or deletes present media. Abandoned Working items report Failed and retain reservations for authenticated Retry failed objects (available even on cancelled jobs). Cancelling interrupted work never releases a claim unless the original selected file is positively identified.
  • Audit evidence: Access audit links outcomes to the durable job ID; structured recording_maintenance logs record requester, scope, expanded bounds, reason, revision, counts, and outcome (hold_override is always false; paths, nonces, and raw failures are redacted).
Job / object stateMeaning
PreparedShort-lived preview awaiting confirmation; no recordings reserved.
ExpiredPlan timed out or catalog restarted; repeat Preview deletion.
QueuedConfirmation and per-object ledger committed atomically; ready for execution.
WorkingLive deletion worker owns the object.
StagedFile moved into private maintenance staging with durable directory-identity checkpoint.
DeletedUnlink verified and catalog completion recorded (irreversible).
FailedRemoval did not finish; claim retained for inspection or Retry failed objects.
CancelledUnstarted objects will not execute; already-deleted objects stay deleted; failed/staged items remain inspectable/retryable.

Administrator-confirmed local archive retention

Fourth-tier local archive copies (Settings → Storage & retention → Local archive, requiring keeppeek.local-archive-retention.v1) protect the last verified copy during retention deletion. Setting Archive retention days (retention_days; blank = indefinite) makes verified replicas eligible for preview only—expiry alone never deletes any archive copy.

Archive deletion runbook

  1. Open Settings → Storage & retention → Local archive.
  2. Choose Preview archive deletion on one ready job, or Preview expired archive copies to preview one page of up to 16 retention-eligible jobs.
  3. Review the target ID, object IDs, bytes, verification timestamp, and per-item eligibility (Eligible for deletion, Within retention period, Restore the primary copy first, Verified primary unavailable, Preservation hold or maintenance, An active reader is using this object, Awaiting fresh verification).
  4. Choose Confirm archive deletion to queue removal for only the eligible items on that page (each additional page requires its own preview and confirmation). Use Cancel queued archive deletion before execution starts if needed.

Last-verified-copy protection rules

  • Two-copy verification & durable staging: Before claiming a job, the worker verifies both complete copies within one operation_timeout_secs, then records retirement intent, quarantines the archive, records staging, and re-verifies both the staged archive and protected primary in the same turn before unlinking. Restart resumes from the last durable step.
  • Evicted primaries & expired exports: Evicted recordings require a completed Restore primary before archive deletion is allowed. Expired exports whose primary was already retired retain their archive copy. Holds, active readers, changed media, or unavailable primary storage block removal (archive_deletion_blocked).
  • Staged restore fence: Archive deletion claims are refused while a pending configuration restore would replace the database or change/disable the active [storage.archive] target ID or root.
  • Permanent catalog exclusion: Once acknowledged, KeepPeek records the deletion in the catalog and permanently excludes that recording or export from automatic archiving and the manual copy picker while that catalog is in use.
Server platformSurviving-primary protection requirement before archive removal
Windows (NTFS)KeepPeek holds a writer-excluding read handle on the primary throughout archive removal.
LinuxPrimary must have both fs-verity and the immutable inode flag (chattr +i); ordinary mutable Linux primaries retain both copies (archive_primary_write_protection_required). After the job reaches acknowledged, a host admin may verify catalog identity/digest and clear the immutable flag with chattr -i -- <path> if moves or retention must resume (never while queued, claimed, or pending acknowledgement).
macOS & other platformsBoth copies are retained; archive removal does not proceed.

Catalog reconciliation (/recordings/maintenance → Reconciliation)

Choose Inspect catalog to run a bounded read-only inventory comparing the catalog against archive roots:

  • Scan bounds: Reads at most 4,096 catalog rows and 4,096 archive entries, returning at most 128 findings. Incomplete reports explicitly disable all remedies. Reports belong to the requesting Administrator, expire after 10 minutes, and at most 16 live in server memory.
  • Findings reported: Missing/unknown files, duplicate paths or catalog file identities, size/identity mismatch, rejected paths, temporary files, interrupted work, corrupt containers, and playback-index mismatch. Protected recordings are not mislabeled as interrupted; active recordings are never parsed.
  • Bounded fMP4 validation: Finalized cataloged files verify box boundaries, decode times, track IDs, sample extents, and decoder metadata up to 4,096 fragments, 8 MiB metadata, 2 tracks, and 65,536 samples/track fragment. This is a structural check, not a full media decode or content checksum.
  • Reports & remedies: Filter by category and save evidence via Download reconciliation report. Mutating remedies share storage coordination with deletion/restore and require a fresh scan afterward. Quarantine, content-checksum comparison, and previewed temp-file deletion are not implemented.
RemedyEligible findingEffect & preconditions
AcknowledgeAny findingMarks ignored in the in-memory report only; touches neither catalog nor disk.
Retain tombstoneMissing, unprotected cataloged fileRechecks absence and catalog revision, removes stale row/playback indexes, preserves deletion-coverage tombstone; deletes no disk bytes. Rejected if file reappears or catalog changed.
Rebuild indexExisting unprotected cataloged file with valid fMP4 and index mismatchRechecks revision, index fingerprint, and file identity, then rebuilds fragment/keyframe indexes and coverage in one transaction; media bytes untouched. Unavailable for unknown files.

Preflight statuses and filesystem trust boundaries

Internal preflight checks run an actor-owned queued job by taking one catalog read transaction first, closing it, and performing at most 128 filesystem checks under a shared 2-second cooperative deadline (paths bounded to 4,096 bytes and 16 components; timeouts return no partial report, and malformed persisted fingerprints fail with redacted errors).

Preflight statusMeaning
PresentPreparation fingerprint, current catalog ID, observed file identity, and byte size all match (contents not hashed).
IdentityChangedPreparation fingerprint, current catalog ID, and observed file disagree.
IdentityUnavailableMissing preparation or catalog identity evidence (path/size alone never match).
MissingFile / MissingCatalogFile absent at resolved path, or catalog row absent (disk state unknown).
CatalogChangedSource, stream, time bounds, or recorded byte count changed since preview.
ActiveRecording / ProtectedRecording / CleanupPendingCatalog state blocks manual maintenance (reported before filesystem inspection).
SizeMismatchObserved file length differs from catalog byte count.
PathRejected / InspectionFailedEscapes, hidden directories (such as .exports), alternate streams, links, nonregular files, or OS I/O error.

What filesystem checks prove—and remaining Alpha boundaries

  • Path & handle safety: Inspection pins the archive root and traverses descendant directories without following links, rejecting hidden directories, traversal, alternate data streams, symlinks, nonregular files, and multi-hardlink files both before and on the opened non-writable handle (nonblocking open on Unix; attribute open on Windows; never reads media bytes or requests write/truncate access).
  • Staging & OS guarantees: Deletion moves files into owner-only private staging, revalidates identity, and syncs directories before catalog completion. Unix enforces process-owned directories, atomic no-replace rename, and post-unlink inode link-count verification. Native Windows requires NTFS with persistent ACLs, checked directory flushes, and handle-based rename/disposition—ReFS (which needs 128-bit identity and a separate durability contract), FAT, and network shares are rejected before mutation.
  • Explicit limits: A Present preflight is never standalone permission to delete (filesystem checks are not atomic with catalog reads or unlinks, and file IDs can be reused after handle drop). Checks do not isolate maintenance from another process running under the same OS user or root; do not treat retries after external staging tampering as qualified recovery.
  • Qualification status: Implementation issue #133 passed native Windows/macOS/Linux synthetic checks, while deployment-specific qualification remains open under Alpha gate #145 (adversarial replacement at mutation boundaries, full NTFS/Linux runtime and unsupported-FS coverage, cross-feature coordination/audit, crash recovery across all file/catalog/tombstone states, remaining reconciliation remedies, browser retry/revocation flows, and final-commit performance evidence).

See Diagnose incomplete recovery when troubleshooting missing bytes vs. catalog drift, Backup and restore before changing storage, and Release readiness and known limitations.

Recording archive recovery

This chapter is the host-operator and Administrator runbook for backing up, rehearsing, restoring, and diagnosing KeepPeek’s complete recording archive (catalog, state databases, media roots, and configuration). KeepPeek provides no recording-archive importer, no database merge tool, and no automatic adoption of unknown MP4 files—a configuration ZIP restores settings and file-backed secrets only, and arbitrary live filesystem copies are not guaranteed recoverable.

Inventory the recovery set

Sign in as an Administrator, open Settings → Storage & retention → Named storage volumes (and Local archive if enabled), and record every configured root, its stable ID, and the OS account that runs KeepPeek. Do not rely on default paths if Settings defines overrides, and never treat exported evidence clips as a recording archive backup.

AssetDefault / configured locationCovered by config ZIP?Recovery requirement
config.toml + sibling secrets.tomlConfig directory (--config)Yes (plaintext secrets)Cameras, policies, grants, dashboards, templates, [runtime_profiles], [storage.archive], settings-backed state_store.
Recording catalog + SQLite sidecars (-wal, -shm)Metadata volume (catalog-<uuid>.db) or legacy recordings.dbNoRecording identities, owned paths, playback indexes, events, coverage/deletion ledger, maintenance intent. Copy as one stopped family.
Runtime state store + sidecarsLong-term storage (state-store.db)No (only state_store config entries)Desired-state docs, leases, namespace/entry revisions, byte counters. Copy and restore as one unit.
Guest review grant store + sidecarsMetadata volume (guest-review/grants.db)NoGuest link verifiers, revocations, view/download counters. Follow Recover guest links when restoring historical images.
Cloud mobile push state + sidecarsLong-term storage (cloud-notifications.db)NoEvent aliases, pending/claimed intents, rolling rate windows, monotonic clock floor. Copy as one stopped family.
Active & archive media volume rootsstorage/media or configured named volumesNoCopy the entire root (including hidden/staging and .active files), not just *.mp4.
Fourth-tier local archive root (optional)[storage.archive] rootNo ([storage.archive] config only)Verified replica files and private staging; preserve paired with catalog manifests and target bindings.
Thumbnail volume rootsstorage/images or configured rootsNoEvent-image bytes referenced by catalog.
Export volume roots + export historystorage/exports + metadata volume (exports-<uuid>.json)NoExport artifacts (independent expiry) and durable export history.
Host & operational dependenciesService unit/SCM, KEEPPEEK_SECRET_*, TLS certs, sibling log-filter, logsNoPreserve separately in your protected operations store.

Copying maintenance private staging preserves forensic evidence, not permission to retry destructive work on another disk: filesystem device/inode identities can change across copies.

Create a consistent offline copy

  1. Finish pending configuration applies or storage moves, export a private configuration ZIP, and finish or cancel active exports/maintenance jobs (retaining reports for unresolved work; if preserving a failure state instead, keep unfinished journals and mark the copy as forensic, not a clean recovery point).
  2. Stop KeepPeek through its service manager and confirm process exit so no writer touches the files. Recording pauses during this window.
  3. Copy the config directory and every inventoried volume root while stopped, including all SQLite sidecar files (-wal, -shm) for the catalog, state-store.db, guest-review/grants.db, and cloud-notifications.db. Never delete sidecars to simplify copying.
  4. Preserve ownership and owner-only permissions, record the exact KeepPeek build and source paths, verify copy integrity off the source disk, then restart the source recorder and confirm new recordings finalize normally.

A live copy of a SQLite file alone can disagree with its WAL or MP4 files, and KeepPeek’s configuration export lock does not snapshot databases or media. If you rely on filesystem/storage snapshots instead of stopping the service, design and test a multi-volume consistent snapshot procedure across every writer.

Recover guest links

Guest link verifiers, revocations, and consumed view/download counters live in guest-review/grants.db on the metadata volume ([sharing] in config.toml holds sharing policy). Restart terminates active guest sessions and transfers.

Restoring a historical metadata volume image can resurrect revoked grants or reset consumed counters. Always neutralize old guest grants before exposing a restored recorder:

  1. Keep the original backup unchanged offline.
  2. Before starting the restored working copy, set enabled = false in [sharing] in config.toml and keep the public guest origin offline.
  3. Delete the entire guest-review/ directory (including grants.db and all SQLite sidecars) from the working copy of the metadata volume. Startup initializes a fresh, empty guest store.
  4. Start KeepPeek in isolation, verify recovered recordings and exports, then re-enable [sharing] and confirm the Administrator’s all-creators guest-link list is empty before restoring public access.
  5. Issue fresh guest links only for recordings and completed exports an Administrator still authorizes.

Restore the runtime state store before accepting watches

Restore state-store.db and its SQLite sidecars together so namespace revisions, entry revisions, and byte counters return as one consistent unit before KeepPeek accepts watches. Restoring entries without their counters lets new writes reuse previously consumed revisions, breaking compare-and-set ordering. After restore, clients open fresh watches and reconcile snapshots against current ServerCapabilities (no resume token crosses a restore). Restore cloud-notifications.db and its sidecars as a matching stopped family when mobile push signaling is enabled.

Rehearse recovery on an isolated host

  1. Leave the original backup read-only; restore a working copy onto an isolated host with matching KeepPeek version, compatible local filesystem (NTFS on Windows), sufficient capacity, and service-account permissions.
  2. Block outbound access to real cameras, notification providers, and MQTT brokers until intentionally tested—imported credentials and rules are live and a duplicate recorder can otherwise trigger duplicate alerts or device contention.
  3. Restore the catalog family, state databases, and all media/archive/thumbnail/export roots together at their original configured paths (do not start with missing archive mounts, and do not edit catalog SQL—use Move storage deliberately for intentional relocation). Restore config.toml, secrets.toml, and KEEPPEEK_SECRET_* env vars separately.
  4. Start KeepPeek and inspect startup logs immediately; stop and preserve logs on any catalog, path, or migration error.
  5. Verify cameras and access grants, open a known older interval in Keep, check coverage and event thumbnails, play a finalized recording, and create a small evidence export whose downloaded MP4 decodes independently (for example with FFmpeg).
  6. Run Recording integrity (/recordings) and Recording maintenance → Reconciliation (Inspect catalog) to verify missing, unknown, interrupted, and protected items before touching old maintenance jobs.

Repeat these checks on production cutover and retain the old copy until acceptance passes.

Understand startup recovery

Startup reconciles narrow interrupted states automatically; it is not a full media scan or substitute for a consistent backup.

AreaAutomatic startup behaviorOperator boundary
Interrupted recording finalizationReconnects eligible catalog rows to finalized siblings; preserves all existing .active files untouched.Inspect preserved .active files rather than deleting by extension.
Missing bound named/archive rootRetains catalog ownership and marks the bound volume/target offline; never recreates a missing directory.Restore the original root identity; an empty replacement directory is rejected.
Interrupted or missing exportsMarks running exports and missing ready artifacts as failed (retryable); cleans partial artifacts only inside owned export storage.Retry requires source recordings to still exist.
Missing event thumbnailsPreserves catalog references and unindexed/temp images; quota cleanup removes only catalog-referenced images in the configured root.Restore thumbnail volume root to recover missing JPEGs.
Interrupted maintenance jobsRuns a 2-second recovery pass that settles completion only when staging-directory identity and absence checks prove unlink finished; leaves other claims Failed and reserved.Copied files may change filesystem IDs, causing restored checkpoints to refuse retry properly. See Recording maintenance.

Diagnose incomplete recovery

SymptomNext action
Settings returned, recordings did notRestore the catalog and media roots together; a configuration ZIP excludes both.
Settings returned, generic runtime state did notRestore state-store.db with its sidecars/counters, then have clients open fresh watches.
Recordings appear missing after a path changeVerify mount availability, service-account permissions, effective paths, and original catalog paths before modifying files.
MP4 files exist on disk but are absent in KeepRun Inspect catalog in Reconciliation. Unknown files are never auto-adopted, and Rebuild index applies only to existing cataloged recordings.
Cataloged recording has a valid MP4 but broken playback indexUse the previewed Rebuild index remedy if offered (rebuilds indexes without altering media bytes).
Reconciliation reports container corruptionPreserve the original file and downloaded report; structural fMP4 checks are not a full decode or content checksum.
Copied maintenance job refuses retryPreserve the job report and checkpoint evidence; never delete private staging or clear catalog reservations manually.
Old ready export is unavailableCheck export expiry and artifact presence; export metadata can outlive bytes, and retry needs surviving source recordings.
Both configuration activation and rollback failPreserve the complete configuration directory (.backups/restore-journal.json, .staged, .rollback) and service logs before manual recovery.

Hard prohibitions: Never delete unknown media, SQLite sidecars, restore journals, or private maintenance staging to clear an error. Retain tombstone records catalog cleanup of missing media—it does not recover bytes. Quarantine, arbitrary archive import, and automatic adoption of unknown files are unsupported.

Record the recovery result

Archive a recovery report capturing: exact KeepPeek build, OS/filesystem, source and destination layouts, backup timestamp, downtime window, commands run, verification checksums, tested pre- and post-backup recording intervals, interrupted-work tests, and any discrepancies. Representative recovery and sustained-operation evidence remains part of the Alpha qualification matrix.

Native camera events

This chapter covers ingesting detections and alarm transitions reported directly by cameras over Reolink Baichuan, Hikvision/Annke/HiWatch ISAPI, generic ONVIF PullPoint, and RTSP ONVIF metadata tracks without an external inference service. Native event processing runs independently of media ingest—an event failure never restarts healthy video or audio—and KeepPeek never modifies camera detection rules, regions, or schedules automatically.

Supported detections enter the same catalog as external events and flow to Events (/events), Keep (/keep), notification rules, and MQTT. Compare native event status separately from Camera and stream health.

Supported adapters and capabilities

AdapterTransport & authRecognized event kindsImages & channel scope
Reolink (Baichuan / reo-proto)Native Baichuan alarm push (StartMotionAlarm / AlarmEventList) over the camera session.person, vehicle, animal, doorbell_press (point event from visitor), plus motion when record_generic_motion_events = true.Scoped to the camera’s configured Baichuan channel. No automatic failover from a failing Baichuan media session.
Hikvision / Annke / HiWatch (ISAPI)Persistent GET /ISAPI/Event/notification/alertStream (multipart XML/JSON, HTTP Digest; no Basic downgrade) or opt-in [isapi_callbacks] HTTP push.person, vehicle, line_crossing, intrusion, region_entry, region_exit, tamper, alarm, ANPR, named target collections, plus opt-in motion (VMD).Up to 16 explicitly correlated JPEGs per event. Channel derived from matching 101/102 (ch 1) or 201/202 (ch 2) URLs; conflicting URLs fail.
Generic ONVIF PullPointONVIF Event Service subscription (WS-Security UsernameToken + HTTP Digest challenge; no Basic/token-free downgrade; 90s lease, 1–30s backoff).18 namespace-aware kinds: motion, tamper, digital_input, audio_detected, video_loss, line_crossing, intrusion, region_entry, region_exit, loitering, person, vehicle, animal, face, license_plate, package, object_count, doorbell_press.0–1 optional JPEG snapshot (<= 1 MiB, 2s budget). One subscription per device IP; narrow multi-channel devices with opaque source_tokens.
RTSP metadata (rtsp-metadata)ONVIF metadata tracks on existing RTSP workers (supports vnd.onvif.metadata+gzip; EXI unsupported). Does not contact an ONVIF Event Service.Same namespace-aware ONVIF event and Profile T/M object mapping as PullPoint.Uses one active main/sub metadata consumer with a 5-second inactivity handoff.

Unknown topics, arbitrary vendor namespaces, PTZ status, and heartbeats (videoloss/inactive, heartBeat/active) never become invented motion events. Hardware verification across non-fixture ONVIF models and smart-rule firmware dialects remains model- and firmware-dependent; fixture tests are not firmware certification.

Enable and verify events

  1. Add the camera and verify live video and recording independently in Camera and stream health.
  2. In the camera’s own web or app interface, enable the detection rule, schedule, detection region, and notification linkage. KeepPeek discovery never writes camera detector settings.
  3. Keep the default [cameras.<name>.events] mode = "auto" unless forcing a specific transport.
  4. If the camera emits only unclassified motion/VMD, enable Store generic motion events (record_generic_motion_events = true, default false) in its KeepPeek camera settings. Explicit person or vehicle classifications remain distinct.
  5. Trigger one known event, open Events (/events), select the camera and UTC date, and inspect kind, source, timestamp, and image. Verify matching footage in Keep (/keep) before enabling alert rules.

A detector-enabled switch on the camera is not an activity indicator, and a camera motion rule with a human-only filter does not guarantee notifications carry an explicit person label. KeepPeek classifies strictly from explicit payload evidence.

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough demonstrates verifying camera-origin events in Events (/events) and Activity (/activity).

Transport modes and file-only configuration

Configure per-camera event overrides under [cameras.<name>.events] in config.toml. These fields are file-only—ordinary UI camera edits preserve them—and require the normal configuration apply/restart workflow. Replace <name> with the existing camera key; never duplicate a camera entry just to change its event path.

[cameras.front.events]
mode = "onvif-pullpoint"
metadata_stream = "auto"
snapshots = true
modeBehavior
auto (default)Prefer an eligible Reolink or ISAPI adapter; otherwise use generic ONVIF PullPoint and permit RTSP metadata. Unsupported ISAPI HTTP 404/405 hands over to generic ONVIF after vendor cleanup.
vendorUse only the eligible vendor adapter without generic fallback.
onvif-pullpointForce generic ONVIF PullPoint regardless of video backend; RTSP metadata remains permitted unless disabled.
rtsp-metadataConsume metadata from existing RTSP workers without contacting an ONVIF Event Service.
disabledStop native event workers and exclude native event capabilities while leaving camera media running.
  • metadata_stream: auto (default), enabled, or disabled. Does not override vendor or disabled.
  • event_service_url: optional HTTP(S) URL restricted to the exact configured camera IP with no credentials, query, or fragment.
  • source_tokens: up to 32 exact camera-supplied opaque identifiers (<= 256 bytes each); never guess profile indexes.
  • include_topics / exclude_topics: up to 32 combined namespace-expanded XML topic paths (<= 1,024 bytes each), such as {http://www.onvif.org/ver10/topics}VideoSource/MotionAlarm; exclusions take precedence.
  • snapshots: true by default; controls optional ONVIF snapshot fetches.
  • [isapi_callbacks]: optional camera-to-server HTTP Digest callback listener in config.toml for eligible non-Reolink cameras. Configuring callbacks suppresses that camera’s pull worker to prevent duplicate events; store callback passwords in secrets.toml via {secret:KEY}.

Authentication failures are reported as terminal errors rather than downgraded or retried across policies. See Configuration reference for complete field limits and secret-reference rules.

Event lifecycle, timestamps, and images

BehaviorRule
CoalescingRepeated active observations update one logical event; an explicit clear closes it. Matching ONVIF PullPoint and RTSP metadata observations deduplicate into one event.
ONVIF baselinesInitialized notifications establish a baseline without alerting; an already-active baseline must clear before opening a new interval.
Timestamps (ONVIF)Valid camera UTC from the preceding 5 minutes supplies event time; future, stale, missing, or invalid times fall back to receipt time with timestamp_source and timestamp_reason metadata.
Timestamps (ISAPI)Recording timeline uses server receipt time; the camera’s original dateTime is retained as camera_time metadata. Sync device clocks when investigating drift.
Inferred endingsISAPI disconnects or a 30-second active-observation timeout (ONVIF: 30s properties, 5s objects) close the span at its last evidence—not proof of physical motion stop.
Snapshots & boxesMissing or failed snapshots never discard event metadata. Bounding boxes draw only on their explicitly matched image; unrelated footage is never substituted.
Observability/metrics exports fixed-cardinality keeppeek_camera_events_* counters and gauges; topics, URLs, tokens, and raw XML are never metric labels.

Troubleshooting

SymptomWhat to check
Video works, events do notNative event status, device event service, credentials, rule schedule, and channel selection (101/102 vs 201/202 or source_tokens).
Only heartbeats arriveEnable the camera’s rule and notification linkage; enable Store generic motion events for unclassified VMD.
Generic service is unsupportedConfirm the device exposes ONVIF Event Service or RTSP metadata; an ONVIF video profile alone is insufficient.
Repeated authentication failureFix saved credentials and device permissions; KeepPeek does not downgrade Digest/WS-Security or repeatedly guess passwords.
Events have no thumbnailCheck snapshot support and attachment state; metadata delivery succeeds independently of image capture.
Duplicate-looking cardsCompare source, event ID, rule, object, and revision; similar text can represent distinct observations.

For wire-level protocol limits and hardware diagnostics, see ONVIF events and Hikvision ISAPI. For model-based detections, see External analysis; for alerting and MQTT, see Notifications and integrations.

External analysis

This chapter explains how independent computer-vision and analytics services subscribe to KeepPeek WebRTC streams and publish durable event revisions back through the MIT-licensed public API (keeppeek.event-publication.v1). KeepPeek does not install, host, or select inference products—model execution, decoding, sampling, deployment, and model licensing belong to the external service—while camera-native detections are covered in Native camera events.

System boundary

ResponsibilityKeepPeek ownsExternal service owns
MediaCamera ingest, recording, WebRTC stream routing (RTP preferred, data-channel fallback).Frame decoding (ffmpeg or native decoder), sampling rate, keyframe gating, bounded queues.
AnalysisSource authorization, advertised event-type catalog, attachment limits.Model weights, licensing, inference runtime, tracking, event lifecycle formation, JPEG encode.
EventsAtomic catalog + attachment commit, revision conflict checks, live/notification/MQTT fanout.Globally unique event_id generation, strictly increasing revisions, idempotent commit retry.
ConfigNamed Administrator credentials and advertised ServerCapabilities.Service-specific config files, env vars, and deployment lifecycle (no KeepPeek browser wizard).

External services must publish strictly through the public API—never write KeepPeek’s SQLite catalog or attachment directories directly, and do not invent undocumented KeepPeek config.toml sections.

Prepare an external integration

  1. Confirm the camera is online in KeepPeek and that its stream codec (H.264 or H.265) can be decoded by the service. Select the low-resolution sub stream explicitly for continuous inference rather than continuously decoding main.
  2. Copy the camera’s stable source_id from camera details (/camera). Display names, transient source_session_id values, and Home Assistant entity IDs are not substitutes.
  3. Create a dedicated named Administrator credential in Settings > Access (Authentication and access control). Event publication currently requires Administrator authority—a User key can read media but cannot publish events, and per-source/per-event-type scopes are not implemented. Use a separate credential per service so it can be revoked independently.
  4. Confirm ServerCapabilities advertises keeppeek.event-publication.v1, the target source (source_id and active source_session_id), a supported media variant, and every event type the service will emit. Unadvertised event types are rejected.
  5. Start the service and verify one committed event in Events (/events) before attaching alert rules or automations.

Publication contract and limits

AspectContract behavior
Metadata-only eventsUse envelope-only PublishEvent when no attachments are included.
Attachment eventsUse the two-phase transaction (StartEventPublication → EventAttachmentChunk over RELIABLE_DATA → CommitEventPublication). Files become visible only after COMMITTED.
Failure & reapingAbort unfinished work with AbortEventPublication; on process crash, KeepPeek expires and deletes staged bytes at expires_at_ms with no partial event visible.
IdempotencyReuse the same publication_id, event_id, and revision on retry. Identical metadata and attachment bytes return COMMITTED without duplicate fanout; differing content returns a revision conflict.
Session quotasAtomic publications are bounded to 4 active and 64 distinct IDs per API session (64 active / 256 retained server-wide). Reconnect after exhausting per-session IDs.
Source restartsRestarts change source_session_id. Rediscover the active session and re-subscribe while preserving the stable source_id for event history.
ObservabilityPrometheus /metrics exports fixed-cardinality keeppeek_external_analysis_* gauges, counters, and bounded 256-sample p50/p95 durable publication latency (ms).

Reference Python example

examples/object_detection_service/ is a minimal Python reference service built for interoperability demonstration and CI. It is not a production detector product.

  • Prerequisites: Python 3.12+ (installed directly into the interpreter—do not use virtual environments), ffmpeg on PATH, an active H.264 or H.265 source, and a KeepPeek server advertising keeppeek.event-publication.v1.
  • Bindings: Run generate_protos.py to generate MIT-licensed bindings directly from api/webrtc.proto. Never copy wire types by hand.
  • Authentication: Supply the key via KEEPPEEK_ACCESS_KEY_FILE (owner-only file) or KEEPPEEK_ACCESS_KEY (takes precedence). Never pass keys in command arguments, URLs, or committed files.
  • Settings: Configure non-secret inputs via KEEPPEEK_URL, KEEPPEEK_SOURCE_ID, KEEPPEEK_STREAM, KEEPPEEK_MODEL, KEEPPEEK_CONFIDENCE, KEEPPEEK_COOLDOWN_SECONDS (5s default per class), and KEEPPEEK_INFERENCE_FPS.
  • Bounded queues: Caps memory by dropping oldest work first—encoded queue: 4 frames / 8 MiB; decoded queue: 1 frame / 24 MiB; publication queue: 16 events / 16 KiB; single frame: <= 8 MiB and 256 fragments.
  • Model weights: Loads Ultralytics yolo11n.pt only after auth and media subscription succeed (Ultralytics may download missing weights under its own AGPL-3.0 / enterprise license; KeepPeek does not distribute weights). Use --detector fake for weight-free CI/local checks.

Verify results in KeepPeek

  1. Open Events (/events) and select the source and UTC date.
  2. Inspect event kind, confidence, text, revision, and canonical image. Higher revisions enrich or close the same logical event rather than creating a new detection.
  3. Select Open at this moment to compare the event against recorded footage in Keep (/keep), and Back to event to return.

Review state and shared bookmarks behave identically for external and native events. An attached JPEG, story, or description is event evidence only—it does not prove continuous video was retained (Recording and evidence).

Troubleshooting

SymptomWhat to check
Subscription rejectedSource visibility, active source_session_id, advertised main/sub H.264/H.265 variant, and client media capabilities.
Publication deniedAdministrator credential role, enabled/unexpired key, current source session, and advertised event type.
Processing falls behindService decode/inference rate and bounded queues; lower KEEPPEEK_INFERENCE_FPS or subscribe to sub instead of queuing stale frames.
Events appear without imagesTwo-phase upload/commit completion, descriptor count/byte limits, and explicit attachment availability state.
Recording continues while detections stopInspect the external process and WebRTC session; inference failure never interrupts camera ingest or recording.
Reconnect produces duplicatesPreserve logical event_id, increment revision strictly, and retry with the original publication_id on ambiguous commits.

Start client development with the MIT-licensed public API and the computer-vision scenario (verify live ServerCapabilities before assuming a scenario workload is enabled on a given build).

Notifications and integrations

This chapter covers server-owned notification rules (browser alerts, Pushover, webhooks, and optional bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling) and the MQTT 5 event forwarder, configured by Administrators in Settings (/settings#notifications and /settings#integrations). Camera and storage workers publish transitions only after committing state and never perform provider or broker I/O, so a slow or unreachable integration cannot disrupt camera ingest, recording, event storage, health projection, or other channels.

Channels and runtimes at a glance

Channel / runtimeUI locationCapability gateStatusKey boundary
Browser alerts & inboxSettings > Notificationskeeppeek.rules.v1AvailableProcess-local per-principal inbox; desktop popups need browser permission and an open frontend.
Push (Pushover)Settings > Notifications (Push)keeppeek.rules.v1AvailableHTTPS Pushover Messages API; write-only secrets in secrets.toml; JPEG <= 4 MiB; priority -2..2.
Keeppeek ServiceSettings > Notifications (Keeppeek Service + Bounded mobile push signaling)keeppeek.cloud-notifications.v1Optional (off by default)Exact 109-byte signed signal only; zero metadata/media sent to cloud; durable cloud-notifications.db.
WebhookSettings > Notifications (Webhook)keeppeek.rules.v1Available5s timeout, no redirects, no custom headers, no URL credentials; second delivery on enrichment opt-in.
Forwarder (rule action)Settings > Notifications (Forwarder)keeppeek.rules.v1Unavailable (channel_unavailable)Exposed in the rule editor but fails visibly as channel_unavailable. Not the MQTT forwarder.
MQTT 5 event forwarderSettings > Integrationskeeppeek.mqtt-forwarder.v1AvailableSeparate MQTT 5 runtime (no 3.1/3.1.1 fallback); JSON snapshots (schema_version = 1), metadata only.

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough demonstrates configuring notification rules, Pushover, bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling, and MQTT 5 forwarder health in Settings.

Build and activate notification rules

Notification rule controls appear in Settings > Notifications only when the server advertises keeppeek.rules.v1.

  1. Select Add rule and choose one or more triggers: Event starts, Event enriched, Event ends, Camera outage (camera_offline, stream_stale, decode_unavailable, recording_interrupted), Camera recovery, Storage health, or Recording health.
  2. Narrow matching with filters: camera/source IDs, groups, event kinds, zones, minimum confidence, minimum duration, image predicate (Any, Image required, No image), severity, review state, and bookmark state.
  3. Configure an IANA timezone, active windows, DST-aware Quiet hours, cooldowns, fixed-window rate limits, and optional Bounded critical bypass.
  4. Add up to 8 ordered actions (Browser, Push, Keeppeek Service, Webhook; avoid Forwarder, which returns channel_unavailable).
  5. Use Save draft to store changes without activating, or Save & activate to validate and atomically promote the draft. Expected-revision checks reject stale edits without altering the previous active revision.
  6. Run the rule’s test action, then verify one real matching event in Events (/events). A test send uses separate rate accounting and validates credentials/transport, not your camera filters or schedule.

Active rules and drafts persist under [notifications] in config.toml across restarts. Pending deliveries, retries, deduplication keys, cooldowns, rate windows, inbox receipts, and history are process-local in memory and reset on restart.

Deduplication, inbox, webhooks, and retries

  • Stable collapse identity: Each logical notification derives a SHA-256 identity from rule ID, source ID, event or outage ID, and lifecycle. Revisions, retries, enrichment, and recovery share one collapse key while running; identical text never merges unrelated cameras or events.
  • Cooldowns and rate limits: Cooldowns support Event family, Camera + event kind, Group, Whole rule, and Outage interval, starting at logical notification creation time. Rate limits apply per rule, channel, principal, or server. Both reset on restart.
  • Preliminary vs enriched delivery: A canonical image or end revision arriving before the enrichment deadline can replace a preliminary alert. Missing imagery never blocks metadata alerts unless the action sets Attachment to Required. Late enrichment is recorded without waking the user unless permitted.
  • Inbox and History: Settings > Notifications provides Rules, Inbox, and History tabs (refreshed every 5 seconds). Inbox actions (mark seen, acknowledge, clear, Clear all → Confirm clear all) apply per authenticated principal and stay separate from event review state, camera health, and Pushover emergency acknowledgements.
  • Browser alerts: Add a Browser action and select Enable browser alerts to grant OS/browser permission. Browser notifications require an active frontend and are not an offline push service for a closed browser.
  • Webhooks: Enforce a 5-second timeout, zero redirects, no arbitrary headers, and no credentials in the URL. Enable Allow second delivery without replacement only when the receiving endpoint expects an enriched follow-up POST.
  • Outbox and retry bounds: The in-memory notification outbox holds at most 10,000 pending/retrying actions; overflow records expired (outbox_full). Timeouts, connection failures, and HTTP 408, 425, 429, and 5xx responses use bounded exponential backoff (clamping Retry-After to rule bounds). Invalid credentials and other 4xx responses fail permanently without infinite retries. Disabling a rule or action expires its pending work immediately; restarting KeepPeek discards queued work.

Pushover delivery

Pushover is the supported third-party push channel:

  1. Register an application at https://pushover.net/apps/build and copy the 30-character application token plus your user or delivery-group key.
  2. In Settings > Notifications, add a Push action to a rule and enter both credentials.
  3. Configure optional fields: comma-separated Devices, Sound, Priority (-2 through 2), public Deep-link base URL, and JPEG Attachment. Emergency priority 2 requires retry (>= 30s) and expiry (1..10,800s) and polls receipts at >= 5s intervals.
  4. Select Save & activate, run the test action, and inspect History for the provider request ID.

Pushover credentials are write-only: KeepPeek stores values in secrets.toml and references in config.toml, never returning them to the browser, health snapshots, logs, or delivery reasons. Editing non-secret fields preserves stored credentials; replacing credentials requires entering both values again. Titles cap at 250 characters, bodies at 1,024, deep links at 512, and JPEG attachments at 4 MiB (below Pushover’s 5 MiB limit). Templates support {{source.name}} and {{source.id}}. Requests use verified HTTPS, a 5-second timeout, zero redirects, and at least 5 seconds between retries.

Bounded Keeppeek Service mobile push signaling

When the server advertises keeppeek.cloud-notifications.v1, Administrators can enable an optional keeppeek_service rule action for compact mobile push signaling. Both signaling and direct still-image previews default to off (enabled = false, image_previews_enabled = false) under [cloud_notifications] in config.toml. Pairing requires a canonical HTTPS service_origin, 16-byte nvr_id, signing_key_version >= 1, and an Ed25519 signing seed referenced solely as signing_key = "{secret:KEY}" in secrets.toml.

Manage the runtime in Settings > Notifications under Bounded mobile push signaling:

  • Toggle Enable Keeppeek Service signaling and optionally Allow private direct still-image previews (requires signaling enabled and active pairing), then select Save cloud settings.
  • Use Send test signal (3/hr limit) and Refresh cloud status to verify admission and inspect state (DISABLED, UNPAIRED, READY, THROTTLED, BACKING OFF, UNAVAILABLE) and rolling 1h / 24h acceptance and suppression counters.
  • Add a Keeppeek Service action to matching rules (Attachment cannot be set to Required on this channel).

Privacy boundary and non-configurable limits

Outbound signals hit POST /api/v1/nvr/events (application/octet-stream) over verified HTTPS (5s timeout, 0 redirects, 4 KiB discarded response cap) with an exact 109-byte binary payload: protocol version (1), 16-byte opaque nvr_id, a random 128-bit cloud_event_id alias persisted once per logical event, issued-at Unix seconds, signing_key_version, and a 64-byte Ed25519 signature. Keeppeek Service never receives local event IDs, camera names or IDs, event kinds, detection payloads, free text, media URLs, attachment descriptors, or image bytes.

If Allow private direct still-image previews is enabled, an authenticated paired mobile credential bound to the current nvr_id and authorized for the event’s camera resolves the 16-byte cloud_event_id directly against the private NVR and fetches static still images (image/jpeg or image/png) via existing event media delivery. Video, animated images, and audio previews are unsupported.

Safety boundaryHard local limit (non-configurable)
Pending signal queue1 claimed/in-flight + 1 newest-only pending intent
Production event spacing30 seconds between distinct event intents
Attempt pacing (GCRA)2 attempts/second, burst 4
Production rolling capExact rolling 120/hour and 500/day
Test-signal capExact rolling 3/hour (separate accounting)
Retry envelopeMax 3 attempts or 5-minute TTL (whichever first), exact 109-byte payload reuse, bounded Retry-After + jitter
Durable local statecloud-notifications.db under long-term storage (preserves aliases, pending intent, budgets, and clock floor across restart)

Signal acceptance confirms Keeppeek Service admission only, not APNs/FCM provider delivery or mobile display. Disabling the channel cancels pending work while preserving spent budgets and accepted-ID deduplication.

MQTT 5 event forwarder

Configure, test, and monitor MQTT forwarding under Settings > Integrations (Edit broker → Save MQTT settings / Test connection / Refresh status) or under [event_forwarder.mqtt] in config.toml. This requires the Administrator role, keeppeek.mqtt-forwarder.v1, and an MQTT 5 broker—MQTT 3.1 and 3.1.1 are rejected with no fallback.

  • Security: Use mqtts:// with system trust or a PEM tls_ca_path outside trusted local dev networks. Credentials embedded in broker_url are rejected; broker passwords are write-only and stored in secrets.toml ({secret:MQTT_PASSWORD}, overridable via KEEPPEEK_SECRET_MQTT_PASSWORD). Test connection uses a temporary client ID and non-retained status so it never evicts the live client.
  • Default topics (dynamic segments are percent-encoded to prevent /, +, # injection):
    • Events: keeppeek/{instance_id}/sources/{source_id}/events/{event_type} (unretained by default)
    • Status: keeppeek/{instance_id}/forwarders/{forwarder_id}/status (retained by default; Last Will sets disconnected)
  • Payload & properties: Every message is a complete JSON snapshot with schema_version = 1 and MQTT 5 properties (application/json, UTF-8 indicator, Correlation Data = event or forwarder ID, User Property schema-version=1). Deduplicate consumers on (instance_id, event_id, revision). Operational outage/recovery records (camera_offline, stream_stale, decode_unavailable, recording_interrupted) use the same envelope. Attachment metadata is published, never image bytes.
  • Delivery & bounds: Default QoS 1 (0, 1, 2 supported; QoS 2 does not survive KeepPeek restart), clean_start = false with 24-hour session expiry, 1 in-flight publish, 256-command ingestion queue, 100,000-key process-local receipt deduplication set, 64 MiB default in-memory outbox (outbox_full fails visibly while recording/storage continue), and 250 ms → 30s reconnect backoff. Restarting KeepPeek discards queued publications and deduplication history (events are not replayed from recordings.db).
  • Observability: Settings and Prometheus (/metrics keeppeek_mqtt_forwarder_*) expose state, redacted errors, pending items/bytes, retries, duplicates, last received/delivered times, and oldest unacknowledged work.

For Home Assistant, the direct camera card displays live video only; configure MQTT topics and deduplication independently in Home Assistant automations.

Troubleshooting

SymptomWhat to check
No event appears in EventsVerify Native camera events or External analysis, camera scope, and UTC day.
Rule saved but never firesClick Save & activate and resolve any draft revision conflict.
suppressed, collapsed, or rate_limitedInspect active filters, Quiet hours, cooldown scope, and next eligible time in History.
Forwarder action failsReturns channel_unavailable by design; configure MQTT under Settings > Integrations instead.
Pushover fails permanentlyVerify both write-only credentials and destination with a test send; invalid credentials do not retry indefinitely.
Keeppeek Service shows THROTTLED or UNPAIREDCheck [cloud_notifications] pairing fields, {secret:KEY}, and the hard 30s / 120-per-hour / 500-per-day pacing counters.
MQTT pending bytes grow continuouslyVerify MQTT 5 broker reachability, TLS trust, credentials, and acknowledgements before the bounded outbox fills.
Inbox or queued work gone after restartNotification outbox, inbox, cooldowns, and MQTT outbox are process-local (only [cloud_notifications] state persists in SQLite).

See the detailed references for notification rules, Pushover, Keeppeek Service signaling, MQTT 5 forwarding, and Camera and stream health.

Home Assistant card

This chapter covers installing, configuring, securing, and testing custom:keeppeek-card, the direct browser-to-KeepPeek Lovelace live-video card (ui/home-assistant/). Home Assistant serves the card module and dashboard config while the viewer browser connects directly to KeepPeek over HTTPS and WebRTC—no Home Assistant backend integration, media proxy, or iframe is required.

flowchart LR
    H[Home Assistant] -->|Card module and configuration| B[Browser]
    B -->|Authenticated session requests| K[KeepPeek]
    K -->|Direct WebRTC video| B
ScopeDetails
SupportedLive video, visual editor (Load sources), camera selection, grid and single layouts, focus, shared connections, HA theme matching.
Not in this cardAudio, PTZ, event ribbons, timeline playback, Home Assistant entity discovery. Use KeepPeek for playback (Recording and evidence) and MQTT for automation events (Notifications and integrations).

Prepare KeepPeek

  1. Verify the target cameras play in KeepPeek.
  2. Create a dedicated User credential with the intended camera grants in Settings > Access (Authentication and access control).
  3. Ensure both the KeepPeek HTTP endpoint and its advertised WebRTC addresses are reachable from the dashboard browser. Home Assistant Cloud does not relay card media; prefer a private VPN over public exposure.
  4. Add every exact Home Assistant browser origin (scheme://host[:port], no path or trailing slash) to [direct_card] in config.toml, then restart KeepPeek:
[direct_card]
allowed_origins = [
  "https://home.example.net",
  "https://homeassistant.local:8123",
]

Use browser-trusted HTTPS in normal deployments—HTTP is permitted only for localhost, 127.0.0.1, or [::1] development. Keep credentials out of endpoint URLs. KeepPeek enables credentialless CORS (POST, OPTIONS, Authorization, Content-Type, Content-Encoding; no cookies, never wildcard *) on /create and /delete for configured origins only. See Configuration reference.

Install, upgrade, and roll back

Each release provides a self-contained ES module (keeppeek.js bundling Svelte, protobuf runtime, icons, and CSS with zero third-party runtime fetches) plus keeppeek-card.json (version, SHA-256 checksum, byte counts).

HACS installation

  1. Select a KeepPeek release that includes keeppeek.js and keeppeek-card.json.
  2. In HACS, add https://github.com/xnorpx/keeppeek as a custom Dashboard repository.
  3. Download the release. If HACS does not auto-register it, add /hacsfiles/keeppeek/keeppeek.js as a JavaScript module dashboard resource.
  4. Reload the dashboard and pick KeepPeek in the card picker.

hacs.json targets release assets and hides default-branch downloads; a local build is not installable through HACS, and custom-repository installation is distinct from HACS default-store inclusion.

Manual installation

  1. Download keeppeek.js and keeppeek-card.json from a release, or build locally from the repo root with bun run --cwd ui build:home-assistant (outputs to target/home-assistant-card/dist/).
  2. Verify keeppeek.js SHA-256 against keeppeek-card.json.
  3. Copy keeppeek.js into Home Assistant’s www/ folder (restart Home Assistant if creating www/ for the first time).
  4. Register /local/keeppeek.js?v=VERSION as a JavaScript module resource and reload.

Upgrade and rollback

Switch versions in HACS, or replace www/keeppeek.js and update the ?v= query suffix, then reload all open dashboard tabs to release old sessions. Keep the card and KeepPeek server on compatible pre-1.0 releases, and never register two versions of the resource simultaneously.

Configure the card

In YAML-mode dashboards, store the dedicated key in Home Assistant’s secrets.yaml (distinct from KeepPeek’s secrets.toml) and reference it with !secret:

type: custom:keeppeek-card
endpoint: https://keeppeek.example.net
token: !secret keeppeek_lovelace_token
title: Entrances
sources:
  - source_id: "192.168.1.20"
    title: Front door
    quality: auto
  - source_id: "192.168.1.21"
    title: Driveway
    quality: low
layout: grid
columns: 2
aspect_ratio: "16:9"
show_name: true

In the visual editor, enter the endpoint and key, then click Load sources to discover visible cameras without subscribing to video. For storage-mode dashboards, enter the key directly in the editor—the existing key is never read back (an empty field with a configured placeholder preserves it; enter a replacement or use the clear action to change it). Always use KeepPeek’s stable source_id, not display names or Home Assistant entity IDs.

OptionDefaultLimits & behavior
endpointRequiredHTTPS base URL (<= 2,048 chars, no userinfo/query/fragment); HTTP allowed only on loopback dev hosts.
tokenRequiredResolved KeepPeek access key (!secret is resolved by Home Assistant, not the card).
sourcesRequired1 to 16 unique source objects. Start with 1 camera—16 valid entries do not guarantee 16-stream browser decode.
sources[].source_idRequiredNonblank stable source ID (<= 160 chars).
sources[].titleCamera name or IDOptional nonblank label below video (<= 160 chars).
sources[].qualityautoauto, low, or high from server-advertised variants.
titleKeepPeekOptional card heading (<= 160 chars).
layoutgridgrid shows all selected sources; single adds a camera selector.
columns2Integer 1..4, capped at visible camera count; stacks to 1 column below 440 px.
aspect_ratio16:916:9, 4:3, or 1:1 (contained, never cropped).
show_nametrueDisplays camera names below video.
viewliveOnly live is supported.

The editor preserves source titles, quality choices, and unrelated Home Assistant layout metadata (such as grid_options).

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough demonstrates the Lovelace visual editor and live custom:keeppeek-card grid, single, and focus controls.

Security boundary

  • Browser trust boundary: The resolved key necessarily reaches the browser. YAML !secret organizes config but does not hide the key from dashboard editors, third-party frontend modules, or browser DevTools network headers. Use a dedicated User key, never an Administrator key, unless every viewer should hold admin rights.
  • Memory-only handling: The card keeps the key and its SHA-256 connection fingerprint in memory only—never in URLs, browser storage, rendered HTML, console logs, or diagnostics.
  • Authorization lives on KeepPeek: sources is a display filter, not access control. KeepPeek’s trusted-local policy still grants Administrator access to browsers inside access.local_networks even when they send a User key—narrow access.local_networks and test restrictive grants from a remote-classified browser. CORS is not authentication.
  • Revocation: Rotating, disabling, revoking, or expiring the key invalidates active sessions per server policy; auth failures stop automatic card retries until the key is updated.

Connection sharing, lifecycle, and troubleshooting

Cards in the same browser document share one WebRTC session per normalized (endpoint, SHA-256(token)) and share one media subscription for identical (source_id, quality) pairs. Different credentials never share media.

Limit / lifecycle behaviorBound / rule
Sharing boundsUp to 8 connection identities/dashboard, 64 consumers/identity, 16 distinct source/quality subscriptions/connection.
Operations & retries10s timeout per HTTP/negotiation/RPC operation, max 32 pending RPCs, backoff 1→2→4→8→16→30s (max 8 automatic attempts).
Demand & cleanupHidden tabs, offscreen/removed cards, and selection changes release demand; removing the final consumer closes the peer and calls /delete.
Layout & theme updatesTheme changes do not reconnect. Lovelace responsive column reflows can detach/reattach cards and trigger a clean reconnect.
SymptomWhat to check
Card missing from pickerConfirm module path and JavaScript module resource URL, then reload.
Connection failsCheck endpoint, TLS cert, exact allowed_origins, and Chromium Local Network Access permission (browsers report all four as an indistinguishable fetch error; never disable browser security).
Access deniedVerify the dedicated key is enabled and unexpired in KeepPeek.
Unknown source IDClick Load sources in the visual editor and select an ID visible to this credential.
One camera offlineInspect that camera in KeepPeek; other sources continue independently.
Video does not playVerify browser codec support and reachability of KeepPeek’s advertised WebRTC addresses.

Test locally and in CI

CheckCommands (from repository root)Scope
Lightweight Lovelace harness previewbun run --cwd ui test:e2e:prepare then bun run --cwd ui demo:home-assistantReal KeepPeek + 2 synthetic RTSP cameras in an emulated dashboard shell; zero impact on operator config.
Real Home Assistant container suitebun run --cwd ui test:home-assistant-container (or :run to reuse artifacts)Docker + Playwright Chromium against official HA 2026.9.1 (pinned multi-arch digest; 2 CPUs, 2 GiB, loopback-only, non-privileged, auto-cleanup). KeepPeek, cameras, and Chromium run on host to avoid Docker NAT.

The Home Assistant Container Ubuntu CI job is required by the UI gate (normal ./check.sh stays Docker-free and typechecks the suite). macOS ARM64 Docker Desktop works for local development testing only and is not a supported Home Assistant production deployment target. Local container tests do not replace post-release HACS install, upgrade, and rollback verification. See the detailed Home Assistant card reference for full transport and test details.

MCP server and LLM configuration

KeepPeek includes built-in Model Context Protocol (MCP) support so AI assistants and MCP clients (such as Claude, Codex, Cursor, and Gemini) can inspect recorder health, logs, events, storage, and recording coverage, onboard and tune cameras without exposing plaintext credentials, and preview or apply validated configuration changes.

MCP is disabled by default for network access. You can connect locally through the keeppeek mcp stdio bridge or enable the embedded Streamable HTTP endpoint (POST /mcp) in config.toml.

flowchart LR
    A[Local AI client] -->|stdio JSON-RPC| B[keeppeek mcp bridge]
    B -->|Bearer auth over HTTP or HTTPS| K[KeepPeek POST /mcp]
    R[Remote MCP client] -->|HTTPS Bearer auth| P[Trusted reverse proxy or VPN]
    P --> K
    U[Local operator browser] -->|Loopback single-use token| S[GET/POST /mcp/secret-entry]
    S --> K

Use the bundled agent skills

KeepPeek ships two canonical agent skills:

  • skills/keeppeek-mcp/SKILL.md teaches LLM assistants how to locate config.toml and secrets.toml, collect missing secrets out-of-band via request_secret_entry or keeppeek secret, probe cameras with {secret:KEY} references, run plan_configuration_change or dry-run hardware previews before requesting writes, and respect privacy and role boundaries.
  • skills/keeppeek-camera-debug/SKILL.md teaches assistants how to opt in safely to [mcp].allow_camera_debugging, capture decrypted PCAPNG and raw elementary bitstreams via camera_debugging, triage artifacts with tshark/ffprobe/ffmpeg, and export sanitized offline fixtures for ReplayCameraServer and keeppeek-camera replay.

List, print, or export bundled skills from any installed KeepPeek binary:

keeppeek mcp skill --list
keeppeek mcp skill
keeppeek mcp skill --output ./skills/keeppeek-mcp
keeppeek mcp skill camera-debug
keeppeek mcp skill camera-debug --output ./skills/keeppeek-camera-debug

Release artifacts and container images also include both SKILL.md files directly:

PackageShipped skill paths
Linux .tar.gz archivekeeppeek-<version>-<platform>/skills/keeppeek-mcp/SKILL.md, keeppeek-<version>-<platform>/skills/keeppeek-camera-debug/SKILL.md
Windows .zip archivekeeppeek-<version>-<platform>\skills\keeppeek-mcp\SKILL.md, keeppeek-<version>-<platform>\skills\keeppeek-camera-debug\SKILL.md
macOS .dmg (KeepPeek.app)KeepPeek.app/Contents/Resources/skills/keeppeek-mcp/SKILL.md, KeepPeek.app/Contents/Resources/skills/keeppeek-camera-debug/SKILL.md
Container image/usr/local/share/keeppeek/skills/keeppeek-mcp/SKILL.md, /usr/local/share/keeppeek/skills/keeppeek-camera-debug/SKILL.md

To guide an assistant (Claude, Codex, Cursor, or Gemini), copy the relevant skill folder into the assistant’s skills directory or point the assistant at the exported SKILL.md file.

Enable and configure [mcp]

All MCP server settings live in the existing config.toml under [mcp]. Every capability that opens network access, mutates configuration, writes on-camera hardware settings, captures camera frames, or captures raw camera traffic defaults to false.

A human Administrator can enable or disable MCP access live without restarting KeepPeek from Settings > Integrations > MCP server (trusted local network access or an interactive OIDC/identity-proxy browser session; explicit confirmation is required to enable — remote browser sessions authenticated with a Bearer access key cannot enable MCP or silence warnings). To change policy limits (allow_writes, allow_snapshots, allow_camera_device_writes, or payload/rate caps), stop KeepPeek before editing config.toml manually, add [mcp], and restart the server:

[mcp]
enabled = true
allow_writes = false
allow_snapshots = false
allow_camera_device_writes = false
allow_camera_debugging = false
debug_camera_ids = []
max_debug_capture_bytes = 67108864
max_debug_storage_bytes = 536870912
max_concurrent_requests = 4
max_response_bytes = 65536
request_timeout_seconds = 15
max_snapshot_bytes = 262144
max_snapshot_dimension = 1280
snapshot_min_interval_seconds = 5

Security warning: Setting [mcp].allow_camera_debugging = true grants Administrator MCP clients and AI assistants full raw access to live video bitstreams, audio streams, decrypted network captures (PCAPNG), and camera credentials for debug_camera_ids (or all configured cameras when empty). KeepPeek emits a prominent tracing::warn! on startup and adds a warning issue to ServerHealthResponse whenever both enabled and allow_camera_debugging are true. Enable it only during active protocol debugging and set allow_camera_debugging = false afterward.

FieldTypeDefaultAllowed rangeMeaning
enabledboolfalsetrue/falseEnable the embedded Streamable HTTP endpoint at POST /mcp (and loopback /mcp/secret-entry). Live via Web UI or disable_mcp_access.
enabled_at_unix_secsOptional u64AbsentUnix epoch secsServer-managed timestamp recorded when enabled becomes true; used for the 7-day enabled warning and cleared when disabled.
long_enabled_warning_silencedboolfalsetrue/falseHuman Administrator acknowledgement silencing the 7-day enabled warning for the current enabled stretch; cleared when disabled.
allow_writesboolfalsetrue/falsePermit Administrator KeepPeek configuration write tools after explicit approval.
allow_snapshotsboolfalsetrue/falsePermit privacy-gated single-frame snapshots via get_camera_snapshot.
allow_camera_device_writesboolfalsetrue/falsePermit live on-camera hardware writes via update_camera_device_settings (dry_run: false).
allow_camera_debuggingboolfalsetrue/falsePermit Administrator live camera packet/bitstream capture and repro-fixture export via camera_debugging.
debug_camera_idsVec<String>[]<= 256 IDsOptional camera ID allowlist for camera_debugging (empty allows all cameras; each trimmed non-empty ASCII ID <= 128 bytes).
max_debug_capture_bytesusize671088641048576..=268435456Maximum capture artifact size in bytes (1 MiB..=256 MiB; default 64 MiB).
max_debug_storage_bytesu6453687091216777216..=4294967296Total disk budget under <config-dir>/camera-debug/ in bytes (16 MiB..=4 GiB; default 512 MiB, 24h TTL, FIFO eviction).
max_concurrent_requestsusize41..=32Maximum simultaneous in-flight MCP requests.
