Opt-in camera debugging tool (1)

camera_debugging is Administrator-only and fails closed unless [mcp].allow_camera_debugging = true. When [mcp].debug_camera_ids is non-empty, requests for cameras outside the allowlist are rejected. Mutating/capture actions (capture, compare_transports, delete_capture) reject --read-only sessions (X-KeepPeek-MCP-Read-Only: true).

ToolMinimum rolePurpose
camera_debuggingAdministratorInspect camera ingest pipelines, capture decrypted PCAPNG and raw bitstreams, compare transports, export sanitized repro fixtures, or clean up captures.

Supported camera_debugging actions:

  • inspect_pipeline: Inspect active camera backend, stream state, health, and profile configuration from live ServerState.
  • capture: Run a bounded capture (duration_seconds 1..=60, max_bytes 65536..=268435456, stream: "main" | "sub" | "both") collecting requested artifacts ("pcapng", "video_bitstream", "audio_bitstream", "muxed_mp4", "protocol_transcript") across protocols ("rtsp", "audio", "onvif", "isapi", "reolink_http", "reo_proto").
  • compare_transports: Compare mode: "media_transports" or mode: "event_channels" side-by-side for a camera.
  • export_repro_fixture: Export a keeppeek.camera-repro.v1 JSON fixture (strip_credentials default true, anonymize_network default true, trim_media: "headers_and_minimal_failing_slice" | "synthetic_payloads" | "keep_raw_frames") with a sharing_safety_report.
  • list_captures: List saved captures under <config-dir>/camera-debug/<capture-id>/ with byte sizes and created_at_unix_secs timestamps (24-hour TTL entries are pruned on list).
  • delete_capture: Delete one capture_id or all saved captures (delete_all: true).

Self-disable governance tool (1)

disable_mcp_access is available to authenticated Administrator callers even when [mcp].allow_writes = false, unless --read-only or X-KeepPeek-MCP-Read-Only: true is active. Re-enabling MCP always requires a human Administrator.

ToolMinimum rolePurpose
disable_mcp_accessAdministratorRequires confirm: true (optional reason). Persists [mcp].enabled = false, clears pending handoffs, and disables /mcp live.

Multi-candidate stream probing and on-camera hardware support

Pre-populated secrets.toml multi-candidate probing

If a site uses a few known camera credential pairs already stored in secrets.toml, an assistant can pass up to 5 {secret:KEY} pairs in probe_camera_streams.credential_candidates:

  • Raw credential fields and inline RTSP userinfo or query-string credentials are rejected.
  • Targets are SSRF-bounded: unspecified, multicast, broadcast, link-local (169.254.0.0/16), public IPs, unconfigured loopback addresses, and mismatched RTSP URL hosts are blocked.
  • Probing stops immediately on the first matching credential pair or if the camera reports lockout, HTTP 429, or becomes unreachable.
  • Responses return only matched_credentials ({secret:KEY} references), failed_candidates indexes, recommended_backend, verified RTSP URLs, and redacted diagnostics.

Honest on-camera hardware capability matrix

get_camera_device_settings and update_camera_device_settings normalize hardware controls into seven sections (encoder, imaging, lighting, motion, osd, time, ptz) and report unsupported sections honestly per protocol:

Camera protocolSupported sections and capabilities
Reolinkencoder (read), imaging (brightness, contrast, saturation, sharpness, day/night, HDR), lighting (IR mode, white LED), motion (MD & AI sensitivity), osd (channel name, max 32 chars), time (clock/NTP), ptz (presets, guard).
Hikvision ISAPIencoder (stream resolution, bitrate, framerate), motion (motion + smart rules: line, field, region_entrance, region_exit), time (manual/ntp), ptz (store_preset 1–300).
ONVIFencoder (video encoder settings validated against camera GetVideoEncoderConfigurationOptions), osd (plain-text OSD up to 64 chars).
RTSP-onlyNone (supported_sections: [], all 7 sections listed in unsupported_sections; live writes return 501).

Hard security exclusions: update_camera_device_settings permanently refuses firmware upload or flashing, factory reset or default restore, camera reboot, password/user/credential modifications, and IP/network/DHCP/gateway/DNS/port reassignment with HTTP 403, even when dry_run: true.

Deep camera protocol debugging and offline replay

When standard health and log tools are not enough to diagnose an RTSP, RTP, ONVIF, ISAPI, or Reolink Baichuan (reo_proto) interoperability defect, enable [mcp].allow_camera_debugging = true temporarily and export the keeppeek-camera-debug skill (keeppeek mcp skill camera-debug).

  1. Capture on the running server: Run camera_debugging (inspect_pipeline -> capture -> compare_transports). KeepPeek writes bounded artifacts under <config-dir>/camera-debug/<capture-id>/ (capture.pcapng with Ethernet/IP/TCP/UDP framing around decrypted payloads, raw .h264/.h265 and audio elementary streams, rtp_packets.bin when raw RTP dump is enabled, muxed.mp4, sdp.txt, protocol_transcript.json, and atomically written capture.json) with a 24-hour TTL and FIFO eviction at max_debug_storage_bytes.
  2. Triage locally with standard tools: Inspect capture.pcapng with tshark (-Y "rtsp || rtp || http || xml", -z follow,tcp,ascii,0, -T fields -e rtp.seq -e rtp.timestamp -e h264.nal_unit_type), ffprobe -show_packets -show_frames muxed.mp4, and ffmpeg -v error -i video.h264 -f null -.
  3. Export a sanitized fixture and reproduce offline: Call camera_debugging (action: "export_repro_fixture", strip_credentials: true, anonymize_network: true) and verify sharing_safety_report.safe_to_share_publicly is true (RFC 5737 192.0.2.x addresses and stripped credentials). Reproduce deterministically using ReplayCameraServer in crates/test-camera/src/replay.rs (keeppeek.camera-repro.v1) or interactively via keeppeek-camera replay --fixture <PATH> [--bind 127.0.0.1:0] [--once | --loop]. Build and test fixes locally with cargo nextest and cargo run --bin keeppeek against a test configuration (live binary hot-swap via keeppeek-supervisor is coming separately).
  4. Clean up and re-lock: Delete local captures with camera_debugging (action: "delete_capture"), stop KeepPeek, set allow_camera_debugging = false in config.toml, and restart KeepPeek.

Safe configuration workflow

KeepPeek enforces plan-before-apply so an assistant never modifies cameras, hardware settings, or storage blindly:

  1. Inspect the current configuration (get_configuration) and available secret key names (list_secret_keys).
  2. Add missing camera or integration passwords out-of-band via keeppeek secret set <KEY> or the loopback request_secret_entry UI handoff (or KEEPPEEK_SECRET_<KEY> in the server environment), and reference them as {secret:KEY} or {secret:KEY|url}. Never paste raw passwords into an LLM prompt.
  3. Run discover_cameras and search_camera_catalog for new devices, verify streams with probe_camera_streams, then call plan_configuration_change (or preview a manage_camera / manage_notification_rule payload).
  4. Review the returned redacted diff, validation errors, revision, and live-apply versus restart summary.
  5. Approve the plan explicitly, then allow the assistant to call apply_configuration_plan (or the approved manage_camera / manage_notification_rule write action).
  6. If tuning on-camera settings, inspect with get_camera_device_settings, preview with update_camera_device_settings (dry_run: true), approve explicitly, and apply with dry_run: false (requires [mcp].allow_camera_device_writes = true).
  7. Check get_server_health and search_logs to confirm activation, or restart KeepPeek when the plan reports that a restart is required.

See Visual configuration management for revision conflicts, inheritance, and template rules.

Security, privacy, and resource boundaries

Authentication, RBAC, and audit

POST /mcp reuses KeepPeek’s standard [access] authentication and authorization pipeline:

  • Remote requests require an active Bearer credential in the Authorization header. Query-string tokens are rejected, and failed authentication rate limits apply.
  • User credentials can call only get_server_health, search_events, get_recording_coverage, and (when enabled) get_camera_snapshot, strictly scoped to their granted cameras.
  • Administrator credentials are required for logs, configuration, secret key listing, secret entry handoffs, catalog search, stream probing, discovery, storage, notifications, profiles, hardware settings, and write tools.
  • Privileged reads, configuration mutations, secret-entry submissions (key names only), on-camera hardware updates, and snapshot requests record bounded entries in KeepPeek’s security audit without logging raw credentials or image bytes.

Remember that KeepPeek’s [access].local_networks policy grants Administrator access to trusted local addresses. Narrow local_networks if a local MCP client must be restricted to a User credential.

Secret redaction

Every MCP response, configuration snapshot, dry-run diff, probe diagnostic, log search result, and error passes through serialization-layer secret redaction before leaving KeepPeek:

  • Raw values loaded from secrets.toml (including URL percent-encoded variants) are scrubbed.
  • Fields with credential-like names (password, secret, token, access_key, verifier, cookie, authorization, api_key, private_key) are redacted unless they are already {secret:KEY} or {secret:KEY|url} references.
  • Embedded URL credentials (scheme://user:pass@host) are scrubbed while {secret:KEY} and {secret:KEY|url} tokens remain visible for safe editing.
  • Explicit camera_debugging boundary: When [mcp].allow_camera_debugging = true, SecretRedactor is bypassed by default on capture and compare_transports (both default include_raw_auth: true — pass include_raw_auth: false to redact), bypassed on inspect_pipeline only when include_raw_auth: true is passed (defaults to false), and bypassed on export_repro_fixture only when strip_credentials: false is passed (defaults to true). SecretRedactor remains strictly enforced on all other 28 MCP tools, error messages, and whenever export_repro_fixture runs with strip_credentials: true.

DNS rebinding and cross-origin protection

POST /mcp validates the Host header and enforces an explicit browser origin allowlist:

  • Non-browser MCP clients that omit Origin are permitted.
  • Unauthenticated local requests (when [access].local_networks grants local trust) require Host to be localhost or an IP literal (127.0.0.1, [::1]) so DNS-rebound domain names are rejected with 403.
  • Requests carrying a browser Origin header are rejected with 403 unless the exact origin is listed in [direct_card].allowed_origins, and require Authorization: Bearer <key> even on local networks (returning 401 if unauthenticated).
  • /mcp/secret-entry is stricter still: it allows loopback peers only, rejects proxy-forwarded headers, and requires an exact same-origin loopback Origin on POST.
  • Unsupported HTTP methods on /mcp or /mcp/secret-entry return 405 Method Not Allowed, and a disabled MCP server returns 404 Not Found.

Fail-closed privacy and snapshot limits

get_camera_snapshot enforces four sequential gates before returning any image bytes:

  1. [mcp].allow_snapshots must be true.
  2. The authenticated principal must have view permission for the requested camera.
  3. The per-camera rate limit (snapshot_min_interval_seconds) must not be exceeded.
  4. Camera privacy state ([privacy] schedules and runtime profile overrides) must be verified as inactive. If privacy mode is active or cannot be verified, KeepPeek fails closed and refuses the snapshot.

Decoded frames use bounded image decoder limits, downscale to at most max_snapshot_dimension pixels per side while preserving aspect ratio, and encode JPEG within max_snapshot_bytes. KeepPeek does not apply server-side pixel masks; only camera-side (hardware) privacy masks already rendered into the camera stream appear in the returned frame.

Concurrency and payload caps

KeepPeek caps simultaneous MCP executions at max_concurrent_requests, enforces request_timeout_seconds, and rejects oversized JSON-RPC responses above max_response_bytes (get_camera_snapshot uses max_snapshot_bytes plus base64 and metadata envelope overhead) so an automated assistant cannot starve camera ingest, recording, or live playback.

Troubleshooting

SymptomCheck
POST /mcp or /mcp/secret-entry returns 404Confirm MCP is enabled by a human Administrator in Settings > Integrations (or [mcp].enabled = true in config.toml + restart).
POST /mcp returns 403Check whether Origin is missing from [direct_card].allowed_origins, Host is non-local for local trust, or role is User.
/mcp/secret-entry returns 403 or 410Open the link directly from loopback without a reverse proxy, and verify the single-use token has not expired or been consumed.
POST /mcp returns 426Remote plain HTTP is blocked by [access].require_secure_remote = true. Use HTTPS via a trusted proxy or a private VPN.
Configuration write tool is deniedConfirm [mcp].allow_writes = true, an Administrator credential, and that --read-only is not set.
On-camera hardware write is denied (403)Confirm [mcp].allow_camera_device_writes = true, --read-only is not set, and no excluded security field was requested.
probe_camera_streams rejects inputReplace any raw password or inline RTSP credentials with {secret:KEY} refs and keep credential_candidates to at most 5.
get_camera_snapshot is deniedConfirm [mcp].allow_snapshots = true, camera view permission, inactive privacy mode, and the per-camera interval limit.
camera_debugging is deniedConfirm [mcp].allow_camera_debugging = true, an Administrator credential, debug_camera_ids scope, and no --read-only flag for capture/delete actions.
Request returns 429 or 503 or times outToo many simultaneous MCP calls (max_concurrent_requests), camera rate-limit/lockout, or slow probe exceeding timeout.
apply_configuration_plan fails conflictConfiguration changed after the plan was created. Call get_configuration and generate a fresh plan.
keeppeek mcp fails to connect remotelyConfirm KEEPPEEK_ACCESS_KEY or --credential-file is set and readable, and verify --server points to the HTTPS origin.

Groups and two-way audio: current status

Live participant groups are not operational in the current KeepPeek server and browser runtime. This page records that unavailability boundary, distinguishes the three different uses of “group” across KeepPeek, and separates planned client-to-client participant groups from shipped camera talkback.

Current status

Surface / layerCurrent state
Settings → Groups (Groups & two-way audio)Displays Group directory unavailable; management actions explain the missing runtime handler.
Generated protobuf contract (api/)Declares GroupCommand (ListGroups, JoinGroup, LeaveGroup), GroupCapability, and GroupState.
Running server & browser runtimeDoes not handle GroupCommand; no group names, participant counts, join sessions, or recordings exist.
config.tomlNo [groups] section exists. Do not invent one or infer runtime support from generated types.

Consult the configuration reference for all supported config.toml sections.

Three different uses of grouping

ConceptShipped todayPurposeWhat it does not grant
Camera group labels and access grantsYesOrganize cameras via TOML namespaces (e.g. outdoor) and scope User accessMembership in any voice, walkie-talkie, or conference session
Saved dashboards and their audienceYesShare a live-wall layout (peek_layouts) with selected viewersAccess to cameras outside the viewer’s own camera grants
Live participant groupsNoPlanned named camera collections with optional live participant audio/video fanoutAny working walkie-talkie, conference, or group admin UI today

Use Authentication and access control for camera group grants and Live wall for saved dashboards. Both work independently of live participant groups.

Camera talkback vs live participant groups

Support for one does not imply support for the other:

FeatureDirection & targetShipped todayWhere to configure / read more
Browser TalkAdministrator microphone → physical camera speakerYesCamera controls
Broadcast talkbackAdministrator microphone → all or camera-group speakersYesCamera controls
Live participant groupsBrowser/client ↔ client media fanout via KeepPeekNoPlanned design only (see below)

Planned group design contract

The group client scenarios document the intended protocol contract for a future implementation:

  • Server-owned definitions: Named collections of static camera streams (never client publications), optional join password (never returned in list/state responses), and optional live audio/video capability. Group definitions are intentionally excluded from ServerCapabilities.
  • Full-duplex media without floor control: All joined participants could publish simultaneously with no moderator or speaking-turn arbitration; push-to-talk would only gate local client microphone capture while keeping the publication warm.
  • Per-participant virtual sources and recording: Each joined participant would receive a server-assigned virtual source_id with independent audio/video recording policy (DISABLED, INHERIT, REQUIRED, PROHIBITED) without altering static camera recording policies.

These scenarios specify intended contract behavior only—they are not evidence that the server handler, client capture path, directory, or participant recording pipeline is implemented or qualified.

How KeepPeek works

This page explains KeepPeek’s runtime architecture, media and event pipelines, persistent state boundaries, and protocol surfaces. Use Users and design choices for product rationale and Feature guide and navigation for UI navigation.

KeepPeek runs recording, live media routing, event storage, and investigation in one local Rust service. Browsers and keeppeek-desktop are clients of that service: closing a tab or desktop window ends viewing work without stopping the server’s configured recording policy.

Media and event paths

flowchart TB
    Camera[IP camera] -->|Encoded media| Core[KeepPeek]
    Core -->|Record| MP4[MP4 and catalog]
    Core -->|Live WebRTC| Browser[Client]
    MP4 -->|Playback via KeepPeek| Browser

Cameras supply encoded video (H.264 or H.265) and supported audio. KeepPeek parses codecs and timing to route, index, and write fragmented ISO MP4 segments without decoding or re-encoding video in the core ingest path; browsers and external services perform their own decoding. (When optional silent MP4 timelapse export is enabled and a user-installed ffmpeg binary is present, KeepPeek runs that external encoder incrementally outside the recording ingest path.)

Camera control, native events, and media can use separate protocol connections, so health on one path does not prove the others are working. Native adapters (Reolink Baichuan, Hikvision/Annke ISAPI, ONVIF PullPoint, RTSP metadata) and optional external WebRTC analysis services publish events into a separate pipeline:

flowchart TB
    Source[Camera or analysis service] --> Events[Validate and store event]
    Events --> Review[Review, search, and analytics]
    Events --> Delivery[Rules, push signaling, and MQTT]

The server isolates camera ingest, storage writes, WebRTC sessions, and outbound notification or MQTT delivery. Bounded queues and explicit health states keep stalls visible; optional analysis, notifications, and MQTT never become prerequisites for ordinary recording.

Persistent state vs. runtime memory

KeepPeek keeps configuration in config.toml and reusable private strings in the companion secrets.toml using {secret:KEY} and {secret:KEY|url} references; do not create separate settings files for layouts, permissions, or desktop targets. See Configuration reference for exact schemas and Backup and restore for restart guarantees.

Store or stateOwner and persistenceOperational consequence
Application configuration (config.toml)Persisted settings, cameras, storage policies, runtime profiles, saved layouts, access policy, notification rules, usage-reporting consent, and optional [desktop].server_url.Edit through validated saves or configuration ZIP apply; preserve across upgrades.
Reusable secrets (secrets.toml)Companion store for named and URL-encoded secret references.KeepPeek preserves references instead of writing resolved secrets into config.toml; protect ZIP exports and backups.
Encoded recordings (.mp4, .mp4.active)Fragmented ISO MP4 files across configured storage paths and named storage volumes.Retention, recording policy, storage pressure, and file integrity determine what remains playable.
Fourth-tier local archiveVerified replica MP4s on an optional confined local destination.Extends retention independently of ingest; recover catalog manifests and archive root together.
Recording and event catalog (recordings.db)Indexed recording identity, coverage, events, bookmarks, saved searches, activity rollups, export jobs, and guest review grants.Back up and recover consistently with media files; see Recording archive recovery.
Event images and exportsFeature-owned thumbnails, clip exports, and timelapse MP4/manifest artifacts under configured paths.Playable recordings do not guarantee that thumbnails or expired export artifacts remain.
Notification, MQTT, and access runtime stateBounded process memory for notification inbox/history/retries, MQTT pending outbox/deduplication, active sessions, and access audit/last-use activity.Resets on restart without replaying pending deliveries; persisted rules and credentials remain durable.
Active connections and subscriptionsEphemeral server and client session state (WebRTC peers, camera workers, subscriptions).Reconnect resolves current capabilities; a stored preference never proves a stream is live.

Control, media, and access plane

SurfaceTransport and contractResponsibilities
WebRTC sessionsRTP/UDP + SCTP data channels (api/webrtc.proto)Live video/audio, stored playback, typed protobuf control, capability negotiation, event search/analytics, and external media/event subscriptions.
HTTP routesDedicated REST endpoints (api/openapi.yaml)Session and OIDC auth (/auth/session, /auth/login, /auth/callback, /auth/logout), WebRTC signaling (/create, /delete), config ZIP export/apply (/config/export, /config/apply), backups (/api/backups/*), guest review (/share), logs (/logs, /logs/snapshot), bug reporting (/reporting/*), coverage (/recording-coverage), and Prometheus (GET /metrics).
Operator clientsBrowser UI, keeppeek-desktop, foreground TUIStatic Svelte 5 app served by KeepPeek, optional Tauri v2 desktop window on Windows/macOS, and terminal health dashboard on interactive TTY runs.

HTTP routes complement WebRTC rather than replacing the WebRTC control protocol. Clients must negotiate capabilities and source identities at runtime instead of assuming identical streams, codecs, controls, or events across cameras. The API is pre-1.0 and does not yet promise cross-version stability; start with the API overview.

Authentication identifies the caller (local trusted network, named credential, or external OIDC provider); authorization enforces Administrator vs. User roles and per-camera/group grants uniformly across dashboards, deep links, WebRTC commands, and HTTP routes. A hidden UI control is never the security boundary (Authentication).

Recording truth and diagnostics

Keep these four diagnostic layers separate so a browser playback issue does not trigger unnecessary storage repair:

  1. Live stream health: Confirms camera transport and incoming frames, not disk writes.
  2. Recorded coverage: Confirms cataloged MP4 intervals and gaps for a camera and UTC day.
  3. Events and analytics: Confirms validated event metadata; a retained event can outlive its thumbnail or underlying video.
  4. Stored integrity and exports: Confirms persisted MP4 containers, catalog alignment, and independently verifiable export bytes.

Start troubleshooting in Camera and stream health and Recording and evidence; run Recording maintenance only when stored-data evidence warrants it.

Extension boundaries and codebase map

External analysis services subscribe to WebRTC media and publish normalized events without receiving camera credentials or direct access to SQLite or recording directories. The Home Assistant card connects the browser directly to KeepPeek instead of proxying video through Home Assistant. Declared protocol types without server handlers—such as live participant groups—are unavailable until their runtime contract ships.

Entry pointResponsibility
src/app.rsApplication startup, subsystem wiring, and lifecycle orchestration.
src/server.rsHTTP routing, auth, config export/apply, backups, diagnostics, and /metrics.
src/webrtc.rsWebRTC peer management, media forwarding, and protobuf control dispatch.
src/storageFragmented MP4 recording, SQLite catalog, volumes, archive, timelapse, and exports.
src/tuiForeground terminal health dashboard.
crates/keeppeek-desktopOptional Windows and macOS Tauri desktop client.

For build, test, and contribution rules, see Contributing.

Users and design choices

This page explains who KeepPeek is built for, how it decides which cameras and protocols to support, and the architectural trade-offs and non-goals behind the recorder. It records product rationale and intended boundaries; use the feature guide for current screen availability, How KeepPeek works for runtime behavior, and release readiness for Alpha qualification limits.

KeepPeek is a local-first network video recorder and WebRTC media gateway with a deliberately narrow center: ingest camera media, record it reliably as standard MP4, serve live and recorded streams, and provide a stable boundary for camera-native or external events. Inference frameworks, models, accelerators, and licensing change much faster than a recorder should, so KeepPeek exposes an open protocol instead of forcing one AI stack into every installation.

Target users

UserStarting pointPrimary priorityKeepPeek’s role
Software ConsumerCommon cameras and a home monitoring goalSensible defaults and dependable playbackComplete first-party recording, live wall, and review experience
Small Business SecurityShop, office, workshop, or yard with domain-specific risksRelevant alerts and portable evidenceStable recorder core plus open boundary for tailored inference
Open Source EnthusiastExisting self-hosted tools and reusable componentsInspectability and replaceable partsComposable open core (AGPL server, MIT API) without vendor lock-in
Home Automation EnthusiastHome Assistant, MQTT, HAP, and household automationsLocal orchestration and stable device identitiesCamera and event source without proxying video through the hub
Residential SysadminHome server, NAS, container host, or monitoring stackPredictable operations and clear diagnosticsNative service with reason codes, bounded logs, and /metrics
Residential Power Optimization EngineerMixed cameras and compute hosts with power/heat limitsDeliberate workload placement and efficiencyLow-overhead media plane that feeds whichever host should decode or analyze

Software Consumer

I just want it to work.

  • Workflow: Install KeepPeek, run guided camera setup with safe defaults, watch live/recorded footage in the Svelte web UI or desktop app, and act on clear diagnoses rather than raw protocol errors.
  • Product implication: Optional architecture stays out of the way until needed; core recording works without a cloud account, AI subscription, or accelerator setup. Start with Get started, then verify camera health and recording coverage.

Small Business Security

Custom detection for the way the business actually works.

  • Workflow: Run KeepPeek for local recording and portable MP4 evidence, attach an external inference service tuned to business-specific objects, zones, and schedules over WebRTC, and route resulting events into timelines, notifications, MQTT, or webhooks.
  • Product implication: Custom business logic lives in an external protocol plugin rather than a private recorder fork, keeping camera credentials and storage ownership inside KeepPeek while recording stays independent of model changes. Need a tailored deployment or custom inference integration? Contact KeepPeek to discuss business-specific requirements.

Open Source Enthusiast

Because someone else already wrote it.

  • Workflow: Deploy KeepPeek alongside existing self-hosted software, inspect the wire contract and generated bindings, and attach or swap independent detectors, transcoders, forwarders, or viewers.
  • Product implication: The licensing model (AGPL server, MIT API) protects server improvements while letting external clients and services choose their own licenses and business models.

Home Automation Enthusiast

Because the porch light should have opinions.

  • Workflow: Embed direct WebRTC views in Home Assistant, trigger automations from normalized MQTT events or webhooks, optionally expose cameras to Apple Home through an independent HAP bridge, and monitor health via Prometheus.
  • Product implication: KeepPeek maintains stable source IDs, normalized event semantics, direct browser media, and explicit integration boundaries without trying to replace an automation hub.

Residential Sysadmin

Unnecessarily professional.

  • Workflow: Run one native service or container, validate every camera before trusting it, monitor explicit server/storage/stream/WebRTC health evidence and bounded logs, and separate transport failures from storage issues.
  • Product implication: KeepPeek exposes stable health reason codes, structured logs, Prometheus metrics, and validated configuration/backup workflows in a predictable Rust binary.

Residential Power Optimization Engineer

  • Workflow: Place KeepPeek near cameras and disks, record camera-native encoded streams without local inference overhead, and subscribe to WebRTC media from whichever host (GPU box, desktop, or repurposed edge device) should decode or analyze it.
  • Product implication: The core stays observable and lightweight, and its media boundary never assumes decoding or inference runs on the recording host.

Cameras, brands, and protocols

Mixed-vendor fleets are normal in homes and small businesses. Compatibility is therefore defined by verified protocols and validated media—never by a brand logo alone. A discovered device is a candidate, not proof that authentication, streaming, controls, or events work.

Brand integration paths

Camera familyIntegration pathCurrent meaning
ReolinkDedicated reo-proto backend for Baichuan over TCP or UDP, with ONVIF and RTSP fallback pathsFirst-class discovery, authentication, main/sub media, supported controls (including confirmed-speaker Talk), and native camera events
Dahua and compatible OEM/rebrand modelsONVIF discovery and profile queries with RTSP media through Retina or explicit stream URLsSupported standards-based family covering common H.264/AAC and H.265/G.711 patterns; each OEM model and firmware still requires validation
Tapo (supported models)ONVIF discovery and profile queries where available, with RTSP media through Retina or explicit stream URLsSupported mainstream vendor through the standards path; exact capabilities depend on model and firmware
Hikvision / AnnkeSADP discovery, ONVIF/RTSP media, and a plain-Rust ISAPI event workerNative event support depends on model, firmware, channel mapping, and enabled device rules; see Hikvision ISAPI
Other ONVIF camerasONVIF discovery and device/profile queries, then RTSP mediaSupport depends on the device’s standards implementation and must be validated per model
Generic or unknown camerasExplicit main and/or sub RTSP URLsRecording works without a recognized brand; discovery, controls, metadata, and native events may be unavailable

Pragmatic support tiers

KeepPeek deliberately targets strong support for a well-known subset rather than claiming universal compatibility across every cloud-only or non-standard camera. New vendor support is added from measured evidence and maintainable protocol paths, not one-off device hacks:

  1. Supported vendor and model: Documented firmware and config pass discovery/manual setup, authentication, main/sub streaming, keyframe detection, finalized MP4 recording, and independent playback validation.
  2. Protocol-compatible camera: Exposes usable ONVIF/RTSP behavior, but the exact model has not yet earned a maintained compatibility claim.
  3. Manual RTSP camera: Records a validated explicit stream URL even when discovery, controls, metadata, and native events are absent.

Protocol responsibilities and media policy

ResponsibilityProtocol or componentRole in KeepPeek
DiscoveryONVIF WS-Discovery, Reolink discovery, Hikvision SADP, bounded local port probesFind candidate devices and collect safe identity hints
Device queries and controlONVIF SOAP plus supported Reolink HTTP or Baichuan commandsRead device info, profiles, capabilities, imaging state, PTZ, and supported speaker talkback
Generic media ingestRTSP/RTP through Retina (TCP or UDP)Receive standards-based media, including explicit main/sub URLs
Reolink media ingestBaichuan through reo-proto (TCP or UDP)Receive complete Reolink video and audio frames without requiring RTSP
Native camera eventsReolink Baichuan, Hikvision/Annke ISAPI, ONVIF PullPoint, and RTSP metadataNormalize camera alarms into stored/live events using explicit event evidence
Viewer and service deliveryWebRTC (RTP/UDP + SCTP data channels)Deliver live/recorded media, protobuf control, and events without exposing camera credentials

KeepPeek accepts H.264 and H.265 video plus AAC, G.711 A-law, G.711 mu-law, and ADPCM audio, recording camera-native main and sub streams without re-encoding. Automatic backend selection picks reo-proto for Reolink and Retina otherwise; TCP is the conservative default, UDP remains available when validated, and explicit RTSP URLs override ONVIF-discovered URLs. Camera setup verifies credentials, incoming keyframes, finalized MP4 output, and independent decode before recommending a live configuration.

Key design choices

Keep inference outside the server

KeepPeek does not bundle object detection, face recognition, license-plate recognition, or model management. Installations choose independently among three modes: recording only (no analytics), camera-native analytics (device alarms stored alongside media), or external analytics (separate service subscribing over WebRTC and publishing normalized events). The repository’s Python object-detection example demonstrates the external path using aiortc and a user-installed FFmpeg binary as a CI reference, not a supported detector product.

KeepPeek forwards encoded streams rather than per-frame inference JPEGs:

Snapshot polling (rejected):  camera -> KeepPeek decode -> JPEG encode -> HTTP upload -> detector decode -> model
KeepPeek media gateway:       camera -> KeepPeek encoded forwarding -> detector decode & sample -> model

Avoided snapshot polling removes video decoding, pixel conversion, JPEG allocation, and repeated decode/encode hops from the core recorder. External detectors keep one hardware-appropriate decoder beside the model, choose their own sampling rate, and drop stale frames. JPEG remains useful strictly as bounded post-inference event evidence attached to a durable event.

Keep recording and media routing at the center

KeepPeek owns the slow-changing, durable responsibilities: ingest, MP4 recording, stable source identities, timelines, health evidence, authorization, and event validation. External inference and transcoding services consume media through the gateway rather than opening camera sessions or touching SQLite or recording directories directly, ensuring recording continues even if optional services stall or go offline.

Use WebRTC as the client and service gateway

flowchart LR
  C1[Supported camera] -->|Encoded stream| K[KeepPeek<br/>Record, route, validate]
  C2[Supported camera] -->|Encoded stream| K
  K -->|MP4| S[(Recording storage)]
  K -->|WebRTC media| B[Browser or viewer]
  K -->|WebRTC media| I[Inference service]
  I -->|Events| K
  K -->|Normalized events| A[Home automation]

WebRTC is the downstream gateway protocol, not a requirement imposed on cameras. It is built into browsers and has interoperable libraries across major service languages—Rust (str0m, used by KeepPeek), Python (aiortc), C++ (libwebrtc), and Node/TypeScript (werift)—so clients and detectors can pick the stack and hardware (server, GPU host, or repurposed mobile/edge device) that fits them without ever receiving camera credentials. Inside one session, RTP over UDP carries low-latency media while SCTP data channels carry ordered protobuf control and binary payloads.

Fit into home automation instead of replacing it

IntegrationConnection boundaryStatus and role
Home AssistantLovelace card connects the browser directly to KeepPeek over WebRTCShipped card for live cameras without proxying video through Home Assistant; optional entity integration remains separate
MQTTBuilt-in data-only MQTT 5 event forwarder configured in Settings > IntegrationsShipped; publishes normalized events and optional snapshots to an existing broker without decoding video
PrometheusMonitoring stack scrapes GET /metricsShipped; exposes server, storage, camera, stream, recording, and WebRTC telemetry
HomeKit (HAP)Independent external bridge translating KeepPeek capabilities into HomeKit accessoriesIntended boundary (not in core); keeps HomeKit credentials and quirks outside the recorder

Store recordings as standard MP4

Recordings use fragmented ISO MP4 (.mp4.active while recording, finalized to .mp4 without remuxing). Initialization metadata followed by keyframe-aligned fragments allows safe flushing, exact byte-range timeline seeks, and direct playback or inspection in VLC, FFmpeg, and ffprobe without KeepPeek installed. The SQLite catalog indexes metadata and fragments for fast lookup, while encoded footage stays in ordinary files on disk. MP4 is a container, not a codec: playing an H.265 .mp4 still requires an H.265-capable player.

Keep video codecs out of the core service

KeepPeek is codec-aware (parsing H.264/H.265 framing, configuration, and keyframes) without bundling video decoders or encoders. Excluding codec packs avoids jurisdiction-dependent patent/licensing entanglement, large native attack surfaces, and unnecessary CPU/memory overhead. On desktop and mobile, the Svelte UI queries browser/OS codec support (Chrome/Chromium, Windows WebView2, macOS WKWebView) and selects a compatible stream; keep an H.264 substream for universal playback, or attach an independent transcoder that publishes a compatible variant through the gateway. Optional silent MP4 timelapse export is capability-gated on a host-installed external ffmpeg binary rather than bundled codecs.

Core stack, footprint, and cross-platform packaging

DecisionRationaleTrade-off / boundary
Rust serverMemory-safe, GC-free concurrent parsing of untrusted network/media streams across Linux, macOS, and Windows.Steeper contribution curve and longer compile times accepted for runtime reliability.
Small, focused coreKeeps inference runtimes, weights, codec packs, and an Electron browser out of the server binary.Baseline RAM stays modest, while the configurable encoded pre-event cache scales intentionally with stream count, bitrates, profiles, and pre-event duration; no hard total-RAM guarantee is published without workload measurements.
Svelte 5 static UICompiled reactivity and generated TypeScript protobuf bindings with no second web application server.Adds a frontend toolchain, but isolates UI work from the media process and works identically across host OSes.
AI-assistable setupStructured config, stable reason codes, and the repo’s Camera Setup Skill make discovery/validation scriptable by humans or assistants.Running the recorder never requires AI; setup skills keep secrets out of chat, stage config, validate MP4s, and require human approval before modifying camera settings.

KeepPeek maintains identical core behavior across platforms while packaging natively for each OS:

PlatformFirst-class distribution
LinuxMulti-arch GHCR Docker image (linux/amd64, linux/arm64) with a persistent configuration volume (primary Linux path), plus native linux-x86_64 and linux-aarch64 archives.
macOSSigned and notarized Apple Silicon (aarch64) KeepPeek.app DMG with a per-user launchd service, plus optional KeepPeek Desktop.app DMG (Tauri v2 client connecting to an existing server).
WindowsAuthenticode-signed, RFC 3161 timestamped x86_64 and aarch64 binaries (keeppeek.exe, keeppeek-service.exe, keeppeek-desktop.exe) and a unified signed NSIS installer registering/upgrading KeepPeekService.

Connecting users to choices

UserMost relevant choicesWhat those choices enable
Software ConsumerSvelte UI + desktop app, common camera paths, MP4, browser decoding, setup SkillInstall, record, review, and diagnose without assembling an integration stack
Small Business SecurityStable recording core, encoded-stream inference, open API, external pluginsCommission tailored analytics and workflows without making evidence depend on the model
Open Source EnthusiastAGPL server, MIT API, standard WebRTC, replaceable external servicesReuse, inspect, replace, and improve each component independently
Home Automation EnthusiastStable identities, normalized events, Home Assistant card, MQTT 5, HAP boundary, /metricsConnect cameras to dashboards, alerts, and automations without proxying video
Residential SysadminRust, compact core, native packages/Docker, health evidence, standard MP4Operate and recover a predictable service with standard infrastructure tools
Residential Power Optimization EngineerEncoded forwarding, external inference, browser decoding, flexible placementPut storage, decoding, inference, and integrations on the best host for power and heat

Boundaries and non-goals

  • KeepPeek is not a model host, training environment, detector marketplace, or GPU manager.
  • KeepPeek does not expose periodic server-generated JPEGs as its inference API.
  • Camera-native analytics must work without an external AI service; recording and viewing must not require camera AI, external AI, or cloud services.
  • External services and plugins never receive camera credentials or direct access to SQLite databases or recording directories.
  • The core service does not bundle a video decoder, encoder, codec pack, or packaged browser runtime.
  • MP4 storage guarantees container portability, not that every player can decode every camera codec.
  • No fixed total-RAM limit is promised before representative workload benchmarks; total memory includes the configured encoded pre-event cache.
  • The MIT-licensed API permits independent commercial clients and services; it does not exempt private modifications to the AGPL server.
  • KeepPeek does not replace Home Assistant, an MQTT broker, HomeKit, or a monitoring system.
  • AI-assisted setup must remain reproducible by a person, protect secrets, and never modify camera settings silently.

Release readiness and known limitations

This page defines KeepPeek’s Alpha qualification bar, supply-chain and release checks, representative deployment evidence, soak criteria, and known current limitations. Use it before promoting or relying on a build; active evidence is tracked in Alpha gate #145, and later phases live on the roadmap.

KeepPeek is in Alpha qualification (POC and MVP gates are complete). Closing Alpha implementation issues does not establish an early-adopter matrix or feature freeze, and passing automated tests alone does not certify production readiness.

What automated validation proves

The repository validation gate builds the Rust workspace (including keeppeek-desktop on Windows and macOS) and browser UI; runs Rust (cargo nextest), TypeScript, Svelte, real-media, playback-performance, and Playwright E2E suites; checks formatting, Clippy, unused dependencies (cargo-machete), mdBook/Mermaid output, and Wiki link integrity; and validates versioned Paper storyboard and visual-harness manifests.

Focused benchmarks under benches/ (event_preroll, event_keyframe_lookup, storage_safety, recording_coverage, advanced_event_search, cloud_notifications, timelapse, health_metrics_http.sh), crates/keeppeek-desktop/scripts/benchmark-desktop.ts, and src/tui/benchmark.rs test latency and memory budgets. Some are ignored tests or standalone Cargo benchmarks that require explicit runs—an ordinary green CI run does not prove their results. Always record the exact benchmark command, build, workload, host, and measurements with qualification evidence.

Automated checks prove deterministic contracts and regression fixtures; they do not prove that a specific camera firmware, network, disk, browser, reverse proxy, push provider, or MQTT broker will hold up under sustained load. Paper integrity tests verify exported bundle assets, dimensions, hashes, and tokens rather than comparing every live page to every artboard; verify mobile Keep workflows directly on target devices.

Release and dependency integrity

SurfaceIntegrity and audit policyScope and caveat
Rust workspace (Cargo.lock)Tracked lockfile; build, test, benchmark, container, and release commands use --locked. Security workflow runs cargo audit --deny warnings with zero advisory exceptions.Reproducible locked Rust resolution.
UI and visual harness (ui/package.json)No committed JS lockfiles; exact direct versions resolve from public npm using Bun 1.4.5. Security workflow generates temporary --lockfile-only --ignore-scripts lockfiles and runs bun audit --audit-level high.Surfaces upstream breakage quickly; transitive JS resolution is not pinned across clean runs.
Python example (examples/object_detection_service/)Python 3.12 with intentionally unpinned requirements.txt. Security workflow audits public PyPI resolution via pip-audit without installing model runtimes.Tracks latest upstream packages by design.
Embedded CCTV Camera DatabasePinned to release v2.8.0; build.rs verifies its SHA-256 digest and ZIP entry safety before unpacking.Deterministic embedded camera catalog.
Containers and signed releasesDocker builds use Bun 1.4.5 and immutable base-image digests, followed by an HTTP listener probe. Windows x86_64/aarch64 binaries and NSIS installer/uninstaller are Authenticode-signed and RFC 3161 timestamped; macOS aarch64 bundles are signed and notarized.Version tags publish release artifacts only for commits on main with a matching Cargo.toml version and a successful push CI run.

Representative deployment matrix

Record concrete deployment evidence across all dimensions below before promotion. This matrix documents tested configurations and is not a universal compatibility guarantee.

DimensionRequired qualification evidence
Cameras and transportsAt least one direct RTSP/ONVIF camera and the established native camera path (e.g., Reolink reo-proto, Hikvision ISAPI, ONVIF PullPoint).
Streams and codecsMain and sub streams, including both browser-compatible and browser-incompatible codec profiles.
Client surfacesDesktop and mobile browser workflows, plus keeppeek-desktop and foreground TUI if used in the deployment.
Network and accessLocal access, remote access through the intended VPN or reverse proxy, and bounded guest review links (/share).
Health transitionsHealthy, stale, reconnecting, recording-failed, storage-pressure, and recovered states.
Events and analyticsEvents with and without images, revised events, a dense event day, boolean/hybrid search (/events), and temporal summaries (/activity).
Notifications and MQTTPushover success, failure, and retry; optional Keeppeek Service signaling limits/backoff if enabled; MQTT success, outage, and recovery.
Exports and timelapseNormal, partial, failed, cancelled, retried, and independently decoded MP4 clip exports (plus silent timelapse MP4 export if used).

Continuous recording soak

Run the final release build across segment finalization, catalog maintenance, camera reconnect, provider retry, and storage cleanup cycles:

  1. Account for every unexpected gap using camera, writer, storage, or catalog evidence.
  2. Verify primary recorded playback stays at source rate while timelines refresh and exports execute.
  3. Confirm bounded resource growth across memory, SQLite databases, thumbnails, export jobs, in-memory notification and MQTT outboxes, logs, threads/tasks, file descriptors, sessions, and browser object URLs.
  4. Restart and verify state contracts: confirm notification runtime state, MQTT outbox/deduplication, access audit/activity, and active sessions reset cleanly, while credentials, grants, durable events, export jobs, and catalog state recover as documented.
  5. Rerun end-to-end workflows without manual database or recording-file edits.

Stop qualification immediately on any silent recording-loss path, remote authentication bypass, secret leak, runaway job, or open release-blocking defect.

MQTT restart rehearsal

  1. Point an isolated installation at a disposable MQTT broker and emit a synthetic event after interrupting broker reachability.
  2. Confirm the pending outbox count increments, then restart KeepPeek.
  3. After restart, confirm the pending outbox is empty; restore broker access and verify the pre-restart pending publication is not replayed (QoS does not make the outbox durable across restarts).
  4. Emit a new event and verify delivery plus consumer deduplication by (instance_id, event_id, revision).
  5. Confirm the original durable event remains in KeepPeek and recording continued uninterrupted.

Current limitations

AreaLimitation and operational boundary
Camera compatibility is evidence-basedDiscovery never proves authentication, stable frames, keyframes, or valid MP4s. Validate each stream through a finalized MP4 and independent decode; prefer reo-proto/TCP for Reolink after validation and RTSP/TCP by default for generic ONVIF.
Browser and webview codec support variesKeepPeek stores media without re-encoding, so a recorder can capture a stream a browser (or macOS WKWebView) cannot decode. Retain an H.264 substream for reliable viewing; truthful codec incompatibility is distinct from missing footage. Automatic transcoding remains external.
Config ZIP excludes recording mediaDirect ZIP export/apply covers config.toml and plaintext secrets.toml with restart activation and startup recovery. recordings.db, MP4 files, and thumbnails are a separate backup/recovery responsibility (Backup and restore, Archive recovery). No foreign-archive importer, catalog downgrade, or automatic adoption of unknown MP4s exists; index rebuild applies only to eligible existing catalog recordings.
Restart resets runtime workNotification inbox/history/retries/counters, MQTT pending outbox/deduplication, and access sessions/audit/last-use are in-memory only and do not replay after restart (What survives a restart).
Maintenance filesystem boundariesManual maintenance is destructive and operates on confirmed whole-recording selections. Windows mutation requires NTFS with persistent ACLs (ReFS is rejected). Structural checks do not certify full decodability or immutability; unknown files are never auto-adopted or auto-deleted (Recording maintenance).
Fixed access roles & UI session raceOnly Administrator and User roles exist (no custom roles). Administrators scope User grants in Settings > Access (User access); default grants cover all current and future cameras, dashboard audiences never grant camera access, and saving grant changes closes affected User sessions (stale saves retain the draft and conflict). Trusted-local clients remain Administrators unless excluded from access.local_networks (Camera and dashboard access). Known UI race: an access change during dashboard initialization can leave an expired-session banner visible until reload while the server denies the invalid session; follow reconnect steps and verify grants after sign-in.
Desktop app OIDC restrictionkeeppeek-desktop connects to an existing server and blocks cross-origin OIDC login redirects and verification popups by design; use a standard browser for OIDC sign-in or administrator OIDC verification (Desktop application).
External AI, optional timelapse encoder & unavailable controlsDetection, models, zones/masks, and training remain external. Optional silent MP4 timelapse export requires a host-installed ffmpeg binary. Live participant groups, notification rule Forwarder action, event-source registry, automatic update checks, and bulk erase remain unavailable (Feature guide).

Promotion record

Record concrete results for the exact candidate build before signing off. Leave unverified items explicit—a green build never satisfies a deployment criterion silently.

EvidenceRequired record
Build and deploymentExact version/commit or image digest, host architecture, filesystem, service identity, browser/desktop versions, and proxy/VPN topology.
Camera matrixCamera model and firmware, transport, main/sub codecs, finalized recording result, and independent decode verification.
AccessAllowed and denied camera checks, dashboard audiences, expired/revoked sessions, guest links, and local vs. remote classification.
Sustained operationStart/end timestamps, workload, every recording gap and its root cause, memory/storage growth, cleanup, and reconnect results.
RecoveryConfiguration ZIP apply, stopped catalog/media copy, restart, upgrade rehearsal, and recovered vs. missing intervals.
Integrations and evidenceProvider/broker outage and restart behavior, completed/failed/cancelled exports, and verified downloaded bytes.
DecisionOpen issues, workarounds, evidence location, and an explicit promote or reject decision.

Use Upgrades and migrations to plan version changes and Reporting bugs for reproducible defects. This page does not close the Alpha gate, declare a feature freeze, or substitute for long-running deployment qualification.

Reporting bugs

This chapter covers inspecting live logs, generating scrubbed diagnostics bundles, using Administrator in-app bug reporting, and filing reproducible defects through .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml. GitHub Issues are strictly for reproducible KeepPeek defects; route setup help, feature proposals, and security vulnerabilities to the appropriate channels below.

Choose the right channel

NeedDestination
Existing KeepPeek behavior reproducibly failsBug report
Setup help, troubleshooting, or usage guidanceQ&A
New behavior or a feature proposalSuggestions
A security vulnerabilityPrivate security report

Search open and closed Issues before filing so evidence stays on one report. Duplicate, support, feature, or under-documented reports may be closed or marked needs more info.

Inspect logs and download diagnostics (/settings/logs)

Open Settings > Diagnostics > View logs or Settings > Logs & diagnostics > Open logs (/settings/logs) to inspect live Server (GET /logs SSE stream) and Browser / Svelte logs, update the Server capture filter, pause/clear the view, or export redacted JSONL.

Immediately after reproducing a defect, select Download diagnostics (available in Settings > Logs & diagnostics and on /settings/logs). KeepPeek builds keeppeek-diagnostics-*.json.gz from GET /logs/snapshot (bounded retained server log buffer with a per-process generation epoch), current browser logs, health, runtime configuration, metrics, and collection metadata. The manifest marks privacy: scrubbed, but automated scrubbing can miss private data—always inspect the bundle before sharing. If a bundle cannot be generated, state the exact reason on the issue.

In-app bug reporting (Administrators)

Administrators can start a bounded capture session from Settings (/settings) or Settings > Logs (/settings/logs) by selecting Report a bug:

  1. Review what will be collected and choose Collect logs only or Record screen. Screen capture calls getDisplayMedia({ video: true, audio: false }) and never requests a webcam, microphone, system audio, or tab audio.
  2. Reproduce the defect normally inside KeepPeek. A persistent status bar displays elapsed time, recorded bytes, Stop, and Cancel. Capture stops automatically at 5 minutes or 100 MiB of screen media.
  3. Review the scrubbed issue text, server/browser log counts, UTC window, evidence warnings (active filters, buffer eviction, stream gaps, or recorder restarts detected via LogSnapshot.generation), artifact sizes, retention window, and private video preview. You can exclude or discard the screen recording before sharing.
  4. Confirm once more to submit when [reporting] is configured in config.toml, or download the scrubbed diagnostics package, optional screen recording, and notes locally. Local download stays available whenever remote submission is unconfigured or fails.

Recorder reporting endpoints and bounds

When [reporting] is configured in config.toml (canonical HTTPS origin, optional credential = "{secret:KEY}" in secrets.toml, retention_days 1..=30, default 7), the recorder exposes three Administrator-only endpoints enforcing Cache-Control: no-store and the remote HTTPS policy (426 on plaintext remote HTTP):

EndpointPurpose & bounds
GET /reporting/statusReturns { configured, origin?, retention_days? } without credentials or secret references ({ configured: false } when [reporting] is omitted).
POST /reporting/grantsAccepts <= 128 KiB { idempotencyKey, manifest }, validates the v1 manifest (diagnostics <= 28 MiB gzip, optional screen <= 100 MiB WebM/MP4, combined <= 128 MiB, max 4 in-flight grants), resolves {secret:KEY} on the recorder, calls POST {origin}/v1/reports over verified HTTPS (no redirects, 10s timeout), registers the returned 5-minute single-report grant for log redaction, audits bug_report_submit, and returns { id, grant, expires }.
POST /reporting/auditAccepts <= 512 B closed-enum lifecycle events (start, cancel, local_download, complete, failed) with optional mode and opaque report_id; writes non-sensitive entries to the access audit log.

The browser uploads artifacts directly to the reporting service with the issued single-report bearer grant (PUT /v1/reports/{id}/artifacts/{kind}, POST /v1/reports/{id}/finalize, GET/DELETE /v1/reports/{id}). Recorder credentials never leave the recorder. See Configuration reference and docs/reporting-service.md.

Browser capability matrix

CapabilityWhen supportedWhen unavailable or denied
navigator.mediaDevices.getDisplayMediaOffers Record screen after explicit consent (video-only).Record screen disabled, or denial falls back to logs-only collection.
MediaRecorder + supported VP8/WebM or MP4 codecEncodes bounded chunks up to 5 minutes / 100 MiB.Record screen disabled; logs-only reporting stays available.
CompressionStream('gzip')Produces gzip-compressed keeppeek-diagnostics-*.json.gz.Discloses that the diagnostics package is unavailable while preserving report notes and recordings.

Manual real-browser capture smoke check

When qualifying a release in a real desktop browser:

  1. Sign in as an Administrator over HTTPS or localhost, open Settings, and click Report a bug.
  2. Choose Record screen, select the current KeepPeek tab in the browser picker, and confirm no microphone, webcam, or audio prompt appears.
  3. Navigate to Settings > Logs, confirm the persistent bar stays visible with updating elapsed time and byte count, then end capture via Stop or the browser sharing banner.
  4. Confirm the review dialog opens with a playable preview, download the recording, and run ffprobe to verify one video stream and zero audio streams.

Video walkthrough

This walkthrough covers the Appearance, System, and Logs viewer (/settings/logs), downloading a scrubbed diagnostics bundle, and the redacted Administrator Report a bug flow.

Privacy-preserving usage reporting (separate from bug reports)

KeepPeek also provides optional, Administrator-controlled Usage reporting under Settings > Usage reporting ([usage_reporting] in config.toml) to help prioritize OS, CPU, camera compatibility, and feature work. It is completely separate from bug reports and disabled by default on every fresh install and upgrade (never prompted during onboarding, generates no UUID, and sends zero requests unless enabled).

  • Hierarchical opt-in: Basic system data is required before enabling Camera compatibility data or Feature adoption data; disabled categories are omitted completely.
  • Privacy and payload bounds: Uses a dedicated pseudonymous UUID v4 + server-only proof token (separate from access keys), caps uncompressed payloads at 64 KiB of strictly allowlisted fields (never camera names, IPs, URLs, credentials, firmware, events, logs, or media), and lets Administrators Preview exact JSON payload locally before opting in.
  • Schedule, retention, and deletion: Sends over HTTPS at most once every 24 hours after a >= 15 minute startup delay. Raw rows expire within 60 days, source IPs are not stored, and public aggregates enforce k >= 10 installations with complementary suppression. Disabling or selecting Reset installation ID sends bounded deletion (DELETE /v1/installations/{installation_id}), with Retry deletion if offline.

See Configuration reference and docs/usage-reporting.md.

Redaction rules

Redact at the source before publishing any issue text, log excerpt, screenshot, or diagnostics package. Never publish:

  • credentials, access keys, tokens, cookies, or authorization headers;
  • camera IP addresses, hostnames, private filesystem prefixes, or stream/snapshot URLs;
  • faces, license plates, private recordings, notification text, or location details;
  • unsanitized logs, screenshots, packet captures, or unreviewed diagnostics bundles.

Do not attach evidence that cannot be confidently sanitized; describe how a maintainer can build an equivalent synthetic fixture instead.

Complete the GitHub bug form (bug.yml)

Keep one issue focused on one observable defect and satisfy every required section of .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml:

  1. Before submitting: Confirm all 6 preflight checks truthfully (duplicate search, reproducible defect, current release or exact build, Health/diagnosis/log/console review, redaction, diagnostics package attached or unavailability explained).
  2. Affected area: Select the primary failing user-visible workflow (one of: Recording and ingest, Peek and live video, Events, search, and previews, Keep, timeline, and recorded playback, Export, Cameras, discovery, and configuration, Camera controls and vendor integration, Storage, retention, backup, and restore, Authentication, authorization, and privacy, Installation, upgrade, and service lifecycle, API, WebRTC, and external integrations, UI, mobile, and accessibility, Other).
  3. Bug description & Reproduction steps: Give the smallest deterministic sequence from a known starting state plus frequency (always, intermittent, or N of M attempts).
  4. Expected and actual behavior: State observable differences (state, HTTP/error code, file, timing, or resource value)—not “it does not work”.
  5. Regression, frequency, and impact: Record prior working status (yes/no/unknown), last good / first bad builds if known, data-loss risk, and workarounds. Use unknown rather than guessing.
  6. Environment: Fill all 9 fields (version/commit/build profile, OS/arch, install method, browser/client version, camera make/model/anonymized firmware, camera source, stream codec/resolution/FPS/audio, camera count & catalog scale, storage type & free space); use N/A only when inapplicable.
  7. Diagnostics package and sanitized evidence: Drag in keeppeek-diagnostics-*.json.gz (or state why it is unavailable) plus correlated timestamps and focused excerpts.
  8. Fix acceptance criteria and verification: Keep each criterion unchecked and pair one observable outcome with one concrete verification test or procedure:
- [ ] AC-1: <the reported reproduction produces the correct observable outcome>
  - Expected outcome: <exact state or result>
  - Verification: <regression test, command, or reproducible procedure>
  1. Performance evidence: For runtime, build, memory, storage, network, or latency defects, report metric + unit, baseline/current value, budget/target, workload/dataset, environment, exact command/harness, and run count + statistic (median/p95). For correctness-only defects, write Performance: N/A and state which performance paths cannot be affected.

Open source and licensing

This chapter defines KeepPeek’s dual-license boundary (AGPL-3.0-only server vs MIT public API), credits the open-source libraries, datasets, and media fixtures KeepPeek builds on, and summarizes how KeepPeek’s scope compares with peer open-source NVR projects.

Licensing model

KeepPeek separates the shared recorder core from its public protocol at an explicit license boundary:

AreaLicensePurpose
Server, first-party UI, desktop wrapper, reference examples (examples/), and most workspace cratesAGPL-3.0-onlyKeeps the shared NVR core open—including modified network deployments—with no camera-count limits.
Public HTTP, WebRTC, protobuf, and SDP contracts under api/, plus bindings generated solely from themMITLets independent clients and services implement the protocol under any open or commercial license.
Permissive protocol library (crates/isapi) and local upstream forks (crates/retina, crates/onvif, crates/mp4, crates/rouille)Upstream MIT / Apache-2.0Retains original upstream authorship and licenses in each crate.

See the root LICENSE for complete AGPL-3.0-only terms and api/LICENSE for the api/ MIT terms.

Protocol-plugin boundary (free and paid services)

Because the recorder is a network service, AGPL-3.0-only ensures operators and users retain source access to the server running for them. Meanwhile, licensing api/ and its generated bindings under MIT establishes a protocol-plugin model rather than an in-process plugin ABI:

  • Independent free, community, or commercial services (object detection, transcoding, workflows, alternate viewers) run as separate processes and talk to KeepPeek through the public API without inheriting AGPL.
  • KeepPeek retains sole ownership of camera credentials, recording paths, and internal databases rather than exposing them to third-party in-process plugins.
StakeholderWhat the split provides
Self-hosters & consumersFree, auditable recorder and first-party viewer with no camera-count paywall; optional services only when desired.
Small businessesAbility to purchase specialized external services at the API boundary without a proprietary NVR fork.
Open-source contributorsCore improvements stay open under AGPL while permissive API clients remain easy to build.
Service authorsSustainable commercial or open offerings without closing the shared recorder core.

Code foundations and upstream forks

Local workspace forks preserve upstream authorship and licenses; carrying a fork is a maintenance decision, not a claim of original authorship.

Project / crateUpstream authorsLicenseRole in KeepPeek
Retina (crates/retina)Scott Lamb & contributorsMIT OR Apache-2.0Local fork providing the RTSP client, RTP depacketization, and codec framing.
ONVIF-rs (crates/onvif)Chris Bruce, Lumeo & contributorsMITLocal fork providing ONVIF discovery, XML schemas, authentication, and camera control operations.
str0mMartin Algesten & contributorsMIT OR Apache-2.0Upstream Sans-I/O Rust WebRTC stack (RTP/frame APIs, data channels, explicit time/network inputs).
mp4-rust (crates/mp4)Alf & contributorsMITLocal fork providing ISO BMFF / MP4 reading and writing.
rouille (crates/rouille)Pierre Krieger & contributorsMIT/Apache-2.0Local fork providing the synchronous HTTP server layer.
crates/isapi & crates/reo-protoKeepPeek contributorsMIT / AGPL-3.0-onlyPlain-Rust Sans-I/O Hikvision ISAPI (provenance) and Reolink Baichuan protocol (informed by neolink and thirtythreeforty’s reverse-engineering notes).

Third-party catalogs and test media fixtures

AssetAttribution / sourceLicenseUsage in KeepPeek
CCTV Camera Database (v2.8.0)ch-bas/cctv-camera-databaseUpstream release archive (verified by SHA-256 in build.rs)Embedded offline camera model catalog for guided camera setup.
Big Buck Bunny© 2008 Blender FoundationCC BY 3.048-second 4K H.264 excerpt (crates/test-camera/testdata/) for the nine-camera demo and benchmarks.
4K resolution sampleJihei (2011), Wikimedia CommonsCC BY-SA 3.01-second H.264/H.265 derivatives (crates/test-camera/testdata/cc-4k-*.mp4) for backend tests.
Intel IoT DevKit sample videos (person-bicycle-car-detection.mp4)intel-iot-devkit/sample-videosCC-BY-4.0Fetched at pinned commit in CI (not committed; see data/README.md) for real-model object-detection integration tests.

See crates/test-camera/testdata/README.md for exact fixture hashes and encoding commands.

Peer open-source NVR projects

Scrypted and Frigate are strong open-source projects with distinct design centers—thank you to their maintainers and communities for advancing self-hosted camera software.

ProjectDistinct design centerStrongest fit when
ScryptedVideo integration platform and NVR with an extensible plugin model, smart detections, and low-latency HomeKit, Google Home, and Alexa bridges.Broad smart-home bridges, an in-process plugin ecosystem, and consumer-platform integration belong in one system.
FrigateLocal NVR centered on real-time AI object detection, accelerator-aware inference, zones, event review, Home Assistant, and MQTT.Tightly integrated, accelerator-tuned local AI detection and home automation are the NVR’s central job.
KeepPeekFocused recorder and WebRTC media gateway that ingests camera events and delegates optional inference, transcoding, and bridges across an open API.Recording must stay isolated from analytics, and clients or services should compose cleanly over an open media/event boundary.

See Contributing for how licenses apply to submitted changes.

Contributing

This chapter defines KeepPeek’s product-scope bar, repository engineering policies (AGENTS.md), local formatting and validation commands (fix.sh/fix.bat, check.sh/check.bat), AI-assisted contribution rules, and pull-request workflow. Read Users and design choices before proposing scope changes; file defects through Reporting bugs and ask questions or propose features in GitHub Discussions.

Scope boundaries and high-fit work

KeepPeek guards its core feature set strictly—a completed implementation does not justify expanding scope. Open a GitHub Discussion before implementing any change to product scope, the public API, storage format, platform contract, or long-term maintenance surface.

Feature proposals are evaluated against five questions:

  1. Does this belong in a focused recorder and WebRTC media gateway?
  2. Can an independent external service provide it over the MIT-licensed public API instead?
  3. Which documented users need it, and does it degrade another user’s core workflow?
  4. What permanent maintenance, security, storage, resource, compatibility, and licensing cost does it add?
  5. Can it be tested deterministically without depending on one private device, account, or cloud service?

Contributions that fit well

  • Bug fixes, diagnostics, tests, and docs: High-signal fixes with deterministic regression coverage that preserve existing boundaries.
  • Camera protocols & RTSP interoperability: Narrow parser, transport, or adapter fixes backed by model/firmware/transport details, a sanitized minimal repro, and a deterministic fixture or fake-camera test. Never weaken validation globally for one camera quirk, never submit secrets/URLs/private captures, and validate real-device main/sub frames, keyframes, MP4 finalization, and independent decode when recording changes.
  • Protected api/ contract: api/ (OpenAPI, protobuf schemas, protocol docs, generated contract sources) is read-only by default. Contract changes require explicit approval for the task, must stay within approved scope, preserve unrelated contracts, and keep docs, generated bindings, and contract tests synchronized. New detectors, transcoders, and bridges belong outside core first.

Repository policies and tooling

Follow AGENTS.md and the language guidelines below for every contribution:

AreaRequirement
RustStable Rust, cargo-nextest, cargo-machete, public crates.io only (.cargo/config.toml). Follow Pragmatic Rust Guidelines.
Frontend (ui/)Bun only (pinned 1.4.5 in ui/.bun-version), public npm registry only (.npmrc, ui/bunfig.toml), bun install --no-save, no npm/pnpm/Yarn lockfiles and no committed JS lockfiles. Follow Pragmatic Svelte 5 Guidelines.
PythonPython 3.12 (examples/object_detection_service/.python-version). Never create or use a virtual environment (.venv). Install examples/object_detection_service/requirements.txt directly into the resolved Python 3.12 interpreter (falling back to --break-system-packages). Requirements stay intentionally unpinned; fix upstream lint/type breaks instead of pinning. Override path via absolute KEEPPEEK_PYTHON (never a venv).
ConfigurationStore settings in config.toml and private strings in secrets.toml ({secret:KEY}, {secret:KEY|url}). Never add separate settings files; keep Configuration reference synchronized.
Engineering styleApply TigerStyle safety/boundedness (excluding static allocation) and Ponytail minimal-diff discipline; write comments only for non-obvious constraints.

Formatting, validation, and CI gates

Run the narrowest relevant test during development, then pass the canonical root validation before requesting review:

GoalmacOS / LinuxWindows (PowerShell / cmd)
Auto-format & refresh Python deps./fix.sh.\fix.bat
Canonical full check./check.sh.\check.bat
Full check + slow Rust testsKEEPPEEK_RUN_SLOW_TESTS=1 ./check.sh$env:KEEPPEEK_RUN_SLOW_TESTS = '1'; .\check.bat
Docs, mdBook, and Wiki checksbun run --cwd ui format:markdown:check && mdbook build book && bun run --cwd ui wiki:typecheck && bun run --cwd ui wiki:test && bun run --cwd ui wiki:check && bun run --cwd ui wiki:buildSame commands (requires mdbook and mdbook-mermaid on PATH).

check.sh / check.bat builds the Rust workspace, runs cargo nextest (--features macos-test-aws-crypto on macOS), runs cargo clippy --all --all-targets -- -D warnings, cargo machete, cargo fmt --check, taplo fmt --check, Python black --check, and in ui/ runs bun run quality:check, bun run test:e2e, and bun run perf:playback:run. fix.sh / fix.bat upgrades Python requirements, formats Rust, TOML, Python, Markdown, and UI files, and runs lint:fix. Document any test you could not run locally in your PR.

CI workflow coverage

WorkflowTriggerWhat runs
CI (PR / merge)Pull requests & merge groupsPath-filtered jobs: Format, Book (mdBook + Mermaid + Wiki checks), Clippy, cargo-machete, doctests, Python object-detection example & integration, macOS/Windows builds + service/desktop smoke checks, full Linux Rust suites via nextest, OS-targeted macOS & Windows Rust core/durable-state subsets, and UI static/unit/coverage/Linux Playwright/Home Assistant Container.
CI (push to main)Push to mainFull Rust core & durable-state suites across Linux, macOS, and Windows, plus signed x64 and ARM64 Windows release artifacts.
CodeQLPRs to main, push, merge group, weeklyActions, JavaScript/TypeScript, Python, and Rust static analysis.
MainPush to main, hourly cronOpt-in slow Rust tests, Rust coverage (cargo-llvm-cov), Linux ARM64 & Windows x64/ARM64 builds/tests + NSIS installer lifecycle, and macOS/Windows Playwright E2E shards.

AI-assisted contributions

AI tools are permitted under the exact same review bar as handwritten code—responsibility never transfers to the model. The submitter is the PR’s first reviewer and must:

  • inspect, understand, and be able to explain every changed line (including tests and configuration);
  • verify API, security, resource, failure, and compatibility assumptions directly against the repository;
  • remove invented behavior, unnecessary abstractions, unrelated cleanup, and filler commentary;
  • run validation locally and investigate failures rather than asking reviewers to debug raw model output.

“The AI wrote it” is not an acceptable design rationale; if the submitter cannot defend control flow, ownership, protocol behavior, or tests, the PR is not ready.

Pull request workflow and review cadence

Use .github/pull_request_template.md for every PR and complete all sections before requesting review:

  1. Summary: Describe the user-visible outcome and link the feature Discussion or reproducible bug issue.
  2. Acceptance criteria verification: Include one row per criterion from the linked context with observable outcome, verification method, and observed result.
  3. Performance evidence: Provide reproducible before/after numbers (baseline, environment, workload, command, runs, median/p95), or Performance: N/A explaining which paths cannot change.
  4. Validation & Review evidence: Confirm canonical checks, slow tests, focused tests, and sanitized artifacts/CI links (zero secrets or private media).

Keep each PR scoped to one coherent behavioral change with regression tests and synchronized docs; omit unrelated formatting or refactors. Use Draft PRs for early feedback once scope is agreed.

Review cadence: Pull requests are reviewed on Sundays. If a Sunday window passes without review, leave one short, polite ping on the existing PR—do not open duplicate PRs or Discussions.

Book, Wiki, and Paper design evidence

  • Single Markdown source: Edit documentation in book/src/ (SUMMARY.md controls navigation) and selected docs/ files via PR—never edit the GitHub Wiki directly. Both the Wiki and the mdBook site are generated from repository Markdown (see docs/wiki-publishing.md). Update Feature guide and navigation when adding or moving screens, and verify live handlers before documenting a feature as available.
  • Paper design references: Versioned UI specs live under ui/design/paper/. keeppeek-nvr-alpha is the active NVR reference; keeppeek-nvr-v34 remains the historical input for the existing visual harness and Linux Loki baselines. A Paper export records design intent, not an approved baseline or proof of working behavior—keep unavailable, loading, denied, empty, and error states explicit.

Licensing

Contributions are accepted under the license of the area modified; see Open source and licensing. Submit only work you have the right to contribute under that license, including fixtures, generated files, and AI-assisted output.
